LEXASSIST LEGAL QA — GENERAL LAW
============================================================

Q: Who is the respondent in the case Union of India vs. Maj. Gen. Manomoy Ganguly?
A: The respondent is Maj. Gen. Manomoy Ganguly.
Case: Union of India vs. Maj. Gen. Manomoy Ganguly
Date: 1st August 2018

---

Q: What was the reason given by the appellants for not promoting Maj. Gen. Manomoy Ganguly to DGMS (Army)?
A: The appellants argued that Maj. Gen. Manomoy Ganguly was not found suitable for the position based on an inter se assessment of suitability among eligible officers.
Case: Union of India vs. Maj. Gen. Manomoy Ganguly
Date: 1st August 2018

---

Q: What was the significance of Section 42(c) of the Partnership Act in the case Davesh Nagalya (D) & Ors. vs. Pradeep Kumar (D) Thr. LRs. and Ors.?
A: Section 42(c) of the Partnership Act states that a partnership is dissolved by the death of a partner. The appellants argued that the partnership between Pradeep Kumar and Subhash Chand dissolved upon the death of Pradeep Kumar, and thus the tenancy should be deemed vacant.
Case: Davesh Nagalya (D) & Ors. vs. Pradeep Kumar (D) Thr. LRs. and Ors.
Date: 10th August 2021

---

Q: What was the role of the District Magistrate in the partnership agreement between Pradeep Kumar and Subhash Chand in the case Davesh Nagalya (D) & Ors. vs. Pradeep Kumar (D) Thr. LRs. and Ors.?
A: The District Magistrate had permitted Subhash Chand to be inducted as a partner with Pradeep Kumar on 15th November 1982, which led to the signing of the partnership deed on 19th November 1982.
Case: Davesh Nagalya (D) & Ors. vs. Pradeep Kumar (D) Thr. LRs. and Ors.
Date: 10th August 2021

---

Q: What were the objections raised by the second defendant, Gopalakrishnan Nair, regarding Survey No.988 in the case Selvi vs. Gopalakrishnan Nair (D) Thr. LRs. and Ors.?
A: Gopalakrishnan Nair consistently objected that Survey No.988 belonged to him and should be excluded from the properties to be partitioned. He also raised concerns about the boundaries and description of the suit properties.
Case: Selvi vs. Gopalakrishnan Nair (D) Thr. LRs. and Ors.
Date: 15th May 2018

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case Shakti Bhog Food Industries Ltd. vs. The Central Bank of India & Anr.?
A: The primary issue was whether the suit filed by Shakti Bhog Food Industries Ltd. for rendition of accounts and recovery of excess amounts charged by the Central Bank of India was barred by the law of limitation.
Case: Shakti Bhog Food Industries Ltd. vs. The Central Bank of India & Anr.
Date: 5th June 2020

---

Q: What was the decision of the trial court regarding the suit filed by Shakti Bhog Food Industries Ltd. in the case Shakti Bhog Food Industries Ltd. vs. The Central Bank of India & Anr.?
A: The trial court rejected the plaint under Order VII Rule 11(d) of the CPC, stating that the suit was barred by the law of limitation as it was filed beyond the prescribed period of three years from the date when the right to sue accrued.
Case: Shakti Bhog Food Industries Ltd. vs. The Central Bank of India & Anr.
Date: 5th June 2020

---

Q: What was the contention of Shakti Bhog Food Industries Ltd. regarding the accrual of the cause of action in the case against The Central Bank of India?
A: Shakti Bhog Food Industries Ltd. contended that the cause of action accrued only upon the rejection of their representation by the Central Bank of India, which was communicated through letters dated 19th September 2002 and 3rd June 2003, and after the final legal notice was served on 7th January 2005.
Case: Shakti Bhog Food Industries Ltd. vs. The Central Bank of India & Anr.
Date: 5th June 2020

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case Union of India vs. Board of Control for Cricket in India & Ors.?
A: The main issue was whether the live broadcast signals of cricket matches shared by BCCI with Prasar Bharati should be retransmitted by cable operators, and whether this arrangement was legal under Section 3 of the Sports Broadcasting Signals (Mandatory Sharing with Prasar Bharati) Act, 2007 and Section 8 of the Cable Television Networks (Regulation) Act, 1995.
Case: Union of India vs. Board of Control for Cricket in India & Ors.
Date: 22nd August 2017

---

Q: What was the Union of India's argument regarding the retransmission of live signals in the case Union of India vs. Board of Control for Cricket in India & Ors.?
A: The Union of India argued that the retransmission of live signals by cable operators was necessary to provide access to the largest number of viewers, especially those in remote areas, and that this was in line with the objectives of the Prasar Bharati Act, 1990 and the Sports Act, 2007.
Case: Union of India vs. Board of Control for Cricket in India & Ors.
Date: 22nd August 2017

---

Q: Why did the Central Government debar Apollo Institute of Medical Sciences & Research from admitting students for two years?
A: The Central Government debared the college based on a negative recommendation from the Medical Council of India (MCI), citing deficiencies in faculty and resident doctors during inspections.
Case: Apollo Institute of Medical Sciences & Research vs. Union of India & Anr.
Date: 31st August 2017

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case Tehseen Poonawalla vs. Union of India & Anr.?
A: The main issue was whether the remarks made against Ms. Indira Jaising in the judgment dated 19th April 2018, indicating that her conduct amounted to contempt or prima facie contempt of court, should be expunged.
Case: Tehseen Poonawalla vs. Union of India & Anr.
Date: 26th September 2018

---

Q: What relief did Ms. Indira Jaising seek in her Miscellaneous Application?
A: Ms. Indira Jaising sought an order to expunge or delete the remarks made against her that indicated her conduct amounted to contempt or prima facie contempt of court.
Case: Tehseen Poonawalla vs. Union of India & Anr.
Date: 26th September 2018

---

Q: What argument did Dr. Abhishek Manu Singhvi present on behalf of Ms. Indira Jaising?
A: Dr. Abhishek Manu Singhvi argued that Ms. Indira Jaising did not intend to make any submissions that would denigrate or scandalize the judiciary and that the remarks made against her were not justified.
Case: Tehseen Poonawalla vs. Union of India & Anr.
Date: 26th September 2018

---

Q: What was Mr. Mukul Rohatgi's stance regarding Ms. Indira Jaising's submissions in the case?
A: Mr. Mukul Rohatgi, representing the State of Maharashtra, opposed the application and argued that the submissions attributed to Ms. Jaising were indeed made by her during the proceedings, and the remarks in the judgment were based on those submissions.
Case: Tehseen Poonawalla vs. Union of India & Anr.
Date: 26th September 2018

---

Q: What was M/s. Saraf Exports' argument regarding the eligibility for deductions under Section 80-IB?
A: M/s. Saraf Exports argued that the profits from Duty Drawback and DEPB schemes should be considered as profits derived from the business of the industrial undertaking and therefore eligible for deductions under Section 80-IB.
Case: M/s. Saraf Exports vs. Commissioner of Income Tax, Jaipur-III
Date: 10th April 2023

---

Q: What was the decision of the Central Administrative Tribunal (CAT) regarding Shri Ram Shridhar Chimurkar's claim for family pension?
A: The CAT allowed Shri Ram Shridhar Chimurkar's claim for family pension, directing the Respondents to consider his claim by treating him as the adopted son of the deceased government servant, Shridar Chimurkar.
Case: Shri Ram Shridhar Chimurkar vs. Union of India & Anr.
Date: 17th January 2023

---

Q: What was the product manufactured by M/s Thermax Ltd. and how was it described?
A: M/s Thermax Ltd. manufactured Modified Vapour Absorption Chillers (MVAC) and described them as heat pumps, which were capable of providing both chilled and hot water as output.
Case: M/s Thermax Ltd. vs. Commissioner of Central Excise, Pune-1
Date: 13th October 2022

---

Q: What argument did M/s Thermax Ltd. present regarding the classification of their product?
A: M/s Thermax Ltd. argued that their MVAC product, capable of producing both chilled and hot water, should be classified as a heat pump. They relied on the Harmonious System of Nomenclature (HSN) Explanatory Notes and industry-related decisions in similar cases involving Blue Star Ltd. and Voltas Ltd.
Case: M/s Thermax Ltd. vs. Commissioner of Central Excise, Pune-1
Date: 13th October 2022

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case Rakesh Kumar Agarwalla & Anr. vs. National Law School of India University, Bengaluru & Ors.?
A: The main issue was whether the National Law School of India University (NLSIU) could conduct a separate admission entrance examination, the National Law Aptitude Test (NLAT), instead of admitting students through the Common Law Admission Test (CLAT) for the academic session 2020-21.
Case: Rakesh Kumar Agarwalla & Anr. vs. National Law School of India University, Bengaluru & Ors.
Date: 21st September 2020

---

Q: What prompted NLSIU to issue the admission notification for NLAT?
A: NLSIU issued the admission notification for NLAT due to delays in the CLAT examination caused by the COVID-19 pandemic, aiming to avoid a 'zero year' and ensure that the academic session 2020-21 could commence on time.
Case: Rakesh Kumar Agarwalla & Anr. vs. National Law School of India University, Bengaluru & Ors.
Date: 21st September 2020

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case Wipro Finance Ltd. vs. Commissioner of Income Tax?
A: The main issue was whether the loss incurred by Wipro Finance Ltd. due to foreign exchange fluctuation should be treated as revenue expenditure or capital expenditure for the assessment year 1997-98.
Case: Wipro Finance Ltd. vs. Commissioner of Income Tax
Date: 12th April 2022

---

Q: What was the decision of the Income Tax Appellate Tribunal (ITAT) regarding the foreign exchange fluctuation loss?
A: The ITAT decided that the foreign exchange fluctuation loss incurred by Wipro Finance Ltd. should be treated as revenue expenditure, allowing the entire claim of Rs. 3,56,57,727/- as a deductible expense.
Case: Wipro Finance Ltd. vs. Commissioner of Income Tax
Date: 12th April 2022

---

Q: What did the office order dated 21st December 2019 issued by Kumaun University state about the retirement dates of the appellants?
A: The office order stated that the respective dates of retirement for the appellants were the last dates in the months they attained the age of superannuation, i.e., 65 years.
Case: Navin Chandra Dhoundiyal vs. State of Uttarakhand & Ors.
Date: 16th October 2020

---

Q: What was the initial decision of the National Company Law Tribunal (NCLT) regarding the appellant's fees?
A: The NCLT directed the Bank of India to pay all expenses incurred by the Resolution Professional (RP) and an additional Rs. 5,00,000 plus GST towards the fee of the RP.
Case: Devarajan Raman vs. Bank of India Limited
Date: 5th January 2022

---

Q: What did the National Company Law Appellate Tribunal (NCLAT) decide on the appellant's appeal regarding his fees?
A: The NCLAT dismissed the appellant's appeal, stating that the consolidated amount of Rs. 5,00,000 plus GST allowed as fee for the entire period was not unreasonable, and that the fixation of fees is not a business decision dependent on the commercial wisdom of the Committee of Creditors (CoC).
Case: Devarajan Raman vs. Bank of India Limited
Date: 5th January 2022

---

Q: Why did Hindustan Copper Ltd. challenge the second-tier arbitration award?
A: Hindustan Copper Ltd. challenged the second-tier arbitration award on the grounds that it was contrary to public policy in India and that the arbitration agreement allowing for a two-tier arbitration process was invalid.
Case: M/S. Centrotrade Minerals and Metals Inc. vs. Hindustan Copper Ltd.
Date: 2nd June 2020

---

Q: What was the main legal issue in the case N.S. Nandiesha Reddy vs. Kavitha Mahesh?
A: The main legal issue was whether the election of N.S. Nandiesha Reddy from the 151 K.R. Pura Legislative Assembly constituency in Bangalore Urban District should be declared void under Section 100(1)(c) of the Representation of People Act, 1951.
Case: N.S. Nandiesha Reddy vs. Kavitha Mahesh
Date: 3rd August 2021

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case Dr. J. Vijayan & Others vs. The State of Kerala & Others?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the State of Kerala was obligated to enhance the retirement age of university teachers to 65 years in accordance with the University Grants Commission (UGC) Regulations, 2010.
Case: Dr. J. Vijayan & Others vs. The State of Kerala & Others
Date: 2nd August 2022

---

Q: What did the UGC Regulations, 2010, stipulate regarding the retirement age of university teachers?
A: The UGC Regulations, 2010, stipulated that the age of superannuation for teachers in central universities and other institutions maintained and/or funded by the UGC should be enhanced to 65 years.
Case: Dr. J. Vijayan & Others vs. The State of Kerala & Others
Date: 2nd August 2022

---

Q: What is the central issue in the case Thiru K. Palaniswamy vs. M. Shanmugam & Ors.?
A: The central issue is related to the internal management and leadership structure of the political party All India Anna Dravida Munnetra Kazhagam (AIADMK), particularly the validity of decisions taken in the General Council meetings and the amendments to the party's byelaws.
Case: Thiru K. Palaniswamy vs. M. Shanmugam & Ors.
Date: 23rd February 2023

---

Q: What triggered the legal dispute in this case?
A: The legal dispute was triggered by the conflicting views within AIADMK regarding amendments to the party's byelaws, specifically the shift from a system of joint leadership (Co-ordinator and Joint Co-ordinator) to a single leadership structure, and the related decisions taken in the General Council meetings.
Case: Thiru K. Palaniswamy vs. M. Shanmugam & Ors.
Date: 23rd February 2023

---

Q: What was the outcome of the General Council meeting held on 11th July 2022?
A: In the General Council meeting held on 11th July 2022, several resolutions were passed, including the abolition of the posts of Co-ordinator and Joint Co-ordinator, the creation of the post of Interim General Secretary, and the election of Thiru E.K. Palaniswamy as the Interim General Secretary.
Case: Thiru K. Palaniswamy vs. M. Shanmugam & Ors.
Date: 23rd February 2023

---

Q: What is the primary legal issue in the case Principal Commissioner of Income Tax, Central-3 vs. Abhisar Buildwell P. Ltd.?
A: The primary legal issue is the scope of assessment under section 153A of the Income Tax Act, 1961, particularly whether the Assessing Officer can assess total income including materials found during a search, and whether such assessment can include completed assessments if no incriminating material is found during the search.
Case: Principal Commissioner of Income Tax, Central-3 vs. Abhisar Buildwell P. Ltd.
Date: 24th April 2023

---

Q: What was the Revenue's argument regarding the scope of assessment under section 153A?
A: The Revenue argued that under section 153A, the Assessing Officer is competent to consider all material available on record, including that found during the search, and make an assessment of total income, even for completed assessments without the necessity of finding incriminating material during the search.
Case: Principal Commissioner of Income Tax, Central-3 vs. Abhisar Buildwell P. Ltd.
Date: 24th April 2023

---

Q: What was the Assessees' argument against the Revenue's interpretation of section 153A?
A: The Assessees argued that the assessment under section 153A should be limited to incriminating material found during the search and that completed/unabated assessments should not be reopened unless such material is found during the search.
Case: Principal Commissioner of Income Tax, Central-3 vs. Abhisar Buildwell P. Ltd.
Date: 24th April 2023

---

Q: What types of products were involved in this case?
A: The products involved in this case were dissolved and compressed industrial gases, liquid chlorine, cotton yarn, and Post Mix Concentrate.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.
Date: 11th May 2018

---

Q: What was the significance of the decision in this case?
A: The significance of the decision is that it clarified the scope of 'transaction value' under Section 4 of the Central Excise Act, 1944, ensuring that all charges that add value to the product until its clearance are included for the purpose of calculating excise duty.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.
Date: 11th May 2018

---

Q: What were the years of assessment involved in the case?
A: The years of assessment involved in the case were 2013-14 and 2015-16.
Case: M/S Modi Naturals Ltd vs. The Commissioner of Commercial Tax UP
Date: 6th November 2023

---

Q: What products are manufactured by M/S Modi Naturals Ltd?
A: M/S Modi Naturals Ltd manufactures Rice Bran Oil (RBO) and Physical Refined RBO, with a by-product of De-Oiled Rice Bran (DORB).
Case: M/S Modi Naturals Ltd vs. The Commissioner of Commercial Tax UP
Date: 6th November 2023

---

Q: What is the primary legal issue in the case Ashok G. Rajani vs. Beacon Trusteeship Ltd. & Ors.?
A: The primary legal issue is whether the National Company Law Appellate Tribunal (NCLAT) was correct in not restraining the Interim Resolution Professional (IRP) from proceeding with the Corporate Insolvency Resolution Process (CIRP) of M/s Seya Industries Limited, despite the settlement reached between the parties.
Case: Ashok G. Rajani vs. Beacon Trusteeship Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 22nd September 2022

---

Q: What was the decision of the NCLAT regarding the CIRP of M/s Seya Industries Limited?
A: The NCLAT issued a notice of the appeal but did not restrain the IRP from proceeding with the CIRP. However, it restrained the IRP from constituting a Committee of Creditors (CoC) until the next hearing date.
Case: Ashok G. Rajani vs. Beacon Trusteeship Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 22nd September 2022

---

Q: What was the nature of the settlement reached between the parties?
A: The parties reached an amicable settlement on 8th August 2021, and entered into a formal settlement agreement, which was submitted to the NCLT, requesting the withdrawal of the CIRP application under Section 12A of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code (IBC).
Case: Ashok G. Rajani vs. Beacon Trusteeship Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 22nd September 2022

---

Q: What was the decision of the Central Administrative Tribunal (CAT) regarding the special pay for Scientists?
A: The Central Administrative Tribunal (CAT) allowed the original applications filed by the respondents (Scientists) and directed that the special pay of Rs. 2,000 and Rs. 4,000 be treated as part of pay for pensionary benefits.
Case: Union of India & Ors. vs. Dr. O.P. Nijhawan & Ors.
Date: 3rd January 2019

---

Q: What are the key factors the court considers when determining cruelty in matrimonial cases?
A: The court considers the social status, educational level, social and economic conditions, culture, human values, and the specific circumstances of each case.
Case: Smt. Roopa Soni vs. Kamalnarayan Soni

---

Q: What was the decision of the Jammu & Kashmir Special Tribunal regarding the eviction order?
A: The Jammu & Kashmir Special Tribunal granted an interim stay on the eviction order on 9th January 1996.
Case: J.S. Luthra Academy & Another vs. State of Jammu and Kashmir & Others

---

Q: What were the main allegations made by Delma Lubna Coelho against Edmond Clint Fernandes?
A: Delma Lubna Coelho alleged that she was ill-treated, insulted, and humiliated by Edmond Clint Fernandes and his family members, and that she was forcibly sent to Mumbai, where he disconnected all relations with her.
Case: Delma Lubna Coelho vs. Edmond Clint Fernandes

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case N.C.V. Aishwarya vs. A.S. Saravana Karthik Sha?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the Family Court proceedings filed by the respondent-husband in Vellore should be transferred to the Family Court in Chennai at the request of the appellant-wife.
Case: N.C.V. Aishwarya vs. A.S. Saravana Karthik Sha

---

Q: What prompted the Commissioner of Income Tax to initiate proceedings against Jagannath Gupta Family Trust?
A: The Commissioner initiated proceedings based on a survey conducted at the School of Human Genetics and Population Health (SHGPH), Kolkata, which revealed a donation entry of Rs. 37,00,000 to the respondent-trust, alleged to be bogus and involving money laundering.
Case: Commissioner of Income Tax (Exemptions), Kolkata vs. Jagannath Gupta Family Trust

---

Q: What was the Trust's defense against the allegations of bogus donations?
A: The Trust argued that the proceedings were contrary to natural justice as they were not given an opportunity to cross-examine the donor's representative, whose statement was used as the basis for the allegations.
Case: Commissioner of Income Tax (Exemptions), Kolkata vs. Jagannath Gupta Family Trust

---

Q: What was the Income Tax Appellate Tribunal's (ITAT) decision on the matter?
A: The ITAT set aside the cancellation order, stating that the Trust should be given an opportunity to cross-examine the donor's representative, and remanded the matter for fresh consideration.
Case: Commissioner of Income Tax (Exemptions), Kolkata vs. Jagannath Gupta Family Trust

---

Q: What was the decision of the Lower Appellate Court regarding Satnam Singh's appeal?
A: The Lower Appellate Court dismissed Satnam Singh's appeal, finding that his contentions were without merit and that the decree against Sibo and Satnam Singh had become final.
Case: Jit Ram (deceased) through LRs. vs. Satnam Singh

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case between Kerala State Beverages Manufacturing & Marketing Corporation Ltd. and The Assistant Commissioner of Income Tax Circle 1(1)?
A: The main issue was whether the debits made in the Profit & Loss Account of Kerala State Beverages Manufacturing & Marketing Corporation Ltd. with respect to the amount of surcharge on sales tax and turnover tax paid to the State Government should be disallowed under Section 40(a)(iib) of the Income-tax Act, 1961.
Case: Kerala State Beverages Manufacturing & Marketing Corporation Ltd. v. The Assistant Commissioner of Income Tax Circle 1(1)

---

Q: What was the main legal issue in the case of Electrosteel Castings Limited vs. UV Asset Reconstruction Company Limited & Ors.?
A: The main legal issue was whether the suit filed by Electrosteel Castings Limited was maintainable in light of the bar under Section 34 of the SARFAESI Act, 2002.
Case: Electrosteel Castings Limited vs. UV Asset Reconstruction Company Limited & Ors.

---

Q: Why was Pradeep Jain suspended and subsequently dismissed from his position?
A: Pradeep Jain was suspended and dismissed following disciplinary proceedings initiated due to his late reporting to work on three days after the death of his father.
Case: Pradeep S/O Rajkumar Jain vs. Manganese Ore (India) Limited & Ors.

---

Q: Why did the Assessing Officer reject the transaction value declared by M/S. Sanjivani Non-Ferrous Trading Pvt. Ltd. for the imported aluminum scrap?
A: The Assessing Officer found the declared transaction value to be low and reassessed the value by considering the grades of aluminum scrap and the quantum of other metals present.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise and Service Tax, Noida vs. M/S. Sanjivani Non-Ferrous Trading Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What were the main reasons for the Tribunal to reject the enhancement of the assessable value?
A: The Tribunal found that the Assessing Officer did not properly examine the evidence available with the department for enhancing the assessable value and failed to establish that the price was not the sole consideration of the transaction value.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise and Service Tax, Noida vs. M/S. Sanjivani Non-Ferrous Trading Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What is the significance of Section 9 of the Electricity Act, 2003 in this case?
A: Section 9 of the Electricity Act, 2003 provides that captive generation and use are permitted and do not require permission from the State Commission, thus not subjecting captive users to additional surcharge under Section 42(4).
Case: Maharashtra State Electricity Distribution Co. Ltd. vs. M/s. JSW Steel Limited & Ors.

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Kedar Nath Kohli (Dead) by LRs. vs. Baldev Singh?
A: The primary legal issue was whether Kedar Nath Kohli, represented by his legal representatives, could claim possession of Plot No. 27 in Wazir Nagar, New Delhi, which was allegedly in the illegal possession of Baldev Singh.
Case: Kedar Nath Kohli (Dead) by LRs. vs. Baldev Singh

---

Q: What was the basis of the income tax proceedings against M/s Rashtradoot (HUF)?
A: The income tax proceedings were initiated against M/s Rashtradoot (HUF) following a search operation conducted by the Income Tax Department on 4th September 1997, covering the block period from 1st April 1987 to 4th September 1997.
Case: Commissioner of Income Tax-I vs. M/s Rashtradoot (HUF)

---

Q: How did the Income Tax Appellate Tribunal (ITAT) rule on the issues arising from the case?
A: The ITAT ruled in favor of the respondent (M/s Rashtradoot (HUF)), allowing their appeal against the assessment order.
Case: Commissioner of Income Tax-I vs. M/s Rashtradoot (HUF)

---

Q: Why did the Division Bench set aside the Single Judge's order?
A: The Division Bench found that the Single Judge had not discussed any issues, provided reasoning, or dealt with the grounds raised by the parties, which led to the setting aside of the Single Judge's order.
Case: Sivakami & Ors. vs. State of Tamil Nadu & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main agreement between Anglo American Metallurgical Coal Pty Ltd. and MMTC Ltd.?
A: The main agreement was a Long Term Agreement (LTA) dated 7th March 2007, where Anglo American Metallurgical Coal Pty Ltd. agreed to supply certain quantities of freshly mined and washed 'German Creek', 'Isaac', and 'Moranbah North' coking coal to MMTC Ltd.
Case: Anglo American Metallurgical Coal Pty Ltd. vs. MMTC Ltd.
Date: 17th December 2020

---

Q: What was the finding of the Majority Award regarding the supply of coal by Anglo American Metallurgical Coal Pty Ltd.?
A: The Majority Award found that Anglo American Metallurgical Coal Pty Ltd. was at all material times in a position to perform its obligations under the Agreement by supplying the requisite quantities of coal in a timely manner.
Case: Anglo American Metallurgical Coal Pty Ltd. vs. MMTC Ltd.
Date: 17th December 2020

---

Q: What was the significance of the Bharatmala Pariyojna – Phase I project mentioned in the case 'The Project Director, Project Implementation Unit vs. P.V. Krishnamoorthy & Ors.'?
A: The Bharatmala Pariyojna – Phase I project was conceived to optimize the efficiency of freight and passenger movement across the country by bridging critical infrastructure gaps through the development of economic corridors, inter-corridors, feeder routes, and other key infrastructure.
Case: The Project Director, Project Implementation Unit vs. P.V. Krishnamoorthy & Ors.
Date: 8th December 2020

---

Q: What were the key dates related to Ashok Khemka's performance appraisal report (PAR) for the period 2016-2017?
A: The key dates were: self-appraisal submitted on 7th June 2017, Reporting Authority's grade of 8.22 on 8th June 2017, Reviewing Authority's upgraded grade of 9.92 on 27th June 2017, and Accepting Authority's downgraded grade of 9 on 31st December 2017.
Case: The State of Haryana vs. Ashok Khemka & Anr.

---

Q: What post was Premlata offered after failing the physical fitness examination for the post of Workshop Hand?
A: After failing the physical fitness examination, Premlata was offered the post of Messenger in the Police Radio Department.
Case: The State of Uttar Pradesh & Ors. vs. Premlata

---

Q: What was the significant change in the OROP policy implementation as per the letter dated 7th November 2015?
A: The significant change was that instead of an automatic revision of pension rates, the revision would now occur at periodic intervals of every five years, effectively altering the initial principle of OROP.
Case: Indian Ex Servicemen Movement & Ors. vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 16th March 2022

---

Q: How did the Union Government justify the revised definition of OROP?
A: The Union Government justified the revised definition of OROP by stating that it was practical and feasible to revise the pension every five years, and that doing so annually would be administratively difficult and impracticable.
Case: Indian Ex Servicemen Movement & Ors. vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 16th March 2022

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Neil Aurelio Nunes & Ors. vs. Union of India & Ors.?
A: The primary legal issue was the challenge to the implementation of a 27% reservation for Other Backward Classes (OBC) and a 10% reservation for the Economically Weaker Section (EWS) in the All India Quota (AIQ) seats for NEET-PG and NEET-UG for the admission year 2021-2022.
Case: Neil Aurelio Nunes & Ors. vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: What is the primary subject matter of the case 'State of Tamil Nadu vs. State of Karnataka and Another'?
A: The primary subject matter is a dispute between the State of Tamil Nadu and the State of Karnataka regarding the construction of dams and diversion structures by Karnataka, which Tamil Nadu claims affects the natural flow of the Pennaiyar river and its tributaries, impacting Tamil Nadu's water usage.
Case: State of Tamil Nadu vs. State of Karnataka and Another

---

Q: What relief did the State of Tamil Nadu seek in the case against the State of Karnataka?
A: Tamil Nadu sought a declaration that Karnataka's unilateral construction of dams and diversion structures is illegal, a permanent injunction to restrain Karnataka from further construction, and a direction to ensure the natural flows of the Pennaiyar river to Tamil Nadu.
Case: State of Tamil Nadu vs. State of Karnataka and Another

---

Q: What was the State of Karnataka's defense regarding the construction projects on the Pennaiyar river?
A: Karnataka argued that the projects were primarily for supplying drinking water to drought-prone areas, were sanctioned and approved by the relevant authorities, and did not require Tamil Nadu's consent under the agreements of 1892 and 1933.
Case: State of Tamil Nadu vs. State of Karnataka and Another

---

Q: How did the Union of India respond to Tamil Nadu's complaint in the case?
A: The Union of India stated that no formal request for arbitration under the Agreement of 1892 was received from any state government, and thus no action could be taken. It also mentioned that the issue could be addressed by setting up a Water Disputes Tribunal under the Inter-State Water Disputes Act, 1956, but no request for such a tribunal had been made.
Case: State of Tamil Nadu vs. State of Karnataka and Another

---

Q: What are the significant projects undertaken by Karnataka that Tamil Nadu objected to?
A: Tamil Nadu objected to several projects, including the construction of a 500 Mcft capacity reservoir across the Markandeya river near Yargol village, and various schemes for pumping and diverting water in the Pennaiyar river basin.
Case: State of Tamil Nadu vs. State of Karnataka and Another

---

Q: What was the decision of the Appellate Court?
A: The Appellate Court reversed the Trial Court's decision, holding that the plaintiff had a half-share in all the suit properties and decreed his suit in its entirety. The counter-claim of defendant Nos. 1 to 4 was dismissed.
Case: Vitthalrao Marotirao Navkhare vs. Nanibai (Dead), through LRs, and others
Date: 8th April 2024

---

Q: What is the case 'M/S Bharti Airtel Limited vs. A.S. Raghavendra' about?
A: The case is about an employment dispute where A.S. Raghavendra, the respondent, alleged that his resignation from Bharti Airtel Limited was forced, and he should be considered a 'workman' under the Industrial Disputes Act.
Case: M/S Bharti Airtel Limited vs. A.S. Raghavendra

---

Q: What was the main legal issue in the case?
A: The main legal issue was whether A.S. Raghavendra could be considered a 'workman' under the Industrial Disputes Act, which would allow him to seek relief for alleged forced resignation.
Case: M/S Bharti Airtel Limited vs. A.S. Raghavendra

---

Q: What is the main legal issue addressed in the case between Arcelor Mittal Nippon Steel India Ltd. and Essar Bulk Terminal Ltd.?
A: The main legal issue is whether the Court has the power to entertain an application under Section 9(1) of the Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996, once an Arbitral Tribunal has been constituted and the meaning of the expression 'entertain' in Section 9(3) of the Arbitration Act.
Case: Arcelor Mittal Nippon Steel India Ltd. vs. Essar Bulk Terminal Ltd.
Date: 14th September 2021

---

Q: What was the Cargo Handling Agreement between Arcelor Mittal and Essar Bulk Terminal Ltd. about?
A: The Cargo Handling Agreement between Arcelor Mittal and Essar Bulk Terminal Ltd. was for Cargo Handling at Hazira Port, and it included an arbitration clause for settling disputes.
Case: Arcelor Mittal Nippon Steel India Ltd. vs. Essar Bulk Terminal Ltd.
Date: 14th September 2021

---

Q: How did the Respondent, Essar Bulk Terminal Ltd., respond to the notice of arbitration from Arcelor Mittal?
A: The Respondent, Essar Bulk Terminal Ltd., contended that the disputes were not arbitrable and claimed that the total amount due and payable by Arcelor Mittal was Rs.673.84 crores, inclusive of interest.
Case: Arcelor Mittal Nippon Steel India Ltd. vs. Essar Bulk Terminal Ltd.
Date: 14th September 2021

---

Q: What was the initial decision of the Trial Court in this case?
A: The Trial Court decreed the appellants' suit for specific performance of the agreement by judgment/decree dated 3rd July 1984.
Case: G. Saraswathi & Anr. vs. Rathinammal & Ors.
Date: 15th February 2018

---

Q: What were the deficiencies pointed out during the inspection conducted on 3rd and 4th November 2016?
A: The deficiencies included a shortfall in Teaching Faculty and Resident Doctors (4.5% and 3.50% respectively), issues with the Central Library not being air-conditioned, inadequate facilities in students' hostels, and lack of residential accommodation in RHTC, among others.
Case: IQ City Foundation & Anr. vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: What was the recommendation of the Hearing Committee regarding the deficiencies?
A: The Hearing Committee recommended that the deficiencies were not such as to warrant disapproval at that stage of the college.
Case: IQ City Foundation & Anr. vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: What was the outcome of the compliance verification conducted by the Assessors on 21st March 2017?
A: The compliance verification conducted by the Assessors on 21st March 2017 noted deficiencies such as a 15.90% deficiency of faculty, a 25.88% shortage of residents, and issues with patient admissions and hostel facilities.
Case: IQ City Foundation & Anr. vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: What deficiencies were pointed out during the surprise inspection conducted on 3rd and 4th November 2016?
A: The deficiencies included a shortfall in Teaching Faculty (4.5%) and Resident Doctors (3.50%), issues with the Central Library not being air-conditioned, inadequate facilities in students' hostels, and lack of residential accommodation in RHTC, among others.
Case: IQ City Foundation & Anr. vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: What did the Hearing Committee recommend regarding the deficiencies found in the inspection?
A: The Hearing Committee recommended that the deficiencies pointed out were not such as to warrant disapproval at that stage.
Case: IQ City Foundation & Anr. vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: What were the findings of the Compliance Verification Assessment conducted on 21st March 2017?
A: The Compliance Verification Assessment noted deficiencies such as a 15.90% deficiency of faculty, a 25.88% shortage of residents, and issues with patient admissions and hostel facilities.
Case: IQ City Foundation & Anr. vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: Why was the recall of the judgment sought by the legal heir of the deceased respondent Omkar Nath Dhar?
A: The recall was sought because the respondent had died on 16th March 2020 and the court had passed an order without seeking substitution of the legal representatives of the deceased respondent.
Case: Union of India & Anr. vs. Omkar Nath Dhar (D) Through L.Rs.

---

Q: Why are overhead power lines considered a threat to the Great Indian Bustard and Lesser Florican?
A: Overhead power lines are considered a threat because the Great Indian Bustard lacks frontal vision and cannot detect the power lines, leading to collisions and deaths. The Wildlife Institute of India reported that power line collisions are a significant cause of mortality for these birds.
Case: M.K. Ranjitsinh & Ors. vs. Union of India & Ors.
Date: 19th April 2021

---

Q: What offer did Mr. J.K. Sethi, the learned Deputy Advocate General of Uttarakhand, make before the Court?
A: Mr. J.K. Sethi offered that the State Government would take over Respondent No. 5 College along with all its assets, infrastructure, and apply for affiliation with Hemwati Nandan Bahuguna, Uttarakhand Medical University, Dehradun.
Case: Sourabh Brala & Ors. vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: What was the role of the Director General of Police, Dehradun, as per the Court's order?
A: The Director General of Police, Dehradun, was directed to seal the premises of Respondent No. 5 College immediately to ensure compliance with the Court's order.
Case: Sourabh Brala & Ors. vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: What was the age and condition of Kajal at the time of the accident?
A: Kajal was a bright young girl of 12 years. She suffered serious injuries resulting in damage to her brain, leaving her with a very low IQ and severe weakness in all her limbs, severe hysteria, and severe urinary incontinence.
Case: Kajal vs. Jagdish Chand & Ors.
Date: 5th February 2020

---

Q: What were the main injuries and their consequences for Kajal?
A: Kajal suffered head injuries that resulted in her having an IQ of less than 20% of a child of her age and a social age of only a 9-month-old child. She has severe weakness in all her limbs, severe hysteria, and severe urinary incontinence. Her disability has been assessed as 100%.
Case: Kajal vs. Jagdish Chand & Ors.
Date: 5th February 2020

---

Q: What legislative framework did the State of Maharashtra enact to regulate the issuance and verification of caste certificates?
A: The State of Maharashtra enacted the Maharashtra Scheduled Castes, Scheduled Tribes, De-Notified Tribes (Vimukta Jatis), Nomadic Tribes, Other Backward Classes and Special Backward Category (Regulation of Issuance and Verification of) Caste Certificate Act, 2000, which established procedures for obtaining and verifying caste certificates and imposed penalties for false claims.
Case: Chairman and Managing Director FCI and Ors vs. Jagdish Balaram Bahira and Ors

---

Q: What actions did the National Green Tribunal (NGT) take during the pendency of the Original Application in the case?
A: The NGT called for a report from the Principal Chief Conservator of Forests (HoFF) after conducting an inspection of the Eco-Sensitive Zone surrounding the Kuldiha Wildlife Sanctuary. Based on the report, the NGT directed the State Government to take steps to bring the entire corridor within the ambit of the eco-sensitive zone and prohibit ingress into the zone.
Case: Binay Kumar Dalei & Ors. v. State of Odisha & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main contention of the appellants in the case?
A: The appellants contended that their stone quarries were not among the 11 quarries that had ingressed into the eco-sensitive zone, and they argued that their quarries were situated on the other side of the hillock, thus there was no reason to stop their operation. They also claimed that the NGT refused to hear their interests before passing the order.
Case: Binay Kumar Dalei & Ors. v. State of Odisha & Ors.

---

Q: What was the response of the State of Odisha regarding the implementation of the Comprehensive Wildlife Management Plan?
A: The State of Odisha submitted that the mining operations would be permitted only after the implementation of the Comprehensive Wildlife Management Plan. They also stated that the process for declaring the traditional elephant corridor as a conservation reserve was in an advanced stage of consideration.
Case: Binay Kumar Dalei & Ors. v. State of Odisha & Ors.

---

Q: What was the respondents' claim regarding the selection process in the recruitment for Post Graduate Assistants in Chemistry?
A: The respondents claimed that meritorious candidates under the MBC quota, who would have been selected irrespective of any reservation, were not considered under the general vacancies but were appointed in the MBC/DNC quota against the backlog vacancies. This caused the respondents not to be appointed.
Case: The State of Tamil Nadu & Ors. vs. K. Shobana Etc. Etc.

---

Q: How does the notification dated 4th March 2021 impact the educational opportunities of OCI cardholders?
A: The notification dated 4th March 2021 restricts OCI cardholders to only NRI quota and supernumerary seats, denying them the opportunity to compete with Indian citizens for general seats in medical entrance exams, which limits their educational opportunities significantly.
Case: Anushka Rengunthwar & Ors. vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 3rd February 2023

---

Q: What was the main issue in the dispute between M/S. Nandhini Deluxe and Karnataka Co-operative Milk Producers Federation Ltd.?
A: The main issue was the use of the mark 'NANDHINI' by M/S. Nandhini Deluxe for its restaurants, which the Karnataka Co-operative Milk Producers Federation Ltd. claimed was deceptively similar to their mark 'NANDINI' used for milk and milk products, leading to potential public confusion.
Case: M/S. Nandhini Deluxe vs Karnataka Co-operative Milk Producers Federation Ltd.
Date: 26th July 2018

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the dispute between Vedanta Limited and the State of Tamil Nadu?
A: The primary issue was the closure of Vedanta Limited's copper smelter in Thoothukudi by the Tamil Nadu Pollution Control Board (TNPCB) and the Government of Tamil Nadu due to violations of numerous environmental norms.
Case: Vedanta Limited vs The State of Tamil Nadu & Ors
Date: 29th February 2024

---

Q: What were the main violations cited by the Tamil Nadu Pollution Control Board against Vedanta Limited?
A: The violations included operating without proper consent for about sixteen years, lack of hazardous waste management authorization for about ten years, improper disposal of hazardous waste, substantial presence of Total Dissolved Solids (TDS) in the water, dumping large amounts of copper slag leading to air and water pollution, and failure to maintain a mandated green belt.
Case: Vedanta Limited vs The State of Tamil Nadu & Ors
Date: 29th February 2024

---

Q: What was the primary concern addressed in the case of Sampurna Behura vs Union of India & Ors.?
A: The primary concern was the virtual non-implementation or tardy implementation of laws beneficial to children, particularly the Juvenile Justice (Care and Protection of Children) Act, 2000 and the Juvenile Justice (Care and Protection of Children) Act, 2015.
Case: Sampurna Behura vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 9th February 2018

---

Q: What did the Chief Justices' Conferences resolve regarding the juvenile justice system?
A: The Chief Justices' Conferences resolved to expedite the setting up of Juvenile Justice Boards, ensure periodic visits to Juvenile Homes by nominated judges, and improve the conditions of these homes and the living conditions of juveniles, among other measures.
Case: Sampurna Behura vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 9th February 2018

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Mukul Kumar Tyagi vs The State of Uttar Pradesh & Ors.?
A: The main issue was the validity of the recruitment process for the post of Technician Grade-II in the Uttar Pradesh Power Corporation Limited (UPPCL), specifically regarding the requirement of a Course on Computer Concept (CCC) certificate or its equivalent.
Case: Mukul Kumar Tyagi vs The State of Uttar Pradesh & Ors.
Date: 16th December 2019

---

Q: What did the advertisement for the recruitment of Technician Grade-II specify regarding computer qualifications?
A: The advertisement specified that candidates must have a CCC certificate or an equivalent computer qualification certificate to be eligible for the post of Technician Grade-II.
Case: Mukul Kumar Tyagi vs The State of Uttar Pradesh & Ors.
Date: 16th December 2019

---

Q: What was the contention of the appellants regarding the CCC certificates?
A: The appellants contended that many candidates included in the select list did not possess the CCC certificate from DOEACC/NIELIT or an equivalent qualification, and the selection process relied on self-certification by candidates without proper verification.
Case: Mukul Kumar Tyagi vs The State of Uttar Pradesh & Ors.
Date: 16th December 2019

---

Q: What was the ruling of the learned Single Judge regarding the select list?
A: The learned Single Judge quashed the select list to the extent it included candidates who did not hold a CCC certificate from DOEACC/NIELIT or an equivalent qualification recognized in law, and directed the Commission to redraw the select list accordingly.
Case: Mukul Kumar Tyagi vs The State of Uttar Pradesh & Ors.
Date: 16th December 2019

---

Q: What did the Disaster Management Act, 2005 and the subsequent rules entail regarding the NDRF?
A: The Disaster Management Act, 2005 led to the creation of the NDRF, and the Disaster Management (National Disaster Response Force) Rules, 2008 detailed the organization and administration of the NDRF, including the transfer of control of battalions from various Central Para Military Forces to the NDRF.
Case: Union of India & Ors. vs R. Thiyagarajan
Date: 3rd April 2020

---

Q: What was the contention of the Union of India regarding the payment of deputation allowance?
A: The Union of India contended that deputation allowance was only payable from 14th January 2013, as per an office memorandum, and argued that the personnel of the Central Para Military Forces were not on deputation until the NDRF formed its own battalions on 13th January 2010.
Case: Union of India & Ors. vs R. Thiyagarajan
Date: 3rd April 2020

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Shri Venkateshwara University vs Union of India & Another?
A: The primary issue was whether Shri Venkateshwara University should be permitted to admit students to its MBBS course for the academic sessions 2017-2018 and 2018-2019, despite the Medical Council of India's (MCI) decision to debar the University due to deficiencies found during inspections.
Case: Shri Venkateshwara University vs Union of India & Another
Date: 1st September 2017

---

Q: What deficiencies were identified by the MCI in their inspections of Shri Venkateshwara University?
A: The deficiencies included faculty members joining just before the inspection, vague complaints of patients, lack of permanent address proof for many faculty members, absence of labor patients and surgeries on the inspection day, and incomplete information on the college website about faculty members.
Case: Shri Venkateshwara University vs Union of India & Another
Date: 1st September 2017

---

Q: What was the Oversight Committee's stance on the deficiencies found during the inspection of Shri Venkateshwara University?
A: The Oversight Committee accepted the University's explanations for the deficiencies and recommended the grant of a Letter of Permission for the academic year 2016-2017, subject to certain conditions, including the provision of a bank guarantee and the removal of deficiencies.
Case: Shri Venkateshwara University vs Union of India & Another
Date: 1st September 2017

---

Q: What was the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare's final decision regarding Shri Venkateshwara University's application for renewal of permission for the academic year 2017-2018?
A: The Ministry reiterated its earlier decision to debar Shri Venkateshwara University from admitting students for the academic years 2017-2018 and 2018-2019 and authorized the MCI to encash the bank guarantee of Rs.2 crore, based on the recommendations of the Hearing Committee and the deficiencies found during inspections.
Case: Shri Venkateshwara University vs Union of India & Another
Date: 1st September 2017

---

Q: What qualifications did Capt. Paul Nadar Bennet Singh possess that were under scrutiny?
A: Capt. Paul Nadar Bennet Singh possessed a Certificate of Competency as Master issued by the Maritime and Port Authority of Singapore, along with qualifications in MBA (Shipping and Port Management), Master of Human Resource, Advanced Diploma in Maritime Transportation, and Master of Science in Counselling and Psychotherapy.

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Puneet Sharma & Ors. vs Himachal Pradesh State Electricity Board Ltd. & Anr.?

---

Q: What was the Himachal Pradesh State Electricity Board's stance on the eligibility of degree holders for the post of Junior Engineer (Electrical)?
A: The HPSEB adopted a neutral position but highlighted that the applicable regulations specified a diploma as the minimum qualification for the post of Junior Engineer (Electrical). They asserted that the Himachal Pradesh Staff Selection Commission (HPSSC) was bound to make recruitments in accordance with these regulations.
Case: Puneet Sharma & Ors. vs Himachal Pradesh State Electricity Board Ltd. & Anr.

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Nilay Gupta vs Chairman NEET PG Medical and Dental Admission/Counselling Board 2020 & Ors.?

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Gajubha Jadeja Jesar vs Union of India & Ors.?
A: The primary issue was whether the project proponent's Cold Rolled Coils unit required prior environmental clearance under the Environment Impact Assessment (EIA) notification dated 14th September 2006.
Case: Gajubha Jadeja Jesar vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 10th August 2022

---

Q: What was the National Green Tribunal's (NGT) decision regarding the Cold Rolled Coils unit?
A: The NGT concluded that the Cold Rolled Coils unit required an environmental clearance and initially stayed all activities of the project. However, the NGT later allowed the unit to operate for one year to obtain the necessary clearance, based on a recommendation from the Expert Appraisal Committee (EAC).
Case: Gajubha Jadeja Jesar vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 10th August 2022

---

Q: What recommendation did the Expert Appraisal Committee (EAC) make regarding the Cold Rolled Coils unit?
A: The EAC recommended granting a one-year grace period for the Cold Rolled Coils unit to obtain the required environmental clearance, given the ambiguity in the EIA notification and the fact that the unit was already operating with consent from the State Pollution Control Board.
Case: Gajubha Jadeja Jesar vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 10th August 2022

---

Q: How did the Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change respond to the EAC's recommendation?
A: The Ministry filed an affidavit supporting the EAC's recommendation, agreeing to provide a one-year grace period for units like the Cold Rolled Coils unit to obtain environmental clearance, addressing the ambiguity in the EIA notification.
Case: Gajubha Jadeja Jesar vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 10th August 2022

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Meena Pradhan & Ors. vs Kamla Pradhan & Anr.?
A: The primary issue was the validity of the Will executed by Bahadur Pradhan on 30th July 1992, and whether the Letter of Administration issued in favor of Kamla Pradhan and her daughter was justified.
Case: Meena Pradhan & Ors. vs Kamla Pradhan & Anr.
Date: 21st September 2023

---

Q: What was the contention of the appellants regarding the execution of the Will?
A: The appellants contended that the Will was forged and raised objections about the authenticity of the document, as well as questioning the testator's mental state and the presence of undue influence during the execution of the Will.
Case: Meena Pradhan & Ors. vs Kamla Pradhan & Anr.
Date: 21st September 2023

---

Q: What were the allegations against Dr. Gajendra Singh regarding the issuance of the insurance cover note?
A: Dr. Gajendra Singh was alleged to have issued an insurance cover note (No. 543675) without collecting any premium, causing financial loss to the insurance company when a claim was filed based on this cover note after an accident.
Case: Dr. Gajendra Singh vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 11th July 2022

---

Q: What was Dr. Gajendra Singh's explanation for issuing the insurance cover note without collecting the premium?
A: Dr. Gajendra Singh explained that he issued the cover note based on the assurance from the insured that the premium would be sent through his person. However, the insured applied for another policy with a cheque that later bounced.
Case: Dr. Gajendra Singh vs Union of India & Ors.
Date: 11th July 2022

---

Q: What was the reason for Methu Meda's acquittal in the kidnapping case?
A: Methu Meda was acquitted because the key witnesses, including the complainant and his wife, turned hostile during the trial, and the prosecution failed to prove the charges beyond a reasonable doubt.
Case: Union of India & Ors. vs Methu Meda
Date: 6th October 2021

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of K. Arumuga Velaiah vs P.R. Ramasamy and Anr.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the properties claimed by the plaintiff, K. Arumuga Velaiah, were part of the joint family properties and whether the will executed by Periyaiya Servai in favor of the plaintiff was valid.
Case: K. Arumuga Velaiah vs P.R. Ramasamy and Anr.
Date: 27th January 2022

---

Q: What was the plaintiff's claim regarding the will executed by Periyaiya Servai?
A: The plaintiff claimed that Periyaiya Servai had executed a registered will on 26th January 1994, bequeathing his share of the joint family properties to the plaintiff.
Case: K. Arumuga Velaiah vs P.R. Ramasamy and Anr.
Date: 27th January 2022

---

Q: What was the defense's argument against the plaintiff's claim?
A: The defense argued that the properties had already been partitioned in 1964 among Periyaiya Servai's sons and that the will claimed by the plaintiff was invalid as Periyaiya Servai had no right to bequeath the properties which were already partitioned.
Case: K. Arumuga Velaiah vs P.R. Ramasamy and Anr.
Date: 27th January 2022

---

Q: What was the finding of the Trial Court regarding the partition deed of 1964?
A: The Trial Court found that the partition deed of 1964 was valid, and the properties were already divided among Periyaiya Servai's sons, thus dismissing the plaintiff's claim.
Case: K. Arumuga Velaiah vs P.R. Ramasamy and Anr.
Date: 27th January 2022

---

Q: What were the objections raised by Gas Point Petroleum India Limited regarding the auction sale?
A: Gas Point Petroleum India Limited raised objections that the auction sale violated Order 21 Rule 84 and Order 21 Rule 85 of the CPC, as the auction purchaser did not deposit 25% of the sale amount immediately and the balance 75% within the prescribed 15-day period.
Case: Gas Point Petroleum India Limited vs Rajendra Marothi & Ors.
Date: 10th February 2023

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Anjana Saraiya vs The State of U.P. & Ors.?
A: The primary issue was whether the cancellation of Anjana Saraiya's allotment of Plot No. 415 in the Organized Development Scheme, Phase-III, Pilkhuwa, District Ghaziabad, due to delayed installment payments was justified.
Case: Anjana Saraiya vs The State of U.P. & Ors.
Date: 12th May 2022

---

Q: What were the reasons given by Anjana Saraiya for the delayed payment of installments?
A: Anjana Saraiya cited her husband's continuous ill-health and resulting financial crisis as reasons for her inability to pay the fourth to seventh installments on time. Despite these challenges, she managed to secure the money and clear all dues, including interest, before receiving the cancellation notice.
Case: Anjana Saraiya vs The State of U.P. & Ors.
Date: 12th May 2022

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Shakti Vahini vs Union of India and others?
A: The main issue was whether the State and Central Governments should take preventive steps to combat honour crimes, including honour killings, and to provide protection and legal measures for couples facing threats from family or community for marrying against traditional norms.
Case: Shakti Vahini vs Union of India and others
Date: 27th March 2018

---

Q: What was Ex. Ct. Mahadev's defense regarding the shooting of Nandan Deb?
A: Ex. Ct. Mahadev claimed he acted in self-defense when confronted by a group of armed intruders, including Nandan Deb, who were smuggling across the border and threatened his life.
Case: Ex. Ct. Mahadev vs The Director General, Border Security Force & Ors.
Date: 14th June 2022

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of R. Hemalatha vs Kasthuri?
A: The primary issue was whether an unregistered Agreement to Sell dated 10th September 2013 could be admitted as evidence in a suit for specific performance despite the Tamil Nadu Amendment Act No. 29 of 2012 requiring such agreements to be registered.
Case: R. Hemalatha vs Kasthuri

---

Q: What did the trial court decide regarding the admissibility of the unregistered Agreement to Sell in the case of R. Hemalatha vs Kasthuri?
A: The trial court held the preliminary issue in favor of the defendant, R. Hemalatha, ruling that the unregistered Agreement to Sell dated 10th September 2013 was inadmissible in evidence.
Case: R. Hemalatha vs Kasthuri

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Dasanglu Pul vs Lupalum Kri?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the nomination paper of Dasanglu Pul, the returned candidate, was improperly accepted due to non-disclosure of properties belonging to her deceased spouse in the affidavit filed with her nomination.
Case: Dasanglu Pul vs Lupalum Kri

---

Q: What were the conflicting precedents considered in this case?
A: The conflicting precedents considered were Kothari Products Ltd. vs State of A.P., Radheshyam Gudakhu Factory vs State of Orissa, and Reliance Trading Co. vs State of Kerala on one side, and CST vs Agra Belting Works on the other. These cases provided differing interpretations on whether items covered by the ADE Act could be taxed under state laws.
Case: M/S Trimurthi Fragrances (P) Ltd. vs Government of N.C.T. of Delhi

---

Q: What were the relevant central enactments discussed in this case?
A: The relevant central enactments were the Central Sales Tax Act, 1956 (CST Act), the Central Excise Tariff Act, 1985 (CET Act), and the Additional Duties of Excise (Goods of Special Importance) Act, 1957 (ADE Act).
Case: M/S Trimurthi Fragrances (P) Ltd. vs Govt. of N.C.T. of Delhi

---

Q: What was the court's decision regarding the interpretation of the DST Act?
A: The court held that the provisions of the DST Act, particularly Sections 3, 4, and 7, were to be interpreted in such a way that goods listed as 'tax-free' under the Third Schedule would not be subject to sales tax unless specifically amended by the competent authority. The inclusion of gutkha in the taxable list was found to be valid.
Case: M/S Trimurthi Fragrances (P) Ltd. vs Govt. of N.C.T. of Delhi

---

Q: What was the date of the agreement to sell between FERRODOUS ESTATES and the respondents?
A: The agreement to sell was dated 12th June 1980.
Case: FERRODOUS ESTATES (PVT.) LTD. vs. P. GOPIRATHNAM (DEAD) & ORS.

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Jayesh H. Pandya & Anr. vs. Subhtex India Ltd. & Ors.?
A: The primary issue was whether the appellants had waived their right to the extension of time for the completion of arbitration proceedings and making the award beyond the stipulated period of four months.
Case: Jayesh H. Pandya & Anr. vs. Subhtex India Ltd. & Ors.

---

Q: What led to the dispute regarding the extension of time for arbitration proceedings?
A: The appellants contended that they had not consented to an extension of time beyond the stipulated four months, whereas the respondents claimed that the appellants had, by their conduct, waived their right to object to the extension.
Case: Jayesh H. Pandya & Anr. vs. Subhtex India Ltd. & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main legal issue in the case of K. Raveendranathan Nair & Anr. vs. Commissioner of Income Tax & Ors.?
A: The main legal issue was the interpretation and application of court fees for appeals filed under Section 260A of the Income Tax Act, 1961, specifically whether the amended provision in Section 52A of the Kerala Court Fees and Suits Valuation Act, 1959 applied retrospectively.
Case: K. Raveendranathan Nair & Anr. vs. Commissioner of Income Tax & Ors.

---

Q: What did Section 260A of the Income Tax Act, 1961, introduced in 1998, provide for?
A: Section 260A of the Income Tax Act, 1961, introduced in 1998, provided for a statutory appeal against the orders passed by the Income Tax Appellate Tribunal, including a prescribed court fee of Rs. 2,000 under sub-section (2)(b).
Case: K. Raveendranathan Nair & Anr. vs. Commissioner of Income Tax & Ors.

---

Q: What did the respondent, Hindustan Construction Co. Ltd., seek through their application under Section 9 of the Arbitration Act?
A: Hindustan Construction Co. Ltd. sought an interim injunction against the encashment of the Performance Bank Guarantee and forfeiture of the security deposit.
Case: General Manager East Coast Railway Rail Sadan & Anr. vs. Hindustan Construction Co. Ltd.

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Maharashtra State Electricity Distribution Co. Ltd. vs. The Appellate Authority & Anr.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the Maharashtra State Electricity Distribution Co. Ltd. (the Board) could levy charges under Section 126 of the Electricity Act, 2003 for unauthorized use of electricity detected during an inspection of the respondent's factory.
Case: Maharashtra State Electricity Distribution Co. Ltd. vs. The Appellate Authority & Anr.

---

Q: What led to the issuance of a provisional assessment bill by the Board?
A: The Board issued a provisional assessment bill after an inspection on 02.08.2003 revealed tampering with the meter seals at the respondent's factory, leading to a revised provisional bill for Rs. 21,38,660/-.
Case: Maharashtra State Electricity Distribution Co. Ltd. vs. The Appellate Authority & Anr.

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.?
A: The primary issue was whether the charges realized by the assessees for providing containers to customers are liable to be included in the transaction value for the purpose of excise duty under Section 4 of the Central Excise Act, 1944.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

---

Q: What products were manufactured by the assessees in this case?
A: The assessees were manufacturers of dissolved and compressed industrial gases, liquid chlorine, cotton yarn, and Post Mix Concentrate.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

---

Q: What was the final decision of the court in this case?
A: The court concluded that the measure of the levy contemplated in Section 4 of the Act is not controlled by the nature of the levy. The court upheld that the transaction value, as defined in Section 4(3)(d) of the Act, statutorily includes the permissible additions to the manufacturing cost and manufacturing profit.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

---

Q: How did the appellate authority, the Commissioner, rule on the appeal filed by the defendants?
A: The Commissioner dismissed the appeal, upholding the decision of the Additional Deputy Commissioner. The Commissioner found no reason to interfere with the order, as it was in conformity with the award of the majority members of the Panchayat.
Case: Janam Singh Kudada & Anr. vs. State of Bihar & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Vashist Narayan Kumar vs. The State of Bihar & Ors.?
A: The main issue was whether the error in the date of birth on the application form, which was inadvertently filled as 08.12.1997 instead of 18.12.1997, was significant enough to disqualify Vashist Narayan Kumar from the selection process for the post of Police Constable.
Case: Vashist Narayan Kumar vs. The State of Bihar & Ors.

---

Q: What was Vashist Narayan Kumar's explanation for the incorrect date of birth on his application form?
A: Vashist Narayan Kumar explained that the error occurred while filling out the form at a cyber café in a nearby town. The date of birth was entered as 08.12.1997 instead of 18.12.1997, but this error did not provide him any benefit as he met the eligibility criteria regardless.
Case: Vashist Narayan Kumar vs. The State of Bihar & Ors.

---

Q: What was the stance of the respondents regarding the error in the application form?
A: The respondents argued that the advertisement clearly required the date of birth to match the 10th board certificate and that any discrepancies would result in cancellation of the application. They maintained that the appellant did not utilize the correction facility provided in the advertisement.
Case: Vashist Narayan Kumar vs. The State of Bihar & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of THE NEW INDIA ASSURANCE CO. LTD. vs. SRI BUCHIYYAMMA RICE MILL & ANR.?
A: The main issue was whether the damage to the rice mill's boiler unit and other components was caused by a lorry collision, as claimed by Sri Buchiyyamma Rice Mill, or by structural failure due to overloading, as argued by the insurance company.
Case: THE NEW INDIA ASSURANCE CO. LTD. vs. SRI BUCHIYYAMMA RICE MILL & ANR.

---

Q: What was the conclusion of the preliminary surveyor regarding the incident?
A: The preliminary surveyor concluded that it was unlikely the incident had occurred in the manner claimed by the insured. The surveyor noted that the damage to the boiling unit structure was not consistent with a lorry collision and suggested that a structural failure might be the real cause of the damage.
Case: THE NEW INDIA ASSURANCE CO. LTD. vs. SRI BUCHIYYAMMA RICE MILL & ANR.

---

Q: What did the structural expert from Andhra University conclude about the cause of the damage?
A: The structural expert from Andhra University concluded that there was no external impact on the failed columns, the storage capacity of the system had been breached, and the incident resulted from exceeding the load-carrying capacity of the column section, leading to the collapse of the entire paddy boiling structure.
Case: THE NEW INDIA ASSURANCE CO. LTD. vs. SRI BUCHIYYAMMA RICE MILL & ANR.

---

Q: What is the main legal issue addressed in the case of Damodar Valley Corporation vs Central Electricity Regulatory Commission & Others?
A: The main legal issue addressed in this case is the challenge by Damodar Valley Corporation (DVC) against the order of the Appellate Tribunal which dismissed their appeal against the tariff order determined by the Central Electricity Regulatory Commission (CERC).
Case: Damodar Valley Corporation vs Central Electricity Regulatory Commission & Others

---

Q: Under which section of the Electricity Act 2003 was the appeal maintained in the Damodar Valley Corporation case?
A: The appeal was maintained under Section 125 of the Electricity Act 2003.
Case: Damodar Valley Corporation vs Central Electricity Regulatory Commission & Others

---

Q: What are the main functions of Damodar Valley Corporation as stated in the judgement?
A: Damodar Valley Corporation is entrusted with multifarious functions including the generation, transmission, and distribution of electrical energy (both hydro and thermal), as well as operating schemes for irrigation, water supply, drainage, and flood control in the Damodar river and its tributaries.
Case: Damodar Valley Corporation vs Central Electricity Regulatory Commission & Others

---

Q: What was the outcome of the initial appeal filed by Damodar Valley Corporation to the Appellate Tribunal?
A: The initial appeal filed by Damodar Valley Corporation was allowed by the Appellate Tribunal, which remanded the matter to the Central Commission for de novo consideration of the tariff order dated 3rd October 2006.
Case: Damodar Valley Corporation vs Central Electricity Regulatory Commission & Others

---

Q: What were the two main contentions raised by Damodar Valley Corporation in their present appeal?
A: The two main contentions raised by Damodar Valley Corporation were: (1) the Commission and the Appellate Tribunal did not give the benefit of Section 38 of the DVC Act in the computation of tariff, and (2) the improper treatment of cumulative depreciation as repayment of loan, thereby reducing the notional loan component in the capital cost.
Case: Damodar Valley Corporation vs Central Electricity Regulatory Commission & Others

---

Q: What is Section 38 of the DVC Act, and how does it relate to the case?
A: Section 38 of the DVC Act states that the Corporation shall pay interest on the amount of capital provided by each participating Government, which is deemed part of the Corporation's expenditure. The case involves whether this interest on capital should be considered in addition to other tariff elements such as interest on loan and return on equity.
Case: Damodar Valley Corporation vs Central Electricity Regulatory Commission & Others

---

Q: What was the Karnataka State Administrative Tribunal's ruling on the respondent's application?
A: The Tribunal allowed the respondent's application, holding that the amendment to the Rules on 20th June 2012 would apply retrospectively to cover the respondent's case, even though his sister died in harness on 8th December 2010.
Case: The Secretary to Govt. Department of Education (Primary) & Ors. vs. Bheemesh Alias Bheemappa

---

Q: What was the decision of the Munsif, Danapur regarding the application filed under Order 7 Rule 11?
A: The Munsif, Danapur rejected the application filed under Order 7 Rule 11 on the ground that determining the question of limitation required oral evidence and thus could only be adjudicated after both parties had led their evidence.
Case: Raghwendra Sharan Singh vs Ram Prasanna Singh

---

Q: What was the primary issue addressed in the case of Avishek Raja & Ors. vs Sanjay Gupta?
A: The primary issue addressed was the non-implementation of the Majithia Wage Board Award by various newspaper establishments, as per the recommendations approved and notified by the Central Government.
Case: Avishek Raja & Ors. vs Sanjay Gupta

---

Q: Who were the appellants and respondents in the case?
A: The appellants were Govindammal (Dead) By Lrs. and others, and the respondents were Vaidiyanathan and others.
Case: Govindammal (Dead) By Lrs. and Ors. vs. Vaidiyanathan and Ors.

---

Q: What justification did the Union of India provide for the extensions granted to the Director of Enforcement?
A: The Union of India argued that the extensions were necessary for public interest, particularly citing the ongoing mutual evaluation of India by the Financial Action Task Force (FATF), which required continuity in leadership for effective representation and completion of the evaluation process.
Case: Dr. Jaya Thakur vs. Union of India & Ors.

Case: E S Krishnamurthy & Ors. vs M/s Bharath Hi Tech Builders Pvt. Ltd.

Case: E S Krishnamurthy & Ors. vs M/s Bharath Hi Tech Builders Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What were the objections raised by Sheoli Hati regarding Aditi's admission to Good Shepherd International School?
A: Sheoli Hati objected that Aditi was not doing well in the boarding school and had suffered medical issues.
Case: Sheoli Hati vs Somnath Das

Q: What were the circumstances of the accident involving Harish Singh Arya?
A: Harish Singh Arya was hit by a Tata Sumo vehicle while answering nature's call on the side of the road, leading to his death while being taken to the hospital.
Case: Smt. Sangita Arya & Ors. vs. Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd. & Ors.

---

Q: What statutory obligation does the National Food Security Act, 2013 impose on the state regarding nutritional support?
A: The National Food Security Act, 2013 imposes a statutory obligation on the state to provide nutritional support to pregnant women, lactating mothers, and children, ensuring they meet the nutritional standards specified in Schedule II of the Act.
Case: Dipika Jagatram Sahani vs Union of India

---

Q: What was the court's directive concerning the reopening of Anganwadi Centres?
A: The court directed all States and Union Territories to reopen Anganwadi Centres outside containment zones by 31st January 2021, unless there were specific reasons for not reopening them, and to ensure the provision of nutritional support as mandated by the National Food Security Act, 2013.
Case: Dipika Jagatram Sahani vs Union of India

---

Q: How did the court suggest monitoring and ensuring the benefits reach the intended beneficiaries?
A: The court suggested that all States and Union Territories implement a monitoring and supervision mechanism for Anganwadi Centres to ensure benefits reach the beneficiaries and establish a Complaint Redressal Mechanism in each district.
Case: Dipika Jagatram Sahani vs Union of India

---

Q: What was the business of the appellant, M/S Bhagwandas B. Ramchandani?
A: The appellant, M/S Bhagwandas B. Ramchandani, is a sole proprietary concern engaged in the business of imports and exports.
Case: M/S Bhagwandas B. Ramchandani vs. British Airways
Date: 29th July 2022

---

Q: What caused the damage to the appellant's cargo on 4th January 2010?
A: The cargo containing fruits and vegetables was damaged and destroyed due to bad weather conditions in London, which prevented the flight from departing to Canada.
Case: M/S Bhagwandas B. Ramchandani vs. British Airways
Date: 29th July 2022

---

Q: What was the claim amount lodged by the appellant on 20th July 2010?
A: The appellant lodged a claim amounting to ₹4,27,922/- for the destruction of the cargo sent on 30th June 2010 due to packaging and other issues.
Case: M/S Bhagwandas B. Ramchandani vs. British Airways
Date: 29th July 2022

---

Q: What was the preliminary issue decided by the Trial Court regarding the suit filed by the appellant?
A: The Trial Court framed a preliminary issue on the ground of limitation and held that the suit was not barred by limitation, as the period prescribed in Rule 30 of the Second Schedule to the Carriage by Air Act, 1972, could be calculated from 28th October 2010.
Case: M/S Bhagwandas B. Ramchandani vs. British Airways
Date: 29th July 2022

---

Q: What were the injuries sustained by the appellant, Sri Lakshmana Gowda B.N., in the road accident?
A: Sri Lakshmana Gowda B.N. sustained multiple cranial fractures, contusion of the right brachial plexus with right hemiplegia, and fractures involving the zygomatic arch and squamous temporal bones. He was treated as an in-patient for ten days and had a permanent disability of 75%.
Case: Sri Lakshmana Gowda B.N. vs. The Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd.

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of National Highways Authority of India v. M/s. Progressive Construction Ltd. decided on 12th February 2021?
