Case: National Highways Authority of India v. M/s. Progressive Construction Ltd.

---

Q: What was the decision of the Single Judge regarding the arbitral award in the case involving the National Highways Authority of India?
A: The Single Judge set aside the arbitral award, observing that the arbitral tribunal had drawn incorrect inferences from the documents on record and had not considered vital and relevant evidence in reaching its conclusions.
Case: National Highways Authority of India v. M/s. Progressive Construction Ltd.

---

Q: What was the outcome of the appeals filed by both parties under Section 37 before the Division Bench?
A: The Division Bench directed that the appeals be confined to the findings with respect to claim nos. 1(i) and (ii) and counter claim (a), and subsequently, the National Highways Authority of India filed an appeal challenging this interim order.
Case: National Highways Authority of India v. M/s. Progressive Construction Ltd.

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case M/s Reckitt Benckiser (India) Ltd. vs. Commissioner Commercial Taxes & Ors.?
A: The primary issue was the classification of various products like Mosquito Mats, Coils, Vaporizers, Mortein Insect Killers, Harpic Toilet Cleaner, Lizol Floor Cleaners, and Dettol Antiseptic Liquid under the Kerala VAT Act, 2003 for tax purposes.
Case: M/s Reckitt Benckiser (India) Ltd. vs. Commissioner Commercial Taxes & Ors.

---

Q: Which products did M/s Reckitt Benckiser (India) Ltd. argue should be classified under Entry No. 44(5) of the III Schedule to the Kerala VAT Act?
A: M/s Reckitt Benckiser (India) Ltd. argued that Mosquito Mats, Coils, Vaporizers, Mortein Insect Killers, Harpic Toilet Cleaner, and Lizol Floor Cleaners should be classified under Entry No. 44(5) of the III Schedule to the Kerala VAT Act as 'pesticides, insecticides' corresponding to HSN Code 3808.
Case: M/s Reckitt Benckiser (India) Ltd. vs. Commissioner Commercial Taxes & Ors.

---

Q: What was the Commissioner of Commercial Taxes' decision regarding the classification of the products?
A: The Commissioner of Commercial Taxes classified Mosquito Mats, Coils, and Vaporizers under Entry 66 of Notification SRO 82/06 as 'Mosquito Repellents,' Mortein Insect Killer under the residuary entry with a tax rate of 12.5%, Harpic Toilet Cleaner and Lizol Floor Cleaner under SI No. 27(4) of Notification SRO 82/06, and Dettol Antiseptic Liquid under the residual entry, not as a medicament.
Case: M/s Reckitt Benckiser (India) Ltd. vs. Commissioner Commercial Taxes & Ors.

---

Q: What was the reason for the dismissal of Phool Chand from the Rajasthan State Road Transport Corporation?
A: Phool Chand was dismissed due to dereliction of duties, specifically his continuous absence from work.
Case: Rajasthan State Road Transport Corporation, Jaipur vs. Shri Phool Chand (Dead) Through L.Rs.

---

Q: What initiated the legal dispute between Future Coupons Private Limited and Amazon?
A: The legal dispute was initiated by Amazon due to a sale transaction between Future Retail Limited (FRL) and the Reliance Group, which Amazon claimed violated their agreements with Future Coupons Private Limited (FCPL). Amazon initiated arbitration proceedings before the Singapore International Arbitration Center (SIAC) and sought an emergency relief to prevent the transaction.
Case: Future Coupons Private Limited & Ors. vs. Amazon.com NV Investment Holdings LLC & Ors.

---

Q: What was the outcome of the arbitration proceeding initiated by Amazon?
A: The Emergency Arbitrator, by order dated 25th October 2020, injuncted FRL from taking any steps to materialize the deal with Reliance, including proceedings before various regulatory authorities. The Arbitral Tribunal later dismissed the applications filed by FRL and FCPL to vacate this award.
Case: Future Coupons Private Limited & Ors. vs. Amazon.com NV Investment Holdings LLC & Ors.

---

Q: What was Amazon's intention when entering into agreements with Future Coupons Private Limited?
A: Amazon entered into Shareholder and Share-Subscription Agreements with FCPL on 22nd August 2019, intending to acquire a 49% stake in FCPL. These agreements included an arbitration agreement to settle disputes according to the Arbitration Rules of the Singapore International Arbitration Center (SIAC).
Case: Future Coupons Private Limited & Ors. vs. Amazon.com NV Investment Holdings LLC & Ors.

---

Q: What was Amazon's response to the sale transaction between Future Retail Limited and the Reliance Group?
A: Amazon initiated arbitration proceedings before the SIAC and sought an emergency relief to prevent FRL and FCPL from proceeding with the transaction. On 25th October 2020, the Emergency Arbitrator passed an interim award in favor of Amazon, directing FRL and FCPL to maintain the status quo regarding the transaction.
Case: Future Coupons Private Limited & Ors. vs. Amazon.com NV Investment Holdings LLC & Ors.

---

Q: What was the final outcome of the appeals filed by the State of Bihar & Ors.?

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Hamdard Institute of Medical Sciences and Research (HIMSR) & Anr. vs. Union of India and Ors.?
A: The main issue was the denial of renewal permission for admission of the 5th batch (100 seats) in the MBBS course for the academic session 2016-17 by the Medical Council of India (MCI) and the Central Government due to deficiencies found during inspections.
Case: Hamdard Institute of Medical Sciences and Research (HIMSR) & Anr. vs. Union of India and Ors.
Date: 8th September 2017

---

Q: What deficiencies were noted by the MCI in its inspection reports?
A: The MCI noted deficiencies such as the non-availability of a functional ventilator in the casualty, only 2 patients in the PICU on the day of assessment, only 2 static X-ray machines available against the requirement of 5, and other deficiencies detailed in the assessment report.
Case: Hamdard Institute of Medical Sciences and Research (HIMSR) & Anr. vs. Union of India and Ors.
Date: 8th September 2017

---

Q: What were the findings of the Hearing Committee after the personal hearing granted to HIMSR?
A: The Hearing Committee observed that the deficiency of faculty was marginal and within permissible limits, but it was not possible to validate the bed occupancy without physical verification. Despite these observations, the Ministry reiterated its earlier decision to debar the college.
Case: Hamdard Institute of Medical Sciences and Research (HIMSR) & Anr. vs. Union of India and Ors.
Date: 8th September 2017

---

Q: What prompted the State Government of Uttarakhand to decide against granting recognition to new B.Ed. colleges?
A: The State Government decided against granting recognition due to the surplus of B.Ed. graduates compared to the demand for teachers. Approximately 13,000 students were passing the B.Ed. course annually against a need for only 2,500 teachers, leading to potential unemployment for the remaining graduates.
Case: The State of Uttarakhand vs. Nalanda College of Education and Others
Date: 10th November 2022

---

Q: What was the appellant's argument regarding the value of the returned goods?
A: The appellant argued that the value of the returned goods should be considered after accounting for their market value as second-hand goods or, alternatively, as raw material for reuse in manufacturing.

Date: 5th September 2022

Q: What was the main issue in the case between Parakh Vanijya Private Limited and Baroma Agro Product?
A: The main issue was whether the respondent-defendant could use the word 'MALABAR' in conjunction with the mark 'BAROMA' for selling Biryani Rice, which the appellant claimed was infringing on their trademark 'MALABAR'.
Case: Parakh Vanijya Private Limited vs. Baroma Agro Product and Others

---

Q: What did the appellant-plaintiff claim regarding the use of the mark 'MALABAR'?
A: The appellant-plaintiff claimed to have been using the mark 'MALABAR' for selling Biryani Rice since 2001 and filed a suit for infringement and passing off, asserting that the respondent's use of 'MALABAR' was identical or deceptively similar to their trademark.
Case: Parakh Vanijya Private Limited vs. Baroma Agro Product and Others

---

Q: What was the Single Judge's order regarding the use of 'MALABAR' by the respondents?
A: The Single Judge allowed the respondents to use 'MALABAR' in conjunction with 'BAROMA', with the condition that the word 'MALABAR' could be 25% larger in font size than the rest of the words, but all words must be in the same font.
Case: Parakh Vanijya Private Limited vs. Baroma Agro Product and Others

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of State of Uttarakhand & Ors. vs. Kumaon Stone Crusher?
A: The primary issue was the levy of transit fee by the States of Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand, and Madhya Pradesh on forest produce, which was challenged by the respondent, Kumaon Stone Crusher.
Case: State of Uttarakhand & Ors. vs. Kumaon Stone Crusher
Date: 15th September 2017

---

Q: What legal provisions were under scrutiny in the case?
A: The case scrutinized the rules framed under Section 41 of the Indian Forest Act, 1927, including the Uttar Pradesh Transit of Timber & Other Forest Produce Rules, 1978, and similar rules by the States of Uttarakhand and Madhya Pradesh.
Case: State of Uttarakhand & Ors. vs. Kumaon Stone Crusher
Date: 15th September 2017

---

Q: What was the contention of Kumaon Stone Crusher regarding the transit fee?
A: Kumaon Stone Crusher contended that once the forest produce (boulders) is converted into commercial commodities like stone grits and chips, it ceases to be forest produce, and no transit fee can be charged.
Case: State of Uttarakhand & Ors. vs. Kumaon Stone Crusher
Date: 15th September 2017

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Aruna vs. The State of Maharashtra and Others?
A: The primary issue was the appellant, Aruna's, challenge against her disqualification as President of the Municipal Council, Kundalwadi, due to the Committee's refusal to verify her caste certificate under Rule 14 of the Maharashtra Scheduled Castes, Scheduled Tribes, De-notified Tribes (Vimukta Jatis), Nomadic Tribes, Other Backward Classes and Special Category (Regulation of Issuance and Verification of) Caste Certificate Rules, 2012.
Case: Aruna vs. The State of Maharashtra and Others
Date: 27th July 2021

---

Q: What was Aruna's argument regarding the verification of her caste certificate?
A: Aruna argued that her caste certificate was validly issued by the competent authority in Maharashtra under Rule 6(1)(a) and that the Committee erred in refusing to verify it based on the incorrect application of Rule 14, which deals with certificates issued by authorities in other states.
Case: Aruna vs. The State of Maharashtra and Others
Date: 27th July 2021

---

Q: What were the key findings of the Medical Council of India (MCI) regarding Dr. Gurmit Singh's conduct?
A: The MCI found that Dr. Gurmit Singh failed to exercise adequate medical competence, leading to a delay in diagnosing bowel perforation and subsequent deterioration of the patient.
Case: Harnek Singh & Ors. vs. Gurmit Singh & Ors.
Date: 18th May 2022

---

Q: What was the primary grievance of the appellants in this case?
A: The appellants' primary grievance was that their seniority was unfairly superseded by the respondents due to the inclusion of training periods in the seniority calculations for LDCs, which they argued was inconsistent with the statutory regulations.
Case: K. Anjaneyulu & Ors. vs. T. Ashok Raju & Ors.
Date: 24th July 2019

---

Q: What was the decision of the Single Judge regarding the integrated seniority list?
A: The Single Judge concluded that the integrated seniority list of LDCs and Typists must be determined in conformity with Regulation 26 and that the Memo dated 16.07.2002, which included the training period in the seniority calculation, was inconsistent with the legal position.
Case: K. Anjaneyulu & Ors. vs. T. Ashok Raju & Ors.
Date: 24th July 2019

---

Q: What is the case Commissioner of Central Excise, Haldia vs. M/s. Krishna Wax (P) Ltd. about?
A: The case involves the Commissioner of Central Excise, Haldia accusing M/s. Krishna Wax (P) Ltd. of manufacturing and clearing products like Foots Oil, Pressed Wax, and Pressed Paraffin Wax without paying excise duty, and the subsequent legal dispute over whether these activities constituted 'manufacture' under the Central Excise Act, 1944.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Haldia vs. M/s. Krishna Wax (P) Ltd.
Date: 14th November 2019

---

Q: What was the initial action taken by the Central Excise Commissionerate against M/s. Krishna Wax (P) Ltd.?
A: On 23rd September 2005, a search was conducted at the registered office and factory premises of M/s. Krishna Wax (P) Ltd. by the Central Excise officers, leading to a Show Cause Notice being issued on 21st March 2006, demanding unpaid excise duty and alleging that the company was manufacturing goods without following due procedure.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Haldia vs. M/s. Krishna Wax (P) Ltd.
Date: 14th November 2019

---

Q: Why did the appellants file an application for condonation of delay?
A: The appellants filed an application for condonation of delay because they missed the statutory deadline for filing an appeal against the NCLT's order dated 25th October 2019. They argued that the delay was due to the late receipt of the certified copy of the order and the lockdown imposed due to the COVID-19 pandemic.
Case: Sagufa Ahmed & Ors. vs. Upper Assam Plywood Products Pvt. Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 18th September 2020

---

Q: What was the NCLAT's reason for dismissing the application for condonation of delay?
A: The NCLAT dismissed the application for condonation of delay on the grounds that it had no power to condone delays beyond the statutory limit of 45 days as stipulated by Section 421(3) of the Companies Act, 2013.
Case: Sagufa Ahmed & Ors. vs. Upper Assam Plywood Products Pvt. Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 18th September 2020

---

Q: What is the case Snehadeep Structures Pvt. Limited vs. Maharashtra Small Scale Industries Development Corporation Ltd. about?
A: The case involves a dispute over the payment and interest on delayed payments for goods supplied by Snehadeep Structures Pvt. Limited (SSPL) to the Maharashtra Small Scale Industries Development Corporation Ltd. (MSSIDCL) under the Interest on Delayed Payments to Small Scale and Ancillary Industrial Undertakings Act, 1993.
Case: Snehadeep Structures Pvt. Limited vs. Maharashtra Small Scale Industries Development Corporation Ltd.

---

Q: What is the case Bharat Coking Coal Ltd. & Ors. vs. AMR-Dev Prabha & Ors. about?
A: The case involves a dispute over the validity of an auction process conducted by Bharat Coking Coal Ltd. (BCCL) for a tender, which was challenged by AMR-Dev Prabha on grounds of technical issues and alleged procedural irregularities during the auction process.
Case: Bharat Coking Coal Ltd. & Ors. vs. AMR-Dev Prabha & Ors.

---

Q: What was the primary issue that led to the dispute between BCCL and AMR-Dev Prabha?
A: The primary issue was the technical problems encountered during the e-auction process, which led to a re-auction. AMR-Dev Prabha argued that the re-auction was unjustified and contrary to the terms of the Notice Inviting Tender (NIT).
Case: Bharat Coking Coal Ltd. & Ors. vs. AMR-Dev Prabha & Ors.

---

Q: What was the original plot allotted to Mohini Dang and at what price?
A: Mohini Dang was originally allotted Plot No.54, Ambedkar Road, Ghaziabad, with an area of 175 sq. mtr. at a price of Rs. 2,000 per sq. mtr. as per the offer letter dated 14th October 1986.
Case: Mohini Dang vs. State of U.P. & Ors.

---

Q: What issue arose regarding the payment for the originally allotted plot?
A: Despite an attempt by the Ghaziabad Development Authority to return the money with interest on 24th September 2013, Mohini Dang had not actually received the money.
Case: Mohini Dang vs. State of U.P. & Ors.

---

Q: What instructions were given to the Family Courts in Jabalpur and Kota?
A: The Family Court in Jabalpur was directed to transmit the entire record to the Family Court in Kota immediately. The Family Court in Kota was instructed to explore the possibility of settlement between the parties through mediation.
Case: Monika Kumari vs. Abhishek Yadav

---

Q: What is the main issue in the case of Union of India & Ors. vs. Lt. Col. Kuldeep Yadav?
A: The main issue is whether the Armed Forces Tribunal was correct in interfering with the punishment of 'Severe Displeasure (Recordable)' awarded to Lt. Col. Kuldeep Yadav for maintaining unauthorized contact with a foreign national and violating security instructions.
Case: Union of India & Ors. vs. Lt. Col. Kuldeep Yadav

---

Q: What was the nature of the misconduct committed by Lt. Col. Kuldeep Yadav?
A: Lt. Col. Kuldeep Yadav maintained unauthorized contact with a foreign national, Ms. Sueli De Oliviera Montilha, through emails, phone calls, and personal meetings, and stayed with her at an Army premises in Goa, violating the 'Instructions on Contact with Foreign Nationals, 1987'.
Case: Union of India & Ors. vs. Lt. Col. Kuldeep Yadav

---

Q: What was the decision of the Armed Forces Tribunal regarding the punishment awarded to Lt. Col. Kuldeep Yadav?
A: The Armed Forces Tribunal decided that the punishment of 'Severe Displeasure (Recordable)' was excessive and directed the competent authority to reconsider the punishment, suggesting a less severe form of censure.
Case: Union of India & Ors. vs. Lt. Col. Kuldeep Yadav

---

Q: What is the main issue in the case of Patel Ahmed Mohammad vs. Balwant Singh Rajput & Ors.?
A: The main issue is the challenge to Patel Ahmed Mohammad's election to the Rajya Sabha on the grounds of alleged corrupt practices, including bribery and undue influence, as well as the improper rejection and acceptance of certain votes by the Returning Officer, which were claimed to have materially affected the election results.
Case: Patel Ahmed Mohammad vs. Balwant Singh Rajput & Ors.

---

Q: What is the main issue in the case of Rajiv Kumar Jindal and Others vs. BCI Staff Colony Residential Welfare Association and Others?
A: The main issue is the validity of the auction process conducted for the sale of assets of M/s Bharat Commerce & Industries Limited (BCI), which was declared a sick company. The auction process and the acceptance of the bid by Rajiv Kumar Jindal and others were challenged on grounds of procedural irregularities and non-compliance with the guidelines set by the Asset Sale Committee (ASC).
Case: Rajiv Kumar Jindal and Others vs. BCI Staff Colony Residential Welfare Association and Others

---

Q: What were the allegations against the auction process conducted by IDBI for the sale of BCI's assets?
A: The allegations included the absence of a competitive bidding process, failure to indicate the reserve price in the auction notice, and the appellants' non-compliance with the ASC guidelines, specifically the failure to furnish a bank guarantee and to pay the sale consideration within the stipulated time.
Case: Rajiv Kumar Jindal and Others vs. BCI Staff Colony Residential Welfare Association and Others

---

Q: What was the agreement to sell executed on 24th September 1986 about?
A: The agreement to sell dated 24th September 1986 involved the sale of three properties (House Nos. 258, 259, and 260) in Sadar Bazar, Allahabad, for a total consideration of ₹55,000, with ₹5,000 paid as earnest money. The sale was to be completed upon obtaining permission from the Ceiling Department.
Case: Hazari Lal (Dead) Thr. LRs. vs. Ramesh Kumar & Others

---

Q: What was the Court's observation regarding the funds collected under the Cess Act?
A: The Court observed that more than Rs. 37,400 crores had been collected under the Cess Act for the benefit of construction workers, but only about Rs. 9,500 crores had been utilized. The Court questioned the use of the remaining Rs. 28,000 crores.
Case: National Campaign Committee for Central Legislation on Construction Labour (NCC-CL) vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: What role did the Court assign to the Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG) in this case?
A: The Court directed the CAG to audit the State Welfare Boards to ensure that the funds collected under the Cess Act were being properly utilized for the benefit of construction workers.
Case: National Campaign Committee for Central Legislation on Construction Labour (NCC-CL) vs. Union of India & Ors.

---

Q: What would happen if Buddha Tri-Ratna Mission failed to deposit the required amount by the stipulated date?
A: If the amount was not deposited by 31st August 2022, the dues would cease to be frozen, and the Union of India could consider canceling the allotment of the plot.
Case: Union of India & Anr. vs. Buddha Tri-Ratna Mission & Anr.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of E. Sivakumar vs. Union of India & Ors.?
A: The main issue was the illegal manufacture and sale of Gutkha and Pan Masala containing tobacco and/or nicotine.
Case: E. Sivakumar vs. Union of India & Ors.
Date: 10th October 2017

---

Q: What led to the arbitration between Sri Chittaranjan Maity and the Union of India?
A: The arbitration was initiated due to various disputes and differences that arose during the execution of the work, including delays and hindrances allegedly caused by the railway administration, leading to the appellant abandoning the work and subsequently raising claims for payment.
Case: Sri Chittaranjan Maity vs. Union of India

---

Q: On what basis did Brahmputra Metallics Ltd. claim the rebate or deduction from electricity duty?
A: Brahmputra Metallics Ltd. based its claim on the Industrial Policy 2012, which provided an exemption from the payment of 50% of electricity duty for five years for captive power plants established for self-consumption or captive use, and the statutory notification issued under Section 9 of the Bihar Electricity Duty Act 1948.
Case: The State of Jharkhand vs. Brahmputra Metallics Ltd., Ranchi and Anr.

---

Q: What was the significance of the Industrial Policy 2012 in this case?
A: The Industrial Policy 2012 aimed to encourage industrialization by providing incentives, including a rebate on electricity duty for captive power plants. The policy stipulated that necessary follow-up notifications would be issued within one month to give effect to its provisions.
Case: The State of Jharkhand vs. Brahmputra Metallics Ltd., Ranchi and Anr.

---

Q: Who were the testators and what were the contents of their Wills?
A: The testators were Mannar Reddiar and Adhilakshmiammal. Adhilakshmiammal's Will, dated 30th January 1995, bequeathed her properties to her two sons, excluding her daughter Kalavathy. Mannar Reddiar's Will, dated 10th December 1998, also excluded Kalavathy and bequeathed his properties to his two sons and grandchildren.
Case: Swarnalatha & Ors. vs. Kalavathy & Ors.

---

Q: Why did the local residents oppose the Solid Waste Disposal Project in the case?
A: The local residents opposed the project due to concerns that it would cause severe water pollution, health hazards, and environmental damage, particularly due to the project's location on a rocky, sloping area near a river and the Sheel Dam, which provides water to Ratnagiri city.
Case: Ratnagiri Nagar Parishad vs. Gangaram Narayan Ambekar & Ors.

---

Q: What was the decision of the trial court in this case?
A: The trial court dismissed the suit filed by the residents, concluding that they had not provided sufficient evidence to support their claims of potential environmental and health hazards.
Case: Ratnagiri Nagar Parishad vs. Gangaram Narayan Ambekar & Ors.

---

Q: What directions were given to the Court at Thane, Maharashtra regarding the case records?
A: The Court at Thane, Maharashtra was directed to immediately transmit the entire case record to the Court of District Judge, Gaya, who in turn shall assign the matter to a Court of competent jurisdiction.
Case: Aprajita Chakraborty vs. Anirban Biswangri

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of State Bank of India and Anr vs. New India Assurance Company Limited?
A: The primary issue was whether the State Bank of India (SBI) was liable to compensate the borrower for an insurance claim that was denied by New India Assurance Company due to a delay in filing the claim.
Case: State Bank of India and Anr vs. New India Assurance Company Limited

---

Q: What argument did SBI present regarding the NCDRC's decision?
A: SBI argued that since the borrower accepted the SCDRC's order and did not appeal, there was no basis for the NCDRC to hold the Bank liable. Additionally, SBI cited a settlement with the borrower in which the borrower agreed to withdraw all claims.
Case: State Bank of India and Anr vs. New India Assurance Company Limited

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of M/S. Innoventive Industries Ltd. vs. ICICI Bank & Anr.?
A: The primary issue was whether the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016, would prevail over the Maharashtra Relief Undertakings (Special Provisions) Act, 1958, in initiating the insolvency resolution process against Innoventive Industries Ltd. due to their default on loans.
Case: M/S. Innoventive Industries Ltd. vs. ICICI Bank & Anr.

---

Q: What was Innoventive Industries Ltd.'s main defense against the insolvency application filed by ICICI Bank?
A: Innoventive Industries Ltd. claimed that their debt liabilities were temporarily suspended under two notifications issued under the Maharashtra Relief Undertakings Act, and hence, no debt was legally due at the time.
Case: M/S. Innoventive Industries Ltd. vs. ICICI Bank & Anr.

---

Q: What did the National Company Law Tribunal (NCLT) decide regarding the applicability of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016?
A: The NCLT held that the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016, would prevail over the Maharashtra Relief Undertakings Act, owing to the non-obstante clause in Section 238 of the Code, and admitted the application for initiating the insolvency resolution process against Innoventive Industries Ltd.
Case: M/S. Innoventive Industries Ltd. vs. ICICI Bank & Anr.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Tamil Nadu Pollution Control Board vs. Sterlite Industries (I) Ltd. & Ors.?
A: The main issue was the environmental compliance and operation of the copper smelter plant by Sterlite Industries in Tamil Nadu, including the legality of various orders issued by the Tamil Nadu Pollution Control Board (TNPCB) and the National Green Tribunal (NGT).
Case: Tamil Nadu Pollution Control Board vs. Sterlite Industries (I) Ltd. & Ors.

---

Q: What were the orders issued by the National Green Tribunal (NGT) that were challenged in this case?
A: The orders issued by the NGT on 31st May 2013, 8th August 2013, and 15th December 2018, which allowed Sterlite Industries to continue operations despite the TNPCB's closure directives, were challenged.
Case: Tamil Nadu Pollution Control Board vs. Sterlite Industries (I) Ltd. & Ors.

---

Q: What conditions were imposed on Sterlite Industries for continuing their operations?
A: Conditions included regular monitoring of air and water quality, proper disposal of hazardous waste, compliance with environmental regulations, and periodic inspections by TNPCB and other authorities.
Case: Tamil Nadu Pollution Control Board vs. Sterlite Industries (I) Ltd. & Ors.

---

Q: What was the finding of the Commissioner of Customs regarding the imported MFDs?
A: The Commissioner of Customs held that the imports were in violation of the Foreign Trade Policy as they were made without the necessary prior authorization and classified the MFDs as 'other wastes' under the Waste Management Rules, imposing a redemption fine and ordering the consignment for re-export.
Case: Commissioner of Customs vs. M/S. Atul Automations Pvt. Ltd. and Others

---

Q: What was the main legal issue in the case of I.C. Sharma vs. The Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd.?
A: The main legal issue was whether the insurance claim for the burglary that occurred in I.C. Sharma's house should be fully compensated despite the alleged under-insurance of the stolen items.
Case: I.C. Sharma vs. The Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd.

---

Q: What was the initial offer made by the Insurance Company to I.C. Sharma for the burglary claim?
A: The Insurance Company initially offered a sum of Rs. 3,500/- in November 2008, which I.C. Sharma refused to accept. Later, an amount of Rs. 29,920/- was offered, which was also not accepted by the appellant.
Case: I.C. Sharma vs. The Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Himachal Pradesh Bus Stand Management and Development Authority (HPBSM&DA) vs The Central Empowered Committee Etc. & Ors.?
A: The main issue was the legality of the construction of a Bus Stand Complex in McLeod Ganj, Himachal Pradesh, which included a Hotel-cum-Restaurant structure, without obtaining the necessary environmental clearances.
Case: Himachal Pradesh Bus Stand Management and Development Authority (HPBSM&DA) vs The Central Empowered Committee Etc. & Ors.

---

Q: What was the conclusion of the Central Empowered Committee (CEC) regarding the Bus Stand Complex in McLeod Ganj?
A: The CEC concluded that the construction of the Hotel-cum-Restaurant structure within the Bus Stand Complex was not permitted and violated the provisions of the Forest (Conservation) Act, 1980, and recommended its demolition.
Case: Himachal Pradesh Bus Stand Management and Development Authority (HPBSM&DA) vs The Central Empowered Committee Etc. & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Glocal Medical College and Super Speciality Hospital & Research Centre vs. Union of India and Another?
A: The main issue was the cancellation of conditional permission granted for the establishment of medical colleges and the debarment of these colleges from admitting students for the academic years 2017-18 and 2018-19.
Case: Glocal Medical College and Super Speciality Hospital & Research Centre vs. Union of India and Another

---

Q: What action did the Government of India take against the medical colleges involved in this case?
A: The Government of India cancelled the conditional permission for the establishment of the medical colleges, debarred them from admitting students for the next two academic years (2017-18 and 2018-19), and authorized the Medical Council of India (MCI) to encash the bank guarantees submitted by the colleges.
Case: Glocal Medical College and Super Speciality Hospital & Research Centre vs. Union of India and Another

---

Q: What deficiencies were pointed out by the Medical Council of India (MCI) in the inspection of the colleges?
A: The MCI pointed out several deficiencies in the colleges during its inspection on 21st and 22nd December 2016, leading to the recommendation that the colleges be debarred from admitting students and that their bank guarantees be encashed.
Case: Glocal Medical College and Super Speciality Hospital & Research Centre vs. Union of India and Another

---

Q: What is the background and lineage of the parties involved in the case?
A: The case involves a family lineage starting with Mangat Ram from Village Narnaul, Rajasthan, who had four sons: Madhav Prashad, Lal Chand, Ram Chand, and Umrao Lal. Madhav Prashad had no issues and adopted Hari Ram, son of Lal Chand. Ram Chand also had no issues and adopted Shriram, another son of Lal Chand. The plaintiff, Bhagwat Sharan, is the son of Radha Krishan, who was the son of Umrao Lal. The family branches in dispute are from the descendants of Madhav Prashad through Hari Ram and the descendants of Umrao Lal.
Case: Bhagwat Sharan (Dead Through LRS) vs. Purushottam & Ors.
Date: 3rd April 2020

---

Q: What was the primary claim of the plaintiff, Bhagwat Sharan, regarding the family properties?
A: The plaintiff, Bhagwat Sharan, claimed that the properties mentioned in the plaint were joint family properties owned by the Hindu Undivided Family (HUF) and sought partition of the same by metes and bounds as per his share. He asserted that these properties were purchased and managed jointly by Madhav Prashad and Umrao Lal, and later by their descendants.
Case: Bhagwat Sharan (Dead Through LRS) vs. Purushottam & Ors.
Date: 3rd April 2020

---

Q: What was the main defense of the respondents against the plaintiff's claim?
A: The respondents contested that the properties were not joint family properties but were self-acquired properties of Madhav Prashad and Hari Ram. They denied the existence of any Hindu Undivided Family (HUF) and asserted that the properties and business mentioned were individual acquisitions and not part of a joint family estate.
Case: Bhagwat Sharan (Dead Through LRS) vs. Purushottam & Ors.
Date: 3rd April 2020

---

Q: What was the trial court's decision regarding the properties in dispute?
A: The trial court decreed the suit in favor of the plaintiff, holding that all the properties were joint family properties and that the plaintiff had a 2.38% share in them. The court decided that the properties should be partitioned accordingly.
Case: Bhagwat Sharan (Dead Through LRS) vs. Purushottam & Ors.
Date: 3rd April 2020

---

Q: What was the background of the financial dispute between Surendra Trading Company and Juggilal Kamlapat Jute Mills Company Limited?
A: Surendra Trading Company, a jute trader, supplied raw jute to Juggilal Kamlapat Jute Mills Company Limited between 2001 and 2003, for which the corporate debtor owed Rs. 17,06,766.95. The debtor acknowledged this debt in a certificate dated 24th October 2004 but did not pay due to ongoing proceedings under the Sick Industrial Companies (Special Provisions) Act, 1985 (SICA). After the repeal of SICA and enactment of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016, the creditor sought resolution under the new Code.
Case: M/S. Surendra Trading Company vs. M/S. Juggilal Kamlapat Jute Mills Company Limited & Ors.
Date: 19th September 2017

---

Q: What was the primary reason for the insurance claim rejection by United India Insurance Co. Ltd.?
A: The insurance claim was rejected because the driver, Dharmendra Singh Chauhan, did not have a valid and effective driving licence at the time of the accident. The licence could not be verified by the transport department.
Case: Nirmala Kothari vs. United India Insurance Co. Ltd.
Date: 4th March 2020

---

Q: What were the findings of the Motor Accident Claims Tribunal regarding the driving licence of Singh Ram?
A: The Tribunal found that the driving licence produced by Singh Ram, issued by the Motor Vehicles Department, Agra, was fake. Additionally, another licence he sought to produce had expired before the accident and was not renewed within the prescribed period.
Case: Singh Ram vs. Nirmala and Ors.
Date: 6th March 2018

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case between the State of Tamil Nadu and K. Fazlur Rahman & Anr.?
A: The primary issue was the validity of the supersession of the Tamil Nadu Waqf Board by the State Government, particularly whether the nomination of two Senior Muslim Advocates as members of the Board affected the composition and functioning of the Board as per the Waqf Act, 1995.
Case: The State of Tamil Nadu & Anr. vs. K. Fazlur Rahman & Anr.
Date: 3rd November 2020

---

Q: What was the reason provided by the State Government for superseding the Tamil Nadu Waqf Board?
A: The State Government superseded the Tamil Nadu Waqf Board on the grounds that the number of elected members had become less than the number of nominated members, which, according to the government, rendered the Board unable to perform its functions effectively.
Case: The State of Tamil Nadu & Anr. vs. K. Fazlur Rahman & Anr.
Date: 3rd November 2020

---

Q: What were the suggestions made by the Amicus Curiae to curb the menace of capitation fees?
A: The Amicus Curiae suggested creating a web portal for reporting capitation fee demands, ensuring all rounds of counseling are completed before the last date for admissions, making the list of students recommended for stray vacancies public, and prohibiting private medical colleges from charging any fees in cash or additional fees not fixed by the Fee Fixation Committee.
Case: Rashtreeya Sikshana Samithi Trust vs. Committee For Fixation of Fee Structure Of Private Professional Colleges & Ors.
Date: 19th May 2022

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of M/S. Bhushan Steel Strips Ltd. vs. State of U.P. & Anr.?
A: The primary legal issue pertained to the disciplinary proceedings initiated by M/S. Bhushan Steel Strips Ltd. against the respondent party.
Case: M/S. Bhushan Steel Strips Ltd. vs. State of U.P. & Anr.
Date: 30th October 2017

---

Q: What was the outcome of the disciplinary proceedings issue during the pendency of the case?
A: During the pendency of the case, the parties entered into a proper settlement dated 12th October 2017, resolving the issue.
Case: M/S. Bhushan Steel Strips Ltd. vs. State of U.P. & Anr.
Date: 30th October 2017

---

Q: What was the impact of the settlement on the pending applications in the case?
A: As a result of the settlement, all pending applications in the case were disposed of.
Case: M/S. Bhushan Steel Strips Ltd. vs. State of U.P. & Anr.
Date: 30th October 2017

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Sampada Yogesh Waghdhare vs. The State of Maharashtra & Ors.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether Sampada Yogesh Waghdhare, who was disqualified as a Municipal Councillor under Section 44(1)(e) of the Maharashtra Municipal Council, Nagar Panchayat and Industrial Township Act, 1965, for unauthorized constructions carried out by her husband, could be held responsible for the same.
Case: Sampada Yogesh Waghdhare vs. The State of Maharashtra & Ors.
Date: 22nd April 2019

---

Q: What grounds did the Collector use to disqualify Sampada Yogesh Waghdhare under Section 44(1)(e) of the Maharashtra Municipal Council Act?
A: The Collector disqualified Sampada Yogesh Waghdhare on the grounds that her husband had carried out unauthorized constructions, and under Section 44(1)(e), a Councillor can be disqualified if their spouse or dependent has constructed any illegal or unauthorized structure.
Case: Sampada Yogesh Waghdhare vs. The State of Maharashtra & Ors.
Date: 22nd April 2019

---

Q: What was Sampada Yogesh Waghdhare's argument against her disqualification?
A: Sampada Yogesh Waghdhare argued that the unauthorized constructions were carried out by her husband, who had deemed permission, and that she should not be held responsible for the actions of her spouse under Section 44(1)(e) of the Act.
Case: Sampada Yogesh Waghdhare vs. The State of Maharashtra & Ors.
Date: 22nd April 2019

---

Q: What actions by Dr. Vijay Mallya led to the contempt charges?
A: Dr. Vijay Mallya transferred USD 40 million out of his account shortly after receiving it, despite an oral undertaking and subsequent court orders preventing such actions. Additionally, he failed to disclose the existence of the bank account and details of the transferred amount.
Case: Dr. Vijay Mallya vs. State Bank of India & Ors.
Date: 31st August 2020

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of SVG Fashions Pvt. Ltd. vs. Ritu Murli Manohar Goyal & Anr.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the application filed by SVG Fashions Pvt. Ltd. under Section 9 of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016, against M/S Arpita Filaments Pvt. Ltd. was barred by limitation, as determined by the National Company Law Appellate Tribunal (NCLAT).
Case: SVG Fashions Pvt. Ltd. vs. Ritu Murli Manohar Goyal & Anr.
Date: 29th March 2022

---

Q: What was the trial court's decision regarding the plaintiffs' relationship with Jagan Nath and the validity of the Will?
A: The trial court held that the plaintiffs were the granddaughters of Smt. Kirpo and that Kirpo was the real sister of Mangal, who was Jagan Nath's father. The court found the Will dated 17.09.1970 to be invalid and decreed the suit in favor of the plaintiffs.
Case: Jarnail Singh & Anr. vs. Bhagwanti (D) Thr. LRs. & Ors.
Date: 29th November 2018

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Avneesh Chandan Gadgil & Anr. vs. Oriental Bank of Commerce & Ors.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether Section 5 of the Limitation Act, 1963, applies to an appeal under Section 30 of the Recovery of Debts Due to Banks and Financial Institutions Act, 1993, concerning the condonation of delay in filing an appeal against an order passed by the Recovery Officer.
Case: Avneesh Chandan Gadgil & Anr. vs. Oriental Bank of Commerce & Ors.
Date: 24th November 2021

---

Q: What was the decision of the Debts Recovery Appellate Tribunal (DRAT) regarding the condonation of delay in this case?
A: The DRAT set aside the order of the Debts Recovery Tribunal (DRT) which had condoned the delay in filing the appeal under Section 30 of the Act, 1993, by applying Section 5 of the Limitation Act. The DRAT held that Section 5 of the Limitation Act was not applicable to such appeals.
Case: Avneesh Chandan Gadgil & Anr. vs. Oriental Bank of Commerce & Ors.
Date: 24th November 2021

---

Q: What was the decision of the arbitral tribunal regarding the claims of the respondent company?
A: The arbitral tribunal (2:1) allowed the claims of the respondent company. The third arbitrator disagreed with the majority and provided a separate dissenting opinion.
Case: Dakshin Haryana Bijli Vitran Nigam Ltd. vs. M/S Navigant Technologies Pvt. Ltd.
Date: 2nd March 2021

---

Q: What was the appellant's argument regarding the commencement of the limitation period for filing objections under Section 34?
A: The appellant argued that the limitation period should commence from the date the signed copy of the award was provided (19th May 2018), not from the date the draft award was circulated (27th April 2018).
Case: Dakshin Haryana Bijli Vitran Nigam Ltd. vs. M/S Navigant Technologies Pvt. Ltd.
Date: 2nd March 2021

---

Q: What were the three main issues that remained for resolution after the disputes were settled?
A: The three main issues remaining were: (i) the order regarding costs, (ii) the fate of the contempt proceedings, and (iii) the payment of arrears to Mohammad Mehraj-ud-Din Khan.
Case: State of Jammu and Kashmir vs. Mohammad Mehraj-ud-Din Khan & Ors.
Date: 21st November 2017

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Union of India vs. R. Sethumadhavan & Anr.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the pension of the respondent, who retired as a Train Examiner, should be calculated based on the replacement scale of Rs. 4500-7000 or the revised scale of Rs. 5000-8000 recommended for the re-designated post of Junior Engineer Grade-II.
Case: Union of India vs. R. Sethumadhavan & Anr.
Date: 22nd March 2018

---

Q: What was the respondent's contention regarding the calculation of his pension?
A: The respondent contended that his post of Train Examiner was re-designated as Junior Engineer Grade-II and that his pension should be based on the revised pay scale of Rs. 5000-8000, as recommended by the 5th Central Pay Commission.
Case: Union of India vs. R. Sethumadhavan & Anr.
Date: 22nd March 2018

---

Q: What was the decision of the Central Administrative Tribunal (CAT) regarding the respondent's pension?
A: The CAT held that the respondent's pension had been correctly fixed based on the replacement scale of Rs. 4500-7000 for the post of Train Examiner, as there was no evidence to suggest that the post was re-designated as Junior Engineer Grade-II.
Case: Union of India vs. R. Sethumadhavan & Anr.
Date: 22nd March 2018

---

Q: What was the main legal issue in the case of State of Gujarat vs. Cadila Healthcare Ltd.?
A: The main legal issue was whether the product 'KADIPROL' manufactured by Cadila Healthcare Ltd. should be classified as 'Poultry Feed' under Entry 25 of Schedule I of the Gujarat Sales Tax Act (GST Act) or as a 'Drug and Medicine' under Entry 26(1) of Schedule II Part A of the GST Act.
Case: State of Gujarat vs. Cadila Healthcare Ltd.
Date: 11th July 2022

---

Q: What was the decision of the Deputy Commissioner of Sales Tax regarding the classification of 'KADIPROL'?
A: The Deputy Commissioner of Sales Tax determined that 'KADIPROL' should be categorized as a 'Drug and Medicine' under Entry 26(1) of Schedule II Part A of the GST Act, as it contained preventive medicine.
Case: State of Gujarat vs. Cadila Healthcare Ltd.
Date: 11th July 2022

---

Q: What was the trial court's decision regarding the Will executed by Leela Devi?
A: The trial court disbelieved the Will put forth by Dhani Ram, decreed the suit in favor of Shiv Singh, set aside the mutation effected on the strength of the Will, and granted Shiv Singh possession of the suit properties.
Case: Dhani Ram (died) through LRs. & others vs. Shiv Singh
Date: 6th October 2023

---

Q: How did the appellate court rule on the appeal filed by Dhani Ram?
A: The appellate court reversed the trial court's judgment, held that the Will stood proved, and found no suspicious circumstances surrounding it. The suit filed by Shiv Singh was dismissed with costs.
Case: Dhani Ram (died) through LRs. & others vs. Shiv Singh
Date: 6th October 2023

---

Q: What was the main legal issue in the case of Principal Commissioner of Income Tax, Central-3 vs. Abhisar Buildwell P. Ltd.?
A: The main legal issue was whether the waiver of limitation as stipulated in Section 150(2) of the Income Tax Act should be read concerning the date of the issue of notice for reassessment under Section 148, especially in the context of assessments under Section 153A or 153C.
Case: Principal Commissioner of Income Tax, Central-3 vs. Abhisar Buildwell P. Ltd.
Date: 12th May 2023

---

Q: What prayers were sought by the Revenue in their miscellaneous application?
A: The Revenue sought clarification that the waiver of limitation in Section 150(2) applies to the date of issue of notice for reassessment under Section 148. They also sought that the findings in paras 11 and 14 apply to all pending proceedings and that fresh proceedings for reassessment could be initiated based on Section 147/148 read with Section 150.
Case: Principal Commissioner of Income Tax, Central-3 vs. Abhisar Buildwell P. Ltd.
Date: 12th May 2023

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Ayurved Vikas Mandal vs. State of Gujarat & Ors.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the State of Gujarat should absorb the 23 teachers who were working in an aided college that was converted into a self-financing institution and whether the institution should be responsible for their salaries and arrears.
Case: Ayurved Vikas Mandal vs. State of Gujarat & Ors.
Date: 12th September 2017

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Gaurav Hargovindbhai Dave vs. Asset Reconstruction Company (India) Ltd. & Anr.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the application filed under Section 7 of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016 (IBC) by the Asset Reconstruction Company (India) Ltd. was within the period of limitation, given that the right to sue accrued on 21st July 2011 when the account was declared NPA.
Case: Gaurav Hargovindbhai Dave vs. Asset Reconstruction Company (India) Ltd. & Anr.
Date: 18th September 2019

---

Q: How did the National Company Law Appellate Tribunal (NCLAT) rule on the appeal against the NCLT's decision?
A: The NCLAT upheld the NCLT's decision, following its earlier judgments, and held that the limitation period for the purposes of the IBC would begin running only on and from 1st December 2016, the date when the IBC came into force.
Case: Gaurav Hargovindbhai Dave vs. Asset Reconstruction Company (India) Ltd. & Anr.
Date: 18th September 2019

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Gyan Prakash Arya vs. M/s Titan Industries Limited?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the order passed by the arbitrator under Section 33 of the Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996, modifying the original award was beyond the scope and ambit of the arbitrator's jurisdiction.
Case: Gyan Prakash Arya vs. M/s Titan Industries Limited
Date: 22nd November 2021

---

Q: What was the original award passed by the arbitrator on 4th December 2010?
A: The original award directed Gyan Prakash Arya to return 3648.80 grams of pure gold along with interest at 18% per annum, calculated at Rs. 740 per gram from 24th July 2004 until the date of delivery. Alternatively, Arya was to pay the monetary value of the gold at Rs. 740 per gram with the same interest rate.
Case: Gyan Prakash Arya vs. M/s Titan Industries Limited
Date: 22nd November 2021

---

Q: What modification did the arbitrator make to the original award under Section 33 of the Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996?
A: The arbitrator modified the original award by substituting the value of gold from Rs. 740 per gram to Rs. 20,747 per 10 grams and made corresponding changes to the interest calculations.
Case: Gyan Prakash Arya vs. M/s Titan Industries Limited
Date: 22nd November 2021

---

Q: What was the argument made by Gyan Prakash Arya regarding the modification of the original award?
A: Gyan Prakash Arya argued that the modification went beyond correcting any arithmetical or clerical error, which is the only permissible scope under Section 33 of the Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996. He contended that the modification introduced a new claim not part of the original award.
Case: Gyan Prakash Arya vs. M/s Titan Industries Limited
Date: 22nd November 2021

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Dr. Ashok Sinha vs. The State of Tripura & Ors.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the State government should administer the Tripura Medical College as a government institution or through an independent society, and whether the fees charged and admission procedures should be aligned with other government medical colleges in the North East.
Case: Dr. Ashok Sinha vs. The State of Tripura & Ors.
Date: 19th July 2019

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Abdul Ahad and Ors. vs. Union of India and Ors.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the students who were admitted to the Glocal Medical College through private counseling, contrary to the centralized counseling mandated by the State of Uttar Pradesh, should be allowed to continue their studies despite their admission being declared illegal by the Medical Council of India (MCI).
Case: Abdul Ahad and Ors. vs. Union of India and Ors.
Date: 17th August 2021

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Rachit Sinha & Ors. vs. Union of India & Ors.?
A: The primary legal issue was the allocation and counseling process for unfilled All India Quota seats in medical colleges, specifically whether these unfilled seats should be reverted to the States or also include deemed/Central institutions.
Case: Rachit Sinha & Ors. vs. Union of India & Ors.
Date: 11th May 2018

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Dr. S.K. Jhunjhunwala vs. Mrs. Dhanwanti Kaur & Anr.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether Dr. S.K. Jhunjhunwala was negligent in performing the surgery on Mrs. Dhanwanti Kaur, resulting in complications and subsequent suffering for the patient.
Case: Dr. S.K. Jhunjhunwala vs. Mrs. Dhanwanti Kaur & Anr.
Date: 1st October 2018

---

Q: What were the allegations made by Mrs. Dhanwanti Kaur against Dr. S.K. Jhunjhunwala?
A: Mrs. Dhanwanti Kaur alleged that Dr. S.K. Jhunjhunwala performed a general surgery without her consent, failed to perform the surgery properly, resulting in post-operative complications including dysentery, loss of appetite, and jaundice, and necessitated another surgery to remove remaining stones.
Case: Dr. S.K. Jhunjhunwala vs. Mrs. Dhanwanti Kaur & Anr.
Date: 1st October 2018

---

Q: What arguments did the appellants present regarding the calculation of notional income of the deceased?
A: The appellants argued that the notional income should be higher, based on affidavits stating that the deceased's classmates earned around Rs. 40,000/- per month. They contended that S. Sathiyanarayan, being an engineering graduate pursuing an MBA, would have secured a well-paying job.
Case: S. Vasanthi & Anr. vs. M/S Adhiparasakthi Engg. College and Another
Date: 11th October 2022

---

Q: What was the primary issue addressed in the case of Rachit Sinha & Ors. vs. Union of India & Ors.?
A: The primary issue was the procedure for the second round of counselling for All India Quota seats and the reversion of unfilled seats to the States.
Case: Rachit Sinha & Ors. vs. Union of India & Ors.
Date: 11th May 2018

---

Q: What was the reason provided by the State of Madhya Pradesh for not completing the second round of counselling by the deadline?
A: The State of Madhya Pradesh cited their best efforts but stated that they were unable to complete the counselling by the deadline of 10th May 2018, mentioning that there were 415 unfilled seats.
Case: Rachit Sinha & Ors. vs. Union of India & Ors.
Date: 11th May 2018

---

Q: What was the initial medical condition of Mrs. Dhanwanti Kaur that led her to consult Dr. S.K. Jhunjhunwala?
A: Mrs. Dhanwanti Kaur experienced abdominal pain in June 1996 and, after consulting a local doctor and Dr. Lakshmi Basu, was diagnosed with two calculi in her gall bladder, requiring laparoscopic surgery.
Case: Dr. S.K. Jhunjhunwala vs. Mrs. Dhanwanti Kaur & Anr.
Date: 1st October 2018

---

Q: What arguments did the appellants present regarding the calculation of notional income of the deceased?
A: The appellants argued that the notional income should be higher, based on affidavits stating that the deceased's classmates earned around Rs. 40,000/- per month. They contended that S. Sathiyanarayan, being an engineering graduate pursuing an MBA, would have secured a well-paying job.
Case: S. Vasanthi & Anr. vs. M/S Adhiparasakthi Engg. College and Another
Date: 11th October 2022

---

Q: What deficiencies did the MCI inspection report note regarding Subharti Medical College?
A: The MCI inspection report noted several deficiencies, including 55.27% bed occupancy, a shortage of residents by 5.81%, inflated data of clinical material and casualty attendance, and issues with nursing stations and ward visibility.
Case: Subharti Medical College vs. Union of India & Ors.
Date: 8th September 2017

---

Q: What action did the Competent Authority of the Central Government take based on the MCI's recommendation?
A: The Competent Authority debarred Subharti Medical College from admitting students for two academic years (2017-18 and 2018-19) and authorized the MCI to encash the bank guarantee of Rs. 2 crore.
Case: Subharti Medical College vs. Union of India & Ors.
Date: 8th September 2017

---

Q: What were the reasons given by the authorities for rejecting Asfaq's parole application?
A: The authorities rejected Asfaq's parole application on the grounds that releasing him on parole could lead to untoward incidents in society, affect young generations adversely, pose a threat to those who had deposed against him, and potentially endanger Asfaq's own life due to public anger and annoyance.
Case: Asfaq vs State of Rajasthan and Others
Date: 11th September 2017

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Ponnaiyah Ramajayam Institute of Medical Sciences vs. Union of India & Anr.?
A: The primary issue was the denial of permission for the establishment of a new medical college with an annual intake of 150 MBBS students for the academic year 2016-17 and the subsequent debarment from admitting students for the next two academic years due to deficiencies in infrastructure and clinical materials.
Case: Ponnaiyah Ramajayam Institute of Medical Sciences vs. Union of India & Anr.
Date: 22nd September 2017

---

Q: What were the deficiencies noted by the Medical Council of India (MCI) during the inspections?
A: The deficiencies noted included a shortage of faculty (22.72% to 46.15%), shortage of residents (26.09% to 45.65%), low OPD attendance, low bed occupancy, and inadequate clinical materials and infrastructure.
Case: Ponnaiyah Ramajayam Institute of Medical Sciences vs. Union of India & Anr.
Date: 22nd September 2017

---

Q: What was the response of the Central Government to the findings of the MCI?
A: The Central Government initially debarred the college from admitting students for the academic years 2017-18 and 2018-19 and authorized the encashment of the bank guarantee of Rs. 2 crores.
Case: Ponnaiyah Ramajayam Institute of Medical Sciences vs. Union of India & Anr.
Date: 22nd September 2017

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of State of Gujarat vs. Reliance Industries Limited?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the tax credit available under the Gujarat Value Added Tax Act, 2003 (VAT Act) should be reduced at the rate of 4% twice for the same goods, under both sub-clauses (ii) and (iii) of Section 11(3)(b).
Case: State of Gujarat vs. Reliance Industries Limited
Date: 22nd September 2017

---

Q: What was the contention of the State of Gujarat regarding the reduction of tax credit?
A: The State of Gujarat contended that the tax credit should be reduced by 4% twice, once under sub-clause (ii) for goods dispatched outside the State as branch transfer and again under sub-clause (iii) for fuels used in the manufacture of goods.
Case: State of Gujarat vs. Reliance Industries Limited
Date: 22nd September 2017

---

Q: What was the decision of the Gujarat Value Added Tax Tribunal on the issue of tax credit reduction?
A: The Gujarat Value Added Tax Tribunal decided that the reduction of tax credit should only be at 4% and not twice, rejecting the view that it should be reduced again for the same goods under sub-clause (iii).
Case: State of Gujarat vs. Reliance Industries Limited
Date: 22nd September 2017

---

Q: Who is the appellant and what is their business in the case Western Coalfields Ltd. vs. Commissioner of Central Excise, Trichy/Madurai?
A: The appellant is Western Coalfields Ltd., a Public Sector Undertaking engaged in coal mining.
Case: Western Coalfields Ltd. vs. Commissioner of Central Excise, Trichy/Madurai
Date: 20th February 2019

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case Morgan Securities And Credits Pvt. Ltd. vs. Videocon Industries Ltd.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the arbitrator has the discretion to grant post-award interest only on the principal sum due under Section 31(7)(b) of the Arbitration and Conciliation Act.
Case: Morgan Securities And Credits Pvt. Ltd. vs. Videocon Industries Ltd.
Date: 1st September 2022

---

Q: What was the agreement between Morgan Securities And Credits Pvt. Ltd. and Videocon Industries Ltd. regarding bill discounting facilities?
A: On 27th January 2003, Morgan Securities And Credits Pvt. Ltd. and Videocon Industries Ltd. entered into an agreement under which Videocon availed of bill discounting facilities, and Morgan Securities disbursed Rs. 5,00,32,656. The dues remained unpaid.
Case: Morgan Securities And Credits Pvt. Ltd. vs. Videocon Industries Ltd.
Date: 1st September 2022

---

Q: What was the outcome of the arbitral award in favor of Morgan Securities And Credits Pvt. Ltd. on 1st March 2013?
A: The sole arbitrator rendered an arbitral award in favor of Morgan Securities And Credits Pvt. Ltd., decreeing the claim of Rs. 5,00,32,656 with interest at various rates: 21% per annum from the date of default to the date of demand notice, 36% per annum with monthly rests from the date of demand notice to the date of award (pre-award interest), and 18% per annum on the principal amount from the date of award to the date of payment (post-award interest).
Case: Morgan Securities And Credits Pvt. Ltd. vs. Videocon Industries Ltd.
Date: 1st September 2022

---

Q: What was the appellant's contention regarding the post-award interest in the case Morgan Securities And Credits Pvt. Ltd. vs. Videocon Industries Ltd.?
A: The appellant contended that the post-award interest of eighteen percent per annum should be granted on the total sum awarded, inclusive of both principal and pre-award interest, rather than just on the principal amount.
Case: Morgan Securities And Credits Pvt. Ltd. vs. Videocon Industries Ltd.
Date: 1st September 2022

---

Q: What was the counter-claim filed by the State of Jharkhand in the arbitration proceedings?
A: The State of Jharkhand filed a counter-claim for Rs. 6,00,78,736, seeking reimbursement on account of unsatisfactory performance by the respondents.
Case: The State of Jharkhand & Ors. vs. M/s HSS Integrated SDN & Anr.

Q: What were the agreements between Bharti Airtel Limited and Aircel Limited regarding spectrum trading?
A: In April 2016, Bharti Airtel Limited and Bharti Hexacom Limited entered into eight spectrum trading agreements with Aircel Limited and Dishnet Wireless Limited for the purchase of the right to use spectrum in the 2300 MHz band, contingent on approval from the Department of Telecommunications (DoT).

Q: What was the decision of the Adjudicating Authority regarding Airtel's right to set-off?

Q: What was the decision of the trial court regarding the application under Order 1 Rule 10 CPC in the case Sudhamayee Pattnaik and Others vs. Bibhu Prasad Sahoo and Others?

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case Swami Samarth Sugars and Agro Industries Ltd. vs. Loknete Marutrao Ghule Patil Dnyaneshwar Sahakari Sakhar Karkhana Ltd. & Ors.?

Q: Why did Loknete Marutrao Ghule Patil Dnyaneshwar Sahakari Sakhar Karkhana Ltd. seek the cancellation of the IEM granted to Swami Samarth Sugars and Agro Industries Ltd.?
A: They sought the cancellation of the IEM on the grounds that Swami Samarth Sugars and Agro Industries Ltd. failed to set up the sugar factory and commence production within the stipulated time frame and that the proposed sugar factory did not meet the minimum distance requirements set by the State of Maharashtra.

Q: What was the main legal issue addressed in the case of M/S. Vijay Industries vs. Commissioner of Income Tax?
A: The main legal issue addressed was the interpretation of Section 80HH of the Income Tax Act, 1961, particularly whether the deduction is to be computed from 'income' as calculated under the Act or from 'profits and gains' without deducting 'depreciation' and 'investment allowance'.
Case: M/S. VIJAY INDUSTRIES vs. COMMISSIONER OF INCOME TAX
Date: 1st March 2019

---

Q: What were the assessment years involved in the case?
A: The assessment years involved were 1979-80 and 1980-81.
Case: M/S. VIJAY INDUSTRIES vs. COMMISSIONER OF INCOME TAX
Date: 1st March 2019

---

Q: What was the position of the Income Tax Department regarding the deduction under Section 80HH?
A: The Income Tax Department's position was that the 20% deduction should be computed after taking into account depreciation, unabsorbed depreciation, and investment allowance.
Case: M/S. VIJAY INDUSTRIES vs. COMMISSIONER OF INCOME TAX
Date: 1st March 2019

---

Q: What did the arbitrator award to Manraj Enterprises?
A: The arbitrator awarded Manraj Enterprises Rs. 78,81,553.08, along with pendente lite and future interest at the rates of 12% and 18% respectively on the entire awarded amount, except for the earnest money deposit and security deposit.
Case: Union of India vs. Manraj Enterprises
Date: 18th November 2021

---

Q: What was the nature of the e-public tender floated by Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited (HPCL) on 4th December 2017?

Case: Caretel Infotech Ltd. vs. Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main contention of Caretel Infotech Ltd. regarding the blacklisting clause in the tender?
A: Caretel Infotech Ltd. contended that the blacklisting clause did not apply to them as they had not been blacklisted at the time of submitting their bid on 19th December 2017. They argued that the blacklisting occurred later, on 22nd February 2018.
Case: Caretel Infotech Ltd. vs. Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited & Ors.
Date: 9th April 2019

---

Q: What objections did M/s Atwal Rice & General Mills raise against the execution of the arbitration award?
A: M/s Atwal Rice & General Mills raised objections regarding the vagueness of the award, incorrect calculation of interest, lack of competence of the arbitrator to award interest, and other procedural issues. They also claimed that the decreetal amount had been fully satisfied.
Case: Punjab State Civil Supplies Corporation Ltd. & Anr. vs. M/s Atwal Rice & General Mills
Date: 11th July 2017

---

Q: What is the main issue raised by the Federation Haj PTOs of India in this case?
A: The main issue raised is the eligibility conditions and other provisions contained in the Haj Policy 2019-2023 formulated by the Government of India.
Case: Federation Haj PTOs of India vs. Union of India
Date: 4th February 2019

---

Q: How did the Government of India formulate the Haj Policy 2019-2023?
A: The Haj Policy 2019-2023 was formulated based on data collected and compiled in a study by IIT Delhi, after inviting and considering suggestions and comments from stakeholders including PTOs/HGOs.
Case: Federation Haj PTOs of India vs. Union of India
Date: 4th February 2019

---

Q: What were some of the changes made in the final Haj Policy 2019-2023 compared to the draft policy?
A: Changes included renaming HGOs to Haj Group Organisers, reducing security deposit amounts, modifying the distribution formula for seat allocation, and introducing a mid-term review of the policy.
Case: Federation Haj PTOs of India vs. Union of India
Date: 4th February 2019

---

Q: What were the categories under which the appellants were originally selected?
A: Five of the appellants were selected under the General Category and one was selected under the Socially and Economically Backward Category (SEBC).
Case: Rabari Prabhatbhai Gugubhai & Ors. vs. State of Gujarat & Ors.
Date: 19th April 2018

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of M/S Godrej Sara Lee Ltd. vs. The Excise and Taxation Officer-cum-Assessing Authority & Ors.?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the Revisional Authority had the jurisdiction to reopen and revise the assessment orders passed by the Assessing Authority, classifying mosquito repellents as taxable at 4% instead of 10%.
Case: M/S Godrej Sara Lee Ltd. vs. The Excise and Taxation Officer-cum-Assessing Authority & Ors.
Date: 1st February 2023

---

Q: What were the grounds for the appellant's challenge against the Revisional Authority's orders?
A: The appellant challenged the Revisional Authority's orders on the grounds that the assessment orders were legally correct, as they were based on the decision of the Haryana Tax Tribunal, which classified mosquito repellents containing 4% Alethrin as insecticides taxable at 4%.
Case: M/S Godrej Sara Lee Ltd. vs. The Excise and Taxation Officer-cum-Assessing Authority & Ors.
Date: 1st February 2023

---

Q: What did the Haryana Tax Tribunal's decision on 21st November 2001 state regarding mosquito repellents?
A: The Haryana Tax Tribunal's decision on 21st November 2001 held that mosquito repellents containing 4% Alethrin were classified as insecticides and were therefore subject to a concessional tax rate of 4%.
Case: M/S Godrej Sara Lee Ltd. vs. The Excise and Taxation Officer-cum-Assessing Authority & Ors.
Date: 1st February 2023

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of M.P. Medical Officers Association vs. The State of Madhya Pradesh & Ors.?
A: The main issue was the challenge to the withdrawal of a circular dated 23rd May 2009, which granted higher pay scales to Medical Officers, Dental Surgeons, and officers in the specialist cadre, and the recovery of excess amounts paid under this circular.
Case: M.P. Medical Officers Association vs. The State of Madhya Pradesh & Ors.
Date: 26th August 2022

---

Q: What was the reason for the withdrawal of the circular dated 23rd May 2009?
A: The circular was withdrawn on the grounds that it was issued without the approval of the Finance Department and had financial implications that were not sanctioned by the appropriate authority.
Case: M.P. Medical Officers Association vs. The State of Madhya Pradesh & Ors.
Date: 26th August 2022

Q: What was the primary dispute in the case of Tata Power Company Ltd. vs. Adani Electricity Mumbai Ltd. & Ors.?
A: The primary dispute was regarding the payment of standby charges by Adani Electricity Mumbai Ltd. (formerly Reliance Energy Limited) to Tata Power Company Ltd. for the electricity supplied in the suburbs of Mumbai.
Case: Tata Power Company Ltd. vs. Adani Electricity Mumbai Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 1st October 2020

---

Q: Why did Tata Power Company Ltd. seek to increase its generating capacity in 1985?
A: Tata Power Company Ltd. sought to increase its generating capacity to reduce its offtake of electricity from Maharashtra State Electricity Board (MSEB) to zero, which would cause a loss of revenue for MSEB.
Case: Tata Power Company Ltd. vs. Adani Electricity Mumbai Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 1st October 2020

---

Q: What arrangement was made between Tata Power Company Ltd. and MSEB to compensate for the loss of revenue?
A: An arrangement was made where Tata Power Company Ltd. would pay standby charges to MSEB initially for 300 MVA, increasing by 50 MVA each year, up to 550 MVA by 1990. These charges were meant to enable Tata Power Company Ltd. to draw upon MSEB's energy in case of outages or failures in Tata Power's generation capacity.
Case: Tata Power Company Ltd. vs. Adani Electricity Mumbai Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 1st October 2020

---

Q: How did the government of Maharashtra intervene in the dispute over standby charges between Tata Power Company Ltd. and Adani Electricity Mumbai Ltd.?
A: The government of Maharashtra appointed a committee to resolve the issue, which recommended that Adani Electricity Mumbai Ltd. should pay Rs. 3.5 crores per month to Tata Power Company Ltd. for standby supply for the period 1998-1999, based on various factors such as generation capacity, electricity supply, and financial positions.
Case: Tata Power Company Ltd. vs. Adani Electricity Mumbai Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 1st October 2020

---

Q: What was the central issue in the case of Nedumpilli Finance Company Limited vs. State of Kerala & Ors.?
A: The central issue was whether Non-Banking Financial Companies (NBFCs) regulated by the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) could also be regulated by State enactments such as the Kerala Money Lenders Act, 1958, and the Gujarat Money Lenders Act, 2011.
Case: Nedumpilli Finance Company Limited vs. State of Kerala & Ors.
Date: 10th April 2023

---

Q: What was the argument made by NBFCs regarding their regulation under the RBI Act?
A: NBFCs argued that since they were regulated by the RBI under Chapter III-B of the RBI Act, they should be exempt from state money lending laws, which was contested by the state authorities.
Case: Nedumpilli Finance Company Limited vs. State of Kerala & Ors.
Date: 10th April 2023

---

Q: What procedural requirements do NBFCs need to fulfill under the Kerala Money Lenders Act?
A: Under the Kerala Money Lenders Act, NBFCs are required to obtain a license to carry on the business of money lending, maintain proper books of accounts, and adhere to the prescribed interest rates, among other regulatory compliances.
Case: Nedumpilli Finance Company Limited vs. State of Kerala & Ors.
Date: 10th April 2023

---

Q: What was the argument made by GPSK Capital Private Limited regarding the fee exemption?
A: GPSK Capital Private Limited argued that since Srikant Mantri had already paid the fees as an individual member, the company, after converting the individual membership to a corporate entity, should be exempt from paying the fees again.
Case: GPSK Capital Private Limited vs. The Securities and Exchange Board of India
Date: 20th March 2023

---

Q: What was the Securities and Exchange Board of India's (SEBI) position on the fee exemption claim?
A: SEBI held that the company did not satisfy the conditions of Para 4 of Schedule III of the Regulations because Srikant Mantri was not a whole-time director during the relevant period, and hence the company was not eligible for fee exemption.
Case: GPSK Capital Private Limited vs. The Securities and Exchange Board of India
Date: 20th March 2023

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Surender Kumar Gupta & Ors. vs. J.M. Housing Limited & Ors.?

---

Q: Why did the respondents approach the National Company Law Appellate Tribunal (NCLAT) instead of the NCLT to vacate the ex-parte order?
A: The respondents approached the NCLAT instead of moving the NCLT for vacating or modifying the ad-interim order passed by the NCLT.
Case: Surender Kumar Gupta & Ors. vs. J.M. Housing Limited & Ors.

---

Q: What was the NCLAT's decision regarding the ex-parte order passed by the NCLT?

---

Q: What was the argument presented by the Commissioner of Central Excise regarding the charges realized by the Assessees?

Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.
Date: 11th May 2018

---

Q: How did the court address the issue of delivery and collection charges?
A: The court addressed that delivery and collection charges, being related to the post-manufacturing stage, should still be included in the assessable value if they are necessary to place the goods in the hands of the customer.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

Q: What was the significance of the non-obstante clause in the KTCP Act mentioned in the judgment?
A: The non-obstante clause in Section 76-M of the KTCP Act declares that the provisions of the Act and the rules, regulations, and bye-laws made thereunder shall have effect notwithstanding anything inconsistent contained in any other law, thus supporting BIAAPA's authority to levy charges.

---

Q: What is the main issue in the case of Indian Oil Corporation Limited vs State of Bihar?
A: The main issue is the demand for payment of Entry Tax by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited under the Bihar Tax on Entry of Goods into Local Areas for Consumption, Use, or Sale Therein Act, 1993, and the subsequent disallowance of VAT set-offs.
Case: Indian Oil Corporation Limited vs State of Bihar & Anr.
Date: 14th November 2017

---

Q: What were the Appellant's main arguments against the demand for Entry Tax?
A: The Appellant argued that VAT should be set off against Entry Tax as previously allowed until 2014, and that retrospective reopening of assessments from 2008-09 based on audit objections was unjustified.
Case: Indian Oil Corporation Limited vs State of Bihar & Anr.
Date: 14th November 2017

---

Q: What procedural steps did the Indian Oil Corporation Limited take after the audit objections were raised?
A: The Indian Oil Corporation Limited responded to the audit objections with letters requesting time to make detailed objections and provided certificates from BPCL and HPCL to show sales outside Patna, but the Assistant Commissioner of Commercial Taxes passed assessment orders and issued demand notices.
Case: Indian Oil Corporation Limited vs State of Bihar & Anr.
Date: 14th November 2017

---

Q: What were the implications of the audit objections raised in 2014 on the Appellant's tax liabilities?
A: The audit objections led to the reopening of assessments from 2008-09 and disallowance of previously allowed set-offs, resulting in an Entry Tax demand of Rs. 1,683.03 crores for the assessment years 2008-09 to 2014-15.
Case: Indian Oil Corporation Limited vs State of Bihar & Anr.
Date: 14th November 2017

---

Q: What are the broader implications of this judgment for other oil marketing companies?
A: The broader implications include potential reassessment of tax liabilities for other oil marketing companies operating under similar circumstances, ensuring compliance with the clarified interpretation of the Entry Tax Act and VAT set-offs.
Case: Indian Oil Corporation Limited vs State of Bihar & Anr.
Date: 14th November 2017

---

Q: How does this judgment impact the enforcement of the second proviso to Section 3(2) of the Entry Tax Act?
A: The judgment reinforces that the second proviso to Section 3(2) of the Entry Tax Act is assessee-based and not goods-based, meaning the set-off is specific to the person liable to pay VAT and Entry Tax, not the goods being taxed.
Case: Indian Oil Corporation Limited vs State of Bihar & Anr.
Date: 14th November 2017

---

Q: What is the main issue in the case of The Municipal Corporation, Faridabad vs Modern School, Faridabad?
A: The main issue is whether the Municipal Corporation can levy user charges on the schools for discharging waste water into the municipal sewer system.
Case: The Municipal Corporation, Faridabad vs Modern School, Faridabad & Ors.
Date: 8th February 2019

---

Q: Why did the Municipal Corporation issue an office order on 14th June 1999?
A: The office order was issued to regularize tubewells and prevent unauthorized discharges of waste water into the municipal sewer system, and to impose charges based on the diameter of the tubewells.
Case: The Municipal Corporation, Faridabad vs Modern School, Faridabad & Ors.
Date: 8th February 2019

---

Q: What was the response of Modern School, Faridabad to the notice about the unauthorized tubewell?
A: Modern School responded that the tubewell was installed to meet the school's water demand due to insufficient municipal water supply and that it was registered with the Central Ground Water Board.
Case: The Municipal Corporation, Faridabad vs Modern School, Faridabad & Ors.
Date: 8th February 2019

---

Q: What are the implications of Section 205 of the Haryana Municipal Corporation Act, 1994, in this case?
A: Section 205 prohibits making any connection with municipal drains without permission, implying that the schools should have obtained approval for discharging waste water into the municipal sewer system.
Case: The Municipal Corporation, Faridabad vs Modern School, Faridabad & Ors.
Date: 8th February 2019
