---

Q: What are the two central issues addressed in the case of Laxmi Pat Surana v. Union Bank of India & Anr.?
A: The two central issues are: (i) Whether an action under Section 7 of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016 can be initiated by the financial creditor against a corporate person concerning a guarantee offered for a loan account of the principal borrower, who is not a 'corporate person' within the meaning of the Code, and (ii) Whether an application under Section 7 of the Code filed after three years from the date of declaration of the loan account as Non-performing Asset (NPA) is barred by limitation.
Case: Laxmi Pat Surana v. Union Bank of India & Anr.

---

Q: What were the loan amounts extended to M/s. Mahaveer Construction by the Union Bank of India?
A: The Union Bank of India extended a term loan of Rs.9,60,00,000 (Rupees nine crore sixty lakhs) and an additional amount of Rs.2,45,00,000 (Rupees two crore forty-five lakhs) to M/s. Mahaveer Construction in 2007 and 2008, respectively.
Case: Laxmi Pat Surana v. Union Bank of India & Anr.

---

Q: When were the loan accounts of M/s. Mahaveer Construction declared as Non-performing Assets (NPAs)?
A: The loan accounts of M/s. Mahaveer Construction were declared as Non-performing Assets (NPAs) on 30th January 2010.
Case: Laxmi Pat Surana v. Union Bank of India & Anr.

---

Q: What action did the Union Bank of India take after declaring the loan accounts as NPAs?
A: After declaring the loan accounts as NPAs, the Union Bank of India issued a recall notice on 19th February 2010 to the principal borrower, M/s. Mahaveer Construction, and the corporate debtor, M/s. Surana Metals Limited, demanding repayment of the outstanding amount of Rs.12,35,11,548 (Rupees twelve crore thirty-five lakhs eleven thousand five hundred forty-eight).
Case: Laxmi Pat Surana v. Union Bank of India & Anr.

---

Q: What was the response of the corporate debtor to the recall notice issued by the Union Bank of India?
A: The corporate debtor, M/s. Surana Metals Limited, replied to the recall notice on 8th December 2018, stating that it was not the principal borrower nor owed any financial debt to the financial creditor and had not committed any default in repayment of the stated outstanding amount.
Case: Laxmi Pat Surana v. Union Bank of India & Anr.

---

Q: On what grounds did the appellant challenge the maintainability of the application under Section 7 of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code?
A: The appellant challenged the maintainability of the application under Section 7 on the grounds that (i) the principal borrower was not a 'corporate person' and hence the Code could not be applied, and (ii) the application was barred by limitation as it was filed beyond the period of three years from the date of default.
Case: Laxmi Pat Surana v. Union Bank of India & Anr.

---

Q: What was the decision of the Adjudicating Authority regarding the objections raised by the appellant?
A: The Adjudicating Authority rejected the objections raised by the appellant, holding that the action could be initiated against the corporate debtor under Section 7 of the Code and that the application was within limitation due to the acknowledgment of debt by the corporate debtor on 8th December 2018.
Case: Laxmi Pat Surana v. Union Bank of India & Anr.

---

Q: What is the significance of the acknowledgment of debt by the corporate debtor on 8th December 2018?
A: The acknowledgment of debt by the corporate debtor on 8th December 2018 is significant because it extended the limitation period, making the application under Section 7 of the Code filed on 13th February 2019 within the permissible time frame.
Case: Laxmi Pat Surana v. Union Bank of India & Anr.

---

Q: How does the court address the issue of limitation in the context of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code?
A: The court addressed the issue of limitation by stating that the acknowledgment of debt by the corporate debtor extended the limitation period. The application under Section 7 of the Code is not for recovery of the amount but for the resolution of insolvency, and the acknowledgment of debt renews the limitation period for initiating such proceedings.
Case: Laxmi Pat Surana v. Union Bank of India & Anr.

---

Q: What directions did the court give in the case of R.K. Sabharwal vs State of Punjab regarding the post-based reservation roster?
A: In R.K. Sabharwal vs State of Punjab, the court directed that the post-based reservation roster should be followed prospectively from 10th February 1995, and the percentage of reservation should be worked out in relation to the number of posts forming the cadre strength.
Case: V. Lakshmikanthan and Anr. vs Union of India and Ors.

---

Q: Who issued the show cause notice to M/S Ganpati Overseas and on what basis?
A: The show cause notice was issued by the Additional Director General, Directorate of Revenue Intelligence, New Delhi, based on secret information that M/S Ganpati Overseas had imported tuners from Hong Kong at grossly under-invoiced prices, thereby evading huge customs duty.
Case: Commissioner of Customs (Imports), Mumbai vs. M/S Ganpati Overseas

---

Q: What was the result of the Directorate of Revenue Intelligence's investigation into M/S Ganpati Overseas?
A: The investigation revealed that M/S Ganpati Overseas had imported twenty consignments during 1997-98 and 1998-99 at under-invoiced prices, resulting in evasion of customs duty amounting to approximately twenty-five to thirty lakhs rupees.
Case: Commissioner of Customs (Imports), Mumbai vs. M/S Ganpati Overseas

---

Q: What was the contention of the respondents regarding the statements made under Section 108 of the Customs Act?
A: The respondents contended that the statements made under Section 108 of the Customs Act were obtained through coercion and duress, and thus, were not voluntary.
Case: Commissioner of Customs (Imports), Mumbai vs. M/S Ganpati Overseas

---

Q: What was the significance of the second set of export declarations filed by the foreign supplier in this case?
A: The second set of export declarations filed by the foreign supplier showed the price of goods matching the price declared in the import invoices, which was accepted by the Hong Kong customs authority with the imposition of a penalty. This undermined the basis of the initial export declarations used to enhance the value of the goods.
Case: Commissioner of Customs (Imports), Mumbai vs. M/S Ganpati Overseas

---

Q: Why did the CESTAT reject the initial export declarations used by the customs department?
A: The CESTAT rejected the initial export declarations because they were unattested photocopies and the foreign supplier had filed a second set of declarations with corrected prices that matched the import invoices, which were accepted by the Hong Kong customs authority.
Case: Commissioner of Customs (Imports), Mumbai vs. M/S Ganpati Overseas

---

Q: What were the main arguments of the appellant in challenging the CESTAT's decision?
A: The appellant argued that the adjudicating authority was justified in determining the value of the imported goods under Rule 8 of the Customs Valuation Rules based on the initial export declarations and that the statements made by the respondents under Section 108 of the Customs Act admitted to under-valuation.
Case: Commissioner of Customs (Imports), Mumbai vs. M/S Ganpati Overseas

---

Q: What could be the potential implications of the CESTAT's decision for future cases of under-valuation?
A: The CESTAT's decision may set a precedent for requiring higher standards of proof and corroboration for claims of under-valuation, potentially making it more challenging for customs authorities to sustain similar allegations without robust, verifiable evidence.
Case: Commissioner of Customs (Imports), Mumbai vs. M/S Ganpati Overseas

---

Case: Ramesh Chandra Sharma & Ors. vs. State of Uttar Pradesh & Ors.

Q: How does the proportionality test apply to this case?
A: The proportionality test was applied to determine if the classification had a rational nexus with the object sought to be achieved, and it was found lacking, leading to the classification being struck down.

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Smt. Sulakshna vs Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd. & Anr.?

Case: Smt. Sulakshna vs Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd. & Anr.

---

Q: Why did the insurance company refuse to pay the claim initially?
A: The insurance company refused to pay the claim on the grounds that the premium was credited to their account on 09.03.2007, after the insured's death on 17.02.2007, and therefore, the policy was considered effective only from 09.03.2007.
Case: Smt. Sulakshna vs Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd. & Anr.

---

Q: Clarifying: What is the significance of the agent's role in this case?
A: The agent was authorized to accept the premium on behalf of the insurance company, and thus, the payment made to the agent was considered as payment made to the insurance company.
Case: Smt. Sulakshna vs Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd. & Anr.

---

Q: Predictive: How might this judgment impact future insurance claims involving delays in premium credit by agents?

Q: What was the main argument of the appellant-bank regarding the auction process?
A: The appellant-bank argued that all necessary procedures were followed, including the publication of proclamations and the obtaining of necessary approvals from the District Deputy Registrar, and that the auction sale was valid.
Case: Deenadayal Nagari Sahakari Bank Ltd. & Another vs. Munjaji & Others

---

Q: What was the significance of the Recovery Certificate dated 6.2.2010 in this case?
A: The Recovery Certificate dated 6.2.2010 was issued in favor of the appellant-bank under Section 101 of the Maharashtra Co-operative Societies Act, 1960, authorizing the recovery proceedings against the borrower.
Case: Deenadayal Nagari Sahakari Bank Ltd. & Another vs. Munjaji & Others

---

Q: What should cooperative banks ensure when conducting auction sales to avoid legal challenges similar to this case?
A: Cooperative banks should ensure strict compliance with procedural rules, maintain clear documentation of all steps taken, and be prepared to defend the validity of their actions in court.
Case: Deenadayal Nagari Sahakari Bank Ltd. & Another vs. Munjaji & Others

---

Q: What did the Central Administrative Tribunal (CAT) decide regarding the respondents' eligibility?
A: The CAT decided in favor of the respondents, directing the Union of India to send them for training and appoint them as Postal Assistants, stating that the advertisement did not exclude candidates from the vocational stream.
Case: Union of India vs. Uzair Imran & Ors.

---

Q: What was the Union of India's argument regarding the amendment to the recruitment rules?
A: The Union of India argued that the recruitment rules were amended in 1992 to exclude candidates from the vocational stream and that this amendment was in force before the 1995 recruitment process started, making the respondents ineligible.
Case: Union of India vs. Uzair Imran & Ors.

---

Q: How should candidates ensure their eligibility for government recruitment processes following this judgment?
A: Candidates should carefully review the statutory recruitment rules and ensure they meet all specified qualifications, avoiding reliance on any executive orders or clarifications that may conflict with these rules.
Case: Union of India vs. Uzair Imran & Ors.

---

Q: What does this judgment clarify about the treatment of vocational stream qualifications in government recruitments?
A: The judgment clarifies that vocational stream qualifications may be excluded from eligibility if specified in statutory recruitment rules, and such exclusions must be clearly communicated and adhered to in the recruitment process.
Case: Union of India vs. Uzair Imran & Ors.

---

Q: How did the appellants challenge the examination results?
A: The appellants argued that the requirement to secure 50% marks should be applied paper-wise rather than subject-wise, and since subjects 3 and 4 were part of the same paper, the aggregate marks should be considered instead of individual subject marks.
Case: Lance Nayak Raj Bahadur & Ors. vs. State of UP & Ors.

---

Q: What evidence supported the validity of the will in this case?
A: The validity of the will was supported by the testimonies of the testator's sons, the testimony of a witness present at the signing, the registered partition deed, and a document executed by Upendra acknowledging the will.
Case: Ashutosh Samanta (D) by LRS. & Ors. vs. Sm. Ranjan Bala Dasi & Ors.

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of The Bijnor Urban Cooperative Bank Limited, Bijnor & others vs. Meenal Agarwal & others?
A: The primary issue was whether Meenal Agarwal was eligible for the One Time Settlement (OTS) scheme despite her loan account being classified as a Non-Performing Asset (NPA).
Case: The Bijnor Urban Cooperative Bank Limited, Bijnor & others vs. Meenal Agarwal & others

---

Q: What was the Bank's argument against granting the OTS benefit to Meenal Agarwal?
A: The Bank argued that Meenal Agarwal was not eligible for the OTS scheme as her loan account was classified as NPA and there were sufficient assets to recover the loan amount through auction.
Case: The Bijnor Urban Cooperative Bank Limited, Bijnor & others vs. Meenal Agarwal & others

---

Q: What implications does this judgment have for future OTS applications?
A: This judgment implies that future OTS applications must strictly adhere to the eligibility criteria and that the decision to grant OTS benefits is at the discretion of the financial institution based on their commercial wisdom.
Case: The Bijnor Urban Cooperative Bank Limited, Bijnor & others vs. Meenal Agarwal & others

---

Q: How should banks approach the assessment of OTS applications following this judgment?
A: Banks should carefully evaluate each OTS application based on the eligibility criteria and ensure that all other recovery measures are considered before granting OTS benefits, maintaining their commercial interests.
Case: The Bijnor Urban Cooperative Bank Limited, Bijnor & others vs. Meenal Agarwal & others

---

Q: What future challenges might borrowers face when applying for OTS benefits?
A: Borrowers might face stricter scrutiny and must ensure that they meet all eligibility criteria, as banks will be more cautious in granting OTS benefits to avoid potential losses and ensure compliance with guidelines.
Case: The Bijnor Urban Cooperative Bank Limited, Bijnor & others vs. Meenal Agarwal & others

---

Q: What ex gratia amount was agreed upon by the appellants to be paid to the respondents?
A: The appellants agreed to pay an ex gratia amount of Rupees one lakh fifty thousand to the respondents in full and final settlement on humanitarian grounds.
Case: Shri Yogiraj Shikshan Prasarak Mandal & Ors. vs. Vidya (Dead) Thru LRs & Anr.

---

Q: What is the significance of the combined report submitted by the Enquiry Committee in this case?
A: The combined report's significance lies in its compliance with Rule 37(6), where two members found the Head Mistress guilty while one member dissented, demonstrating that the procedural requirements were met.
Case: Shri Yogiraj Shikshan Prasarak Mandal & Ors. vs. Vidya (Dead) Thru LRs & Anr.

---

Q: What future implications does this judgment have for disciplinary enquiries in private schools?
A: This judgment underscores the importance of strict compliance with procedural rules in disciplinary enquiries, ensuring that all steps are properly documented and the requirements of Rule 37(6) are fulfilled to avoid legal challenges.
Case: Shri Yogiraj Shikshan Prasarak Mandal & Ors. vs. Vidya (Dead) Thru LRs & Anr.

---

Q: How should future valuation reports be approached to ensure their credibility?
A: Future valuation reports should be prepared by qualified and competent valuers, with thorough documentation and evidence to support their methodology and findings, ensuring transparency and accuracy to withstand legal scrutiny.
Case: The Executive Engineer, M.I.W. vs. Vitthal Damodar Patil & Anr.

---

Q: What is the case about?
A: The case is about a dispute between National High Speed Rail Corporation Limited (NHSRCL) and Montecarlo Limited regarding the rejection of Montecarlo's technical bid for a tender related to the Mumbai-Ahmedabad High Speed Rail Project.
Case: National High Speed Rail Corporation Limited vs. Montecarlo Limited & Anr.

---

Q: Why was Montecarlo Limited's bid rejected by NHSRCL?
A: Montecarlo Limited's bid was rejected because it was found to be technically non-responsive, specifically for not signing Form CON: 2.0 (Pending Litigation) and Form CON: 3.0 (Litigation History) in the physically submitted bid by the authorized representative.
Case: National High Speed Rail Corporation Limited vs. Montecarlo Limited & Anr.

---

Q: What role did JICA play in the bid evaluation process?
A: JICA (Japan International Cooperation Agency) appointed a consultant, JICC, to evaluate the technical bids. JICC's evaluation was approved by JICA, and NHSRCL acted based on this evaluation in rejecting Montecarlo's bid.
Case: National High Speed Rail Corporation Limited vs. Montecarlo Limited & Anr.

Q: What were the recommendations of the Tribal Advisory Council of Jharkhand regarding the reservation policy?

Q: What was the main legal issue in the case of Mohammed Abdul Wahid vs Nilofer & Anr.?

Date: 14th December 2023

Q: Why did the Cooperative Bank declare the loan as a Non-Performing Asset (NPA)?

Case: Erudhaya Priya vs. State Express Transport Corporation Ltd.

Q: What were the injuries sustained by Erudhaya Priya in the accident?

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Dr. Kriti Lakhina and Others vs. State of Karnataka and Others?
A: The main issue was the challenge to Clause 4.1 of the Information Bulletin issued by the Directorate of Medical Education and the Karnataka Examinations Authority, which imposed domicile restrictions for admission to post-graduate medical and dental courses in Karnataka.
Case: Dr. Kriti Lakhina and Others vs. State of Karnataka and Others
Date: 4th April 2018

---

Q: What did Clause 4.1 of the Information Bulletin require for eligibility for government seats in post-graduate medical and dental courses?
A: Clause 4.1 required candidates to be of Karnataka origin and have studied MBBS or BDS in a medical or dental college located in Karnataka or outside Karnataka, with certain domicile conditions.
Case: Dr. Kriti Lakhina and Others vs. State of Karnataka and Others
Date: 4th April 2018

---

Q: How did the State of Karnataka justify Clause 4.1 of the Information Bulletin?
A: The State of Karnataka argued that the eligibility conditions were necessary to ensure that the State's requirement for skilled human resources was met, especially given the large number of vacancies in public health care institutions and medical colleges.
Case: Dr. Kriti Lakhina and Others vs. State of Karnataka and Others
Date: 4th April 2018

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Mukesh Kumar vs. The State of Bihar & Ors.?
A: The primary issue was the failure of the Bihar State Pharmacy Council and the State of Bihar to ensure that only registered pharmacists were allowed to compound, prepare, mix, or dispense medicines in government hospitals, which was alleged to be in violation of the Pharmacy Act, 1948 and the Pharmacy Practice Regulations, 2015.
Case: Mukesh Kumar vs. The State of Bihar & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of M/S Orator Marketing Pvt. Ltd. vs. M/S Samtex Desinz Pvt. Ltd.?
A: The main issue was whether a person who gives a term loan to a Corporate Person, free of interest, on account of its working capital requirements, is a Financial Creditor under Section 7 of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016 (IBC).
Case: M/S Orator Marketing Pvt. Ltd. vs. M/S Samtex Desinz Pvt. Ltd.
Date: 26th July 2021

---

Q: What was the decision of the National Company Law Appellate Tribunal (NCLAT) regarding the appeal filed by M/S Orator Marketing Pvt. Ltd.?
A: The NCLAT upheld the NCLT's decision, dismissing the appeal on the grounds that the loan did not carry any interest and, therefore, could not be considered a 'financial debt'.
Case: M/S Orator Marketing Pvt. Ltd. vs. M/S Samtex Desinz Pvt. Ltd.
Date: 26th July 2021

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Aurangabad Municipal Corporation vs. Jayant S/O Sarvottamrao Kharwadkar & Ors.?

Case: Aurangabad Municipal Corporation vs. Jayant S/O Sarvottamrao Kharwadkar & Ors.

---

Q: What were the Applicant's main contentions for seeking the recall of the Judgment?
A: The Applicants contended that the Court Notices were sent to an old address, and they only learned about the Judgment from a news clipping. They argued that their Chartered Accountant, Mr. Sanjeev Narayan, received the documents but misunderstood them due to his cataract condition.
Case: Principal Commissioner of Income Tax (Central) – 1 vs. NRA Iron & Steel Pvt. Ltd.
Date: 25th October 2019

---

Q: What was the Revenue Department's argument against the recall of the Judgment?
A: The Revenue Department argued that the dasti Notice was duly served on Mr. Sanjeev Narayan, who was the authorized representative of the Applicant - Company and had sufficient time to inform his clients before his cataract surgery. They also pointed out that Mr. Narayan had appeared before Tax Authorities after the Notice was served.
Case: Principal Commissioner of Income Tax (Central) - 1 vs. NRA Iron & Steel Pvt. Ltd.

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of M/s. Standard Essential Oil Industries & Anr. vs. Forest Range Officer Kasargod & Ors.?

Date: 19th April 2018

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Maringmei Acham vs. M Maringmei Khuripou?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the suit filed by the appellant's late father, Maringmei Thaitoungam, seeking a declaration of his right as the village chief (Khullakpa), could continue after his death, considering the failure to implead his legal representatives in time.
Case: Maringmei Acham vs. M Maringmei Khuripou
Date: 3rd November 2022

---

Q: What was the appellant's argument regarding his right to continue the suit after his father's death?
A: The appellant argued that as per the Rongmei Kabui Customary Law, the post of village chief (Khullakpa) is hereditary, and he, being the eldest son, had the right to continue the suit filed by his father.
Case: Maringmei Acham vs. M Maringmei Khuripou
Date: 3rd November 2022

---

Q: What was the main legal issue in the case of R. Muthukumar & Ors. vs. The Chairman and Managing Director TANGEDCO & Ors.?

Case: R. Muthukumar & Ors. vs. The Chairman and Managing Director TANGEDCO & Ors.
Date: 7th February 2022

Q: What were the main contentions of Dhruva Enterprises against the NGT's decision?

---

Q: What is the purpose of the RBI Master Circular on Wilful Defaulters dated 01.07.2013?
A: The purpose of the RBI Master Circular is to disseminate credit information about wilful defaulters to caution banks and financial institutions to ensure that further bank finance is not made available to them.
Case: State Bank of India vs. M/s. Jah Developers Pvt. Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 8th May 2019

---

Q: What are the key components of the Grievance Redressal Mechanism in the Master Circular?
A: The Grievance Redressal Mechanism involves identifying wilful defaulters through a committee, advising the borrower about the proposal to classify them as a wilful defaulter, allowing the borrower reasonable time to represent against the decision, and providing a hearing if the borrower claims wrongful classification.
Case: State Bank of India vs. M/s. Jah Developers Pvt. Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 8th May 2019

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of State of Kerala vs. M/S Akay Flavours and Aromatics Ltd.?
A: The primary legal issue was the interpretation of SRO 1727/1993, an exemption notification issued by the State of Kerala, and determining the correct commencement date for the five-year exemption period.
Case: State of Kerala vs. M/S Akay Flavours and Aromatics Ltd.
Date: 2nd February 2023

---

Q: What were the contentions of the Revenue regarding the exemption period?
A: The Revenue contended that the exemption period should commence from the date of approval by the Central Government, which was 16th December 1993, when the permission letter was issued, enabling the assessee to import capital goods and start exporting finished products.
Case: State of Kerala vs. M/S Akay Flavours and Aromatics Ltd.
Date: 2nd February 2023

---

Q: What was the assessee's argument regarding the commencement of the exemption period?
A: The assessee argued that the exemption period should commence from the date of commencement of production, which was 1st October 1995, rather than from the date of approval by the Central Government.
Case: State of Kerala vs. M/S Akay Flavours and Aromatics Ltd.
Date: 2nd February 2023

---

Q: How did the Tribunal interpret the exemption notification?
A: The Tribunal concluded that the exemption period should not be commuted prior to the setting up of the unit, rejecting the Revenue's contention that the period should start from the date of approval by the Central Government.
Case: State of Kerala vs. M/S Akay Flavours and Aromatics Ltd.
Date: 2nd February 2023

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Toyota Jidosha Kabushiki Kaisha vs. M/S Prius Auto Industries Ltd. & Ors.?
A: The main issue was whether the defendants' use of the trade mark 'Prius' constituted passing off and infringement of the plaintiff's well-known trade marks, including 'Toyota', 'Toyota Innova', 'Toyota Device', and 'Prius'.
Case: Toyota Jidosha Kabushiki Kaisha vs. M/S Prius Auto Industries Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 14th December 2017

---

Q: What was the decision of the learned trial Judge regarding the use of the trade mark 'Prius'?
A: The learned trial Judge held that the defendants' use of the trade mark 'Prius' constituted passing off, as the plaintiff was the prior user of the mark globally and had established its reputation and goodwill. The court issued a permanent injunction against the defendants from using the mark 'Prius'.
Case: Toyota Jidosha Kabushiki Kaisha vs. M/S Prius Auto Industries Ltd. & Ors.
Date: 14th December 2017

---

Q: What did Section 80-IA of the Income Tax Act provide for?
A: Section 80-IA provided for deductions in respect of profits and gains from industrial undertakings or enterprises engaged in infrastructure development, subject to certain conditions, for a period of ten consecutive Assessment Years.
Case: Mahabir Industries vs. Principal Commissioner of Income Tax
Date: 18th May 2018

---

Q: Why did Mahabir Industries claim deductions under Section 80-IC after initially claiming under Section 80-IA?
A: Mahabir Industries claimed deductions under Section 80-IC after completing substantial expansion of its manufacturing unit in Himachal Pradesh, which qualified it for 100% deductions under the new provision aimed at promoting industrial growth in certain states.
Case: Mahabir Industries vs. Principal Commissioner of Income Tax
Date: 18th May 2018

---

Q: What was the argument of the Principal Commissioner of Income Tax regarding the deductions?
A: The Principal Commissioner of Income Tax argued that the total period for claiming deductions under Section 80-IA and Section 80-IC combined should not exceed ten years, as stipulated by Section 80-IC(6) of the Income Tax Act.
Case: Mahabir Industries vs. Principal Commissioner of Income Tax
Date: 18th May 2018

---

Q: What was the status of respondent no. 1 during the litigation process?
A: During the pendency of the litigation, respondent no. 1 had superannuated on 31st July 2019.
Case: Director General, Council of Scientific and Industrial Research vs. J.K. Prashar & Ors.

---

Q: What were the key reasons the trial court originally ruled in favor of Ratanlal's adoption claim?
A: The trial court ruled in favor of Ratanlal's adoption claim based on evidence such as photographs of the adoption ceremony, testimony from the priest who performed the ceremony, and letters and invitations addressed to Ratanlal with his adoptive father's name.

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Shivraj Singh Chouhan & Ors. vs. Speaker Madhya Pradesh Legislative Assembly & Ors.?
A: The main issue was whether the Governor of Madhya Pradesh could direct the Chief Minister to hold a floor test in the Legislative Assembly to prove his majority, amidst allegations that 22 Members of the Legislative Assembly had resigned under pressure.
Case: Shivraj Singh Chouhan & Ors. vs. Speaker Madhya Pradesh Legislative Assembly & Ors.
Date: 13th April 2020

---

Q: What prompted the Governor of Madhya Pradesh to direct the Chief Minister to hold a floor test?
A: The Governor directed the floor test after receiving information about the resignations of 22 Members of the Legislative Assembly, which allegedly reduced the ruling party's majority, and after the Chief Minister himself expressed willingness to prove his majority in the forthcoming session.
Case: Shivraj Singh Chouhan & Ors. vs. Speaker Madhya Pradesh Legislative Assembly & Ors.
Date: 13th April 2020

---

Q: What was the decision of the Family Court regarding the application for a DNA test?
A: The Family Court allowed the application for a DNA test, stating that the respondent had made out a prima-facie case justifying the exercise of discretion to direct a DNA test.
Case: Aparna Ajinkya Firodia vs. Ajinkya Arun Firodia
Date: 20th February 2023

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of Transmission Corporation of Andhra Pradesh Limited vs. M/S Rain Calcining Limited & Others decided on 29th November 2019?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the Transmission Corporation of Andhra Pradesh Limited (APTRANSCO) had the authority to levy wheeling charges and grid support charges on private power generators under the Andhra Pradesh Electricity Reforms Act, 1998.
Case: Transmission Corporation of Andhra Pradesh Limited vs. M/S Rain Calcining Limited & Others
Date: 29th November 2019

---

Q: What were the wheeling charges proposed by APTRANSCO for the year 2001-2002, and what was the Commission's final decision on these charges?
A: APTRANSCO proposed a wheeling charge of Rs.1 per Kwh for energy transmitted through its network. The Commission ultimately decided that the wheeling charges for the year 2002-2003 would be Paise 50 per Kwh, effective from 1st April 2002, along with system loss charges of 28.4 percent of energy input by the project developer.
Case: Transmission Corporation of Andhra Pradesh Limited vs. M/S Rain Calcining Limited & Others
Date: 29th November 2019

---

Q: What were the initial steps taken by S. Mahesh regarding his Scheduled Tribe certificate upon joining NLC?
A: S. Mahesh initially produced a photocopy of his Scheduled Tribe certificate and requested time to provide the original. He later informed NLC that he could not obtain the original certificate from the Revenue Authorities and requested to be treated as a general category candidate.
Case: S. Mahesh vs. The Chairman Cum Managing Director, Neyveli Lignite Corporation Ltd. Neyveli Tamil Nadu & Ors.
Date: 29th October 2018

---

Q: How did NLC initially respond to S. Mahesh's inability to produce the original Scheduled Tribe certificate?
A: NLC allowed S. Mahesh to join as a general category candidate and later promoted him to Junior Engineer Grade I, despite his inability to produce the original Scheduled Tribe certificate.
Case: S. Mahesh vs. The Chairman Cum Managing Director, Neyveli Lignite Corporation Ltd. Neyveli Tamil Nadu & Ors.
Date: 29th October 2018

---

Q: What was the trial court's decision regarding JCS's application for impleadment?
A: The trial court dismissed JCS's application for impleadment, holding that he was not a necessary or proper party and that he could file a separate suit for his claims.
Case: Pruthvirajsinh Nodhubha Jadeja vs. Jayeshkumar Chhakaddas Shah & Ors.
Date: 4th October 2019

---

Q: What was the role of NOIDA in the construction of Towers T-16 and T-17?
A: NOIDA was found to have colluded with Supertech Limited by approving the revised building plans and not ensuring compliance with the building regulations and the UP Apartments Act 2010.
Case: Supertech Limited vs Emerald Court Owner Resident Welfare Association
Date: 31st August 2021

---

Q: What were the reasons provided by ITI Limited for rolling back the retirement age from 60 to 58 years?
A: ITI Limited cited financial losses and recommendations from a consultant, M/s. Price Waterhouse Coopers, to reduce manpower costs as reasons for rolling back the retirement age from 60 to 58 years.
Case: Chairman-cum-M.D. ITI Limited vs. K. Muniswamy & Ors.
Date: 2nd March 2023

---

Q: What was the appellant's argument regarding his caste certificate in the case?
A: The appellant argued that he belongs to the Jaat caste, which falls within the category of Other Backward Classes (OBCs) in the State of Uttar Pradesh, and he was issued a certificate to this effect by the Tesildar.
Case: Khilendra Singh vs. Union of India Ministry of Agriculture
Date: 28th November 2017

---

Q: What did the court conclude regarding the prescription of minimum qualifying marks for interviews?
A: The court concluded that prescribing minimum qualifying marks for interviews is permissible and not in violation of the All India Judges (2002) decision. The court upheld the validity of Clause 11 of the Bihar Rules, 1951 and Rule 8(3) of the Gujarat Rules, 2005.
Case: Abhimeet Sinha & Ors. vs. High Court of Judicature at Patna & Ors.
Date: 6th May 2024

---

Q: What was the outcome of the pending applications related to the appeals?
A: The pending applications, if any, were disposed of, and there were no orders as to costs.
Case: Shankar vs. The State of Maharashtra & Ors.
Date: 7th November 2017

---

Q: What is the Anugraha Narayan Sinha Institute of Social Studies, and how is it governed?
A: The Institute is incorporated by the Anugraha Narayan Sinha Institute of Social Studies Act, 1964, and is governed by a Board of Control, which includes nominees and representatives from the State Government, Indian Council of Social Science Research, University Grants Commission, and other educational institutions.
Case: The State of Bihar & Anr. vs. Dr. Sachindra Narayan & Ors.
Date: 30th January 2019

---

Q: What argument did the State of Uttar Pradesh present in defense of the 2016 amendment to the 1981 Act?
A: The State of Uttar Pradesh argued that the 2016 amendment provided a legitimate basis for allotting government accommodation to former Chief Ministers, asserting that these individuals formed a distinct class deserving of special privileges.
Case: Lok Prahari through its general secretary vs. The State of Uttar Pradesh & Ors.
Date: 7th May 2018

---

Q: What impact did the court's decision have on the 2016 amendment to the 1981 Act?
A: The court's decision struck down Section 4(3) of the 1981 Act, rendering the 2016 amendment invalid. This ruling effectively nullified the provision allowing former Chief Ministers to retain government accommodation for their lifetime.
Case: Lok Prahari through its general secretary vs. The State of Uttar Pradesh & Ors.
Date: 7th May 2018

---

Q: What argument did the Commissioner of Income Tax present against the eligibility of ICDs for tax deductions?
A: The Commissioner of Income Tax argued that the activities undertaken by the assessee did not fall within the definition of 'infrastructure facility' as per Section 80-IA(4) of the Income Tax Act and that the CBDT's notification allowing such deductions was no longer valid after the amendment in 2001.
Case: Commissioner of Income Tax, Delhi vs. M/s Container Corporation of India Ltd.
Date: 24th April 2018

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the appeal filed by Life Insurance Corporation of India against the State of Rajasthan?
A: The primary legal issue was whether the state of Rajasthan has the power and jurisdiction to levy and collect stamp duty on insurance policies issued within the state.
Case: Life Insurance Corporation of India vs. The State of Rajasthan & Ors.
Date: 30th April 2024

---

Q: What was the main issue addressed in the miscellaneous applications filed by the Union of India?
A: The miscellaneous applications were filed for the modification of paragraph 38(i) of the judgment dated 01.09.2020, seeking clarification that the judgment does not bar the Union of India from considering and rectifying clerical or arithmetical errors in the computation of AGR dues.
Case: Union of India vs. Association of Unified Telecom Service Providers of India and Ors.

---

Q: What was the argument made by the applicants regarding calculation errors in AGR dues?
A: The applicants argued that certain arithmetical errors had arisen due to inadvertence on the part of the Department of Telecommunications while computing the dues. They emphasized that they did not intend to reopen issues already decided but sought rectification of these errors.
Case: Union of India vs. Association of Unified Telecom Service Providers of India and Ors.

---

Q: What justification did the State of Madhya Pradesh provide for introducing Rule 12 (8) (a)?
A: The State of Madhya Pradesh introduced Rule 12 (8) (a) to prevent manipulations in the admission process, ensuring that lesser meritorious candidates do not get admission in better colleges due to blocking of seats and subsequent resignations.
Case: Index Medical College, Hospital and Research Centre vs. The State of Madhya Pradesh & Ors.

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.?
A: The primary issue was whether the charges realized by the Assessees for providing containers to customers should be included in the value for the purpose of levy of duty under Section 4 of the Central Excise Act, 1944.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

---

Q: What products are manufactured by the respondents in the case?
A: The respondents are manufacturers of dissolved and compressed industrial gases, liquid chlorine, and other allied products, as well as cotton yarn and Post Mix Concentrate.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

---

Q: What was the conflict between the two decisions that led to the referral to a larger bench?
A: The conflict was between the decisions in Union of India and Ors. v. Bombay Tyre International Ltd. and Ors. and Commissioner of Central Excise, Pondicherry v. Acer India Ltd., regarding whether the charges realized by the Assessees should be included in the value for the purpose of levy of duty.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

---

Q: What did the court conclude regarding the measure of levy under Section 4 of the Central Excise Act?
A: The court concluded that the measure of the levy contemplated in Section 4 of the Act will not be controlled by the nature of the levy, and that 'transaction value' as defined in Section 4(3)(d) includes permissible additions to the value of the product until its clearance.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs. M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Caretel Infotech Ltd. vs. Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited?
A: The primary issue was whether Caretel Infotech Ltd. violated the tender conditions by not disclosing a show cause notice for blacklisting received after the tender was floated but before the bid submission.
Case: Caretel Infotech Ltd. vs. Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited & Ors.

---

Q: What action did Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited (HPCL) take on 4th December 2017?
A: HPCL floated an e-public tender for setting up call centres for receiving, recording, and replying to information enquiries and complaints from LPG customers.
Case: Caretel Infotech Ltd. vs. Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited & Ors.

---

Q: What was the content of the show cause notice issued to Caretel Infotech Ltd. on 5th December 2017?
A: The show cause notice alleged that Caretel Infotech Ltd. had endeavoured to procure a tender by providing false, misleading, and wrong information, and it requested the company to clarify its position within seven days.
Case: Caretel Infotech Ltd. vs. Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited & Ors.

---

Q: What were the consequences of the blacklisting order issued on 22nd February 2018 against Caretel Infotech Ltd.?
A: The blacklisting order debarred Caretel Infotech Ltd. from participating in any tender process of the Government of India, Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers’ Welfare for two years from the date of the order.
Case: Caretel Infotech Ltd. vs. Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Limited & Ors.

---

Q: What was the decision of the Additional District Judge regarding the arbitration award?
A: The Additional District Judge upheld the arbitration award on 4th June 2009, dismissing the respondents' application under Section 34 of the Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996.
Case: Punjab State Civil Supplies Corporation Ltd. & Anr. vs. M/s Atwal Rice & General Mills

---

Q: What were some of the changes made in the final Haj Policy 2019-2023?
A: Changes included reducing the security deposit for HGOs, modifying the distribution formula to ensure a minimum allocation of seats, and simplifying documentation requirements for proof of payment for accommodation and air travel.
Case: Federation Haj PTOs of India vs Union of India

---

Q: What was the primary issue challenged by M/S Godrej Sara Lee Ltd. in this case?
A: The primary issue challenged by M/S Godrej Sara Lee Ltd. was the jurisdiction of the Deputy Excise and Taxation Commissioner (ST)-cum-Revisional Authority, Kurukshetra to reopen proceedings and pass final orders increasing the tax rate on mosquito repellants from 4% to 10%.
Case: M/S Godrej Sara Lee Ltd. vs. The Excise and Taxation Officer-cum-Assessing Authority & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main grievance of the M.P. Medical Officers Association in this case?
A: The main grievance of the M.P. Medical Officers Association was the quashing and setting aside of the benefits granted under the circular dated 23.05.2009, which provided higher pay scales to Medical Officers, Dental Surgeons, and officers in the specialist cadre.
Case: M.P. Medical Officers Association vs. The State of Madhya Pradesh & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main legal issue addressed in the case?
A: The main legal issue was whether the judgment in N.V. International v. State of Assam lays down the law correctly regarding the period of limitation for filing appeals under section 37 of the Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996.
Case: Government of Maharashtra (Water Resources Department) vs M/S Borse Brothers Engineers & Contractors Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What was the appellant's argument regarding the limitation period?
A: The appellant, Government of Maharashtra, argued that the rationale or logic contained in N.V. International should apply only to appeals from section 34 orders, but not to orders passed under other sections such as sections 8, 9, 16, and 17 of the Arbitration Act.
Case: Government of Maharashtra (Water Resources Department) vs M/S Borse Brothers Engineers & Contractors Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.?
A: The primary issue was whether the various charges realized by the Assessees for making available or filling up containers provided by the customers should be included in the value for the purpose of levy of duty under Section 4 of the Central Excise Act, 1944, as amended with effect from 1st July, 2000.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

---

Q: What items were manufactured by the respondent, M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.?
A: The respondent, M/S Grasim Industries Ltd., manufactured dissolved and compressed industrial gases, liquid chlorine, and other allied products. Cotton yarn and Post Mix Concentrate manufactured by two other individual assessees were also in issue.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

---

Q: What was the conflict perceived between two decisions of the court that led to this case being referred to a larger bench?
A: The conflict was perceived between the decisions in Union of India and Ors. v. Bombay Tyre International Ltd. and Ors. and Commissioner of Central Excise, Pondicherry v. Acer India Ltd., regarding whether the concept of 'transaction value' made any material departure from the deemed normal price concept of the erstwhile Section 4(1)(a) of the Act.
Case: Commissioner of Central Excise, Indore vs M/S Grasim Industries Ltd.

---

Q: What was the outcome of the ex-parte decree passed on 16th September 1997 in the case?
A: An ex-parte decree was passed in favor of Respondent No.2, awarding Rs. 22,400/- along with 9% interest, as the defendant (Sri Krishna) failed to appear in court.
Case: Vishwabandhu vs. Sri Krishna and Anr.

---

Q: Why did the appellants seek condonation of delay in the case?
A: The appellants sought condonation of delay of 349 days in filing the appeal, claiming they only became aware of the judgment dated 10.05.2012 on 06.05.2013 when informed by a constable from the Police Station Ghosi.
Case: Mohd. Sahid and Others vs. Raziya Khanam (D) Thr. LRs and Another

---

Q: Who were the appellants and respondents in this case?
A: The appellants were St. Mary's Education Society and its disciplinary committee, and the respondents were Rajendra Prasad Bhargava and others.
Case: St. Mary's Education Society & Anr. vs. Rajendra Prasad Bhargava & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Union of India & Ors. vs. M/s. Hamdard (Waqf) Laboratories?
A: The main issue was the classification of 'Rooh Afza' under the Central Excise Tariff Act, 1986. The respondent claimed it should be classified under sub-heading 2201.90, but the Revenue classified it under sub-heading 2107.91.
Case: Union of India & Ors. vs. M/s. Hamdard (Waqf) Laboratories

---

Q: What was the initial issue that led to the disciplinary action against Rajit Singh?
A: Rajit Singh, a Junior Engineer at Balia, was found to have committed financial irregularities causing a loss to the Government. Disciplinary proceedings were initiated, and he was served with a charge sheet.
Case: The State of Uttar Pradesh and Ors. vs. Rajit Singh

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Sansera Engineering Limited vs. Deputy Commissioner, Large Tax Payer Unit, Bengaluru?

Case: Sansera Engineering Limited vs. Deputy Commissioner, Large Tax Payer Unit, Bengaluru

---

Q: Why was Anjum Ara initially disqualified from her position as Anganwari Sevika?

Case: Anjum Ara vs The State of Bihar

---

Q: What was the final decision of the court regarding these appeals?

Case: Patni Hushenbhai Sidibhai Kotvala vs National Highways Authority of India

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Indian Oil Corporation Limited vs State of Bihar & Anr.?
A: The primary issue was regarding the demands made from the Appellant (Indian Oil Corporation Limited) for payment of Entry Tax under the Bihar Tax on Entry of Goods into Local Areas for Consumption, Use or Sale Therein Act, 1993, specifically concerning the set-off of VAT against Entry Tax.
Case: Indian Oil Corporation Limited vs State of Bihar & Anr.

---

Q: What was the Appellant's argument regarding the set-off of VAT against Entry Tax?
A: The Appellant argued that the Entry Tax Act in Bihar was essentially to ensure that VAT was collected under the VAT Act in the State. They claimed that the set-off on goods which bear VAT should be allowable under Section 3(2) second proviso of the Entry Tax Act. This practice was followed until 2014, but was later disallowed due to audit objections.
Case: Indian Oil Corporation Limited vs State of Bihar & Anr.

---

Q: What were the specific bonds involved in the case, and what were their interest rates?
A: The bonds involved were SIDBI Bonds 2003 (4th Series) with 13.50% interest and SIDBI Bonds 2004 (5th Series) with 12.50% interest.
Case: Small Industries Development Bank of India vs M/S. SIBCO Investment Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What actions did the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) take that affected the case?
A: The RBI issued directives to SIDBI not to transfer or pay interest on the bonds without permission from the Official Liquidator, due to the winding-up proceedings against CRB Capital, the original holder of the bonds.
Case: Small Industries Development Bank of India vs M/S. SIBCO Investment Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What was the outcome of the liquidation proceedings initiated by the RBI against CRB Capital in relation to the bonds held by SIBCO?
A: The Company Court ruled that the bonds were beyond the purview of the liquidation proceedings, allowing SIBCO to present the bonds to SIDBI for payment, which was made with interest calculated up to the date of redemption.
Case: Small Industries Development Bank of India vs M/S. SIBCO Investment Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What was the appellant's argument regarding the limitation period in Vishram Varu & Co. vs. Union of India?
A: The appellant argued that the limitation period should start from the date of the legal notice invoking the arbitration clause, not from the date of work completion.
Case: Vishram Varu & Co. vs. Union of India

---

Q: How much payment had the appellants made to the respondent-developer by 23rd June 2014?
A: The appellants had made payment of 90% of the total sale consideration, which amounted to nearly Rs. 2,21,56,942.42, to the respondent-developer by 23rd June 2014.
Case: Sanjay Chaudhary and Anr. vs. Pioneer Urban Land & Infrastructure Ltd. and Anr.

---

Q: What is the main issue addressed in the case of DKG Buildcon Private Ltd. vs. SEBI?
A: The main issue addressed in the case is the non-compliance with summons issued by SEBI for the production of documents and furnishing of information during an investigation into the manipulation of share prices and aiding Ketan Parekh in rigging the securities market.
Case: DKG Buildcon Private Ltd. vs. The Adjudicating & Enquiry Officer, S.E.B.I

---

Q: What was the outcome of the SEBI investigation concerning DKG Buildcon Private Ltd.?
A: The SEBI investigation concluded that DKG Buildcon Private Ltd. and other entities had facilitated Ketan Parekh in manipulating the securities market. SEBI imposed a penalty of rupees one crore on DKG Buildcon for non-compliance with the summons.
Case: DKG Buildcon Private Ltd. vs. The Adjudicating & Enquiry Officer, S.E.B.I

---

Q: What were the specific allegations against DKG Buildcon regarding the manipulation of share prices?
A: The allegations against DKG Buildcon included transferring shares of Shonkh Technology International Ltd. to entities controlled by Ketan Parekh, who then manipulated the securities market by creating artificial volumes and synchronized trading to inflate share prices.
Case: DKG Buildcon Private Ltd. vs. The Adjudicating & Enquiry Officer, S.E.B.I

---

Q: What steps did SEBI take before imposing the penalty on DKG Buildcon?
A: Before imposing the penalty, SEBI issued multiple summons to DKG Buildcon to furnish information and documents and appear before the Investigating Authority. Due to DKG Buildcon's repeated non-compliance, SEBI initiated adjudication proceedings and issued a show cause notice before finally imposing the penalty.
Case: DKG Buildcon Private Ltd. vs. The Adjudicating & Enquiry Officer, S.E.B.I

---

Q: What were the facts leading to the appeal?
A: Sau. Sangeeta W/O Sunil Shinde along with respondents 3, 4, and 5 were elected as members of the Panchayat Samiti, Shrirampur in 2017 on the INC Party ticket. Initially, Sangeeta was selected as the Gatneta in 2017, but later, due to internal conflicts, respondent 3 was selected as the Gatneta by a majority resolution, which was approved by the District Collector.
Case: SAU. SANGEETA W/O SUNIL SHINDE vs THE STATE OF MAHARASHTRA AND ORS.

---

Q: What did the appellant argue regarding her removal as Gatneta?
A: The appellant argued that according to the rules submitted to the District Collector, she was elected as Party Leader for a period of five years and could not be removed before that period. She also contended that the meeting to remove her was convened by an outsider, the President of Ahmednagar District INC Party, which she claimed was against the rules.
Case: SAU. SANGEETA W/O SUNIL SHINDE vs THE STATE OF MAHARASHTRA AND ORS.

---

Q: What was the appellant's argument regarding his involvement in the suit and settlement?
A: The appellant argued that he was neither a party to the suit nor to the settlement.
Case: Sudhir Kumar Jain vs. Neeraj Kumar Jain & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case between The Municipal Corporation, Faridabad and Modern School, Faridabad?
A: The main issue was the levy of user charges by the Municipal Corporation for discharging waste water from the school's tubewells into the municipal sewer system, which the schools contested as being unauthorized.
Case: The Municipal Corporation, Faridabad vs. Modern School, Faridabad & Ors.

---

Q: Why did the Municipal Corporation, Faridabad issue a notice to Modern School regarding their tubewell?
A: The Municipal Corporation issued a notice because the school had installed a tubewell without prior approval, and was using it to discharge water into the municipal sewer system, for which the corporation demanded user charges.
Case: The Municipal Corporation, Faridabad vs. Modern School, Faridabad & Ors.

---

Q: What was the central issue in the case of Government of Kerala & Anr. vs. Mother Superior Adoration Convent decided on 1st March 2021?

Case: Government of Kerala & Anr. vs. Mother Superior Adoration Convent

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of S. Rukmini Madegowda vs The State Election Commission & Ors.?
A: The primary legal issue was the alleged false declaration of assets by S. Rukmini Madegowda in her affidavit during the election process, which led to the setting aside of her election as Councillor from Ward No.36-Yeraganahalli, Karnataka.
Case: S. Rukmini Madegowda vs The State Election Commission & Ors.

---

Q: What was the construction project undertaken by Shree Ram Urban Infrastructure Ltd.?
A: Shree Ram Urban Infrastructure Ltd. undertook the construction of a building at Plot No. 5B+6, including a main residential building of 56 floors and a Public Parking Lot (PPL) with three basements, ground floor, and 15 upper floors.
Case: Shree Ram Urban Infrastructure Ltd. & Anr. vs State of Maharashtra & Ors.

---

Q: What was the controversy regarding the Public Parking Lot (PPL) constructed by Shree Ram Urban Infrastructure Ltd.?
A: The controversy centered around the legality of the construction of the PPL above the plinth level without obtaining the required commencement certificates, and whether Shree Ram Urban Infrastructure Ltd. could claim incentive Floor Space Index (FSI) for the residential building based on the PPL.
Case: Shree Ram Urban Infrastructure Ltd. & Anr. vs State of Maharashtra & Ors.

---

Q: What was the primary legal question raised in the case of Dushyant N. Dalal and Another vs. Securities and Exchange Board of India?
A: The primary legal question raised was whether interest can be recovered on orders of penalty and/or disgorgement of unlawful gains under Section 28A of the Securities and Exchange Board of India Act, 1992 when the amounts have remained unpaid.
Case: Dushyant N. Dalal and Another vs. Securities and Exchange Board of India

---

Q: What penalties were imposed on Dushyant N. Dalal and Puloma D. Dalal by SEBI?
A: SEBI imposed the following penalties on Dushyant N. Dalal and Puloma D. Dalal: (a) a 45-day ban from buying, selling, or dealing in the securities market, (b) disgorgement of the unlawful gain of Rs. 4.05 crores, (c) payment of Rs. 1.95 crores as simple interest at 12% per annum for four years, (d) payment of Rs. 6 crores within 45 days, and (e) an additional seven-year ban from the securities market if the amount was not paid within the specified time.
Case: Dushyant N. Dalal and Another vs. Securities and Exchange Board of India

---

Q: Why did the appellant believe there was a typographical error in the settlement agreement?
A: The appellant believed there was a typographical error because the settlement agreement referred to the trademarks 'FX-991ES PLUS/ FX/ 991' instead of the intended 'FX-991ES PLUS/ FX-991'. The appellant argued that 'FX' is a generic term and not capable of being independently trademarked.
Case: Ajanta LLP vs Casio Keisanki Kabushiki Kaisha d/b/a Casio Computer Co. Ltd.

---

Q: How did the Railways respond to the directive to follow post-based reservation rosters in the case of V. Lakshmikanthan and Anr. vs Union of India and Ors.?
A: The Railways clarified that the post-based reservation rosters would be followed only with effect from 16th November 2005, despite the directive in Virpal Singh Chauhan (1995) to follow it from 10th February 1995.
Case: V. Lakshmikanthan and Anr. vs Union of India and Ors.

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the appeals filed by the Commissioner of Customs, Mumbai?
A: The primary issue was whether the CESTAT was justified in holding that the enhancement of value of the imported goods and the penalties imposed on the respondents by the Commissioner of Customs (Adjudication-1), Mumbai could not be sustained and consequently in setting aside the same.
Case: Commissioner of Customs (Imports), Mumbai vs. M/S Ganpati Overseas through its proprietor Shri Yashpal Sharma & Anr.

---

Q: What were the findings of the CESTAT regarding the export declarations filed by the foreign supplier?
A: The CESTAT found that the export declarations filed by the foreign supplier before the Hong Kong customs authority could not be relied upon for enhancing the value of the goods because they were unattested photocopies. The supplier had filed another set of declarations with corrected prices, which were accepted by the Hong Kong customs authority after imposing a penalty, which the supplier paid.
Case: Commissioner of Customs (Imports), Mumbai vs. M/S Ganpati Overseas through its proprietor Shri Yashpal Sharma & Anr.

---

Q: What were the key arguments made by the respondents in their defense?
A: The respondents argued that the statements of Mr. Yashpal Sharma and Mr. Suresh Chandra Sharma were not voluntary and were obtained under coercion. They also contended that the price declared in the import invoices was correct and comparable to contemporaneous imports. They emphasized that the unattested photocopies of export declarations could not be relied upon and that the second set of declarations filed by the supplier showed the correct prices.
Case: Commissioner of Customs (Imports), Mumbai vs. M/S Ganpati Overseas through its proprietor Shri Yashpal Sharma & Anr.

---

Q: What was the main legal issue addressed in the judgment involving Pravin Electricals Pvt. Ltd. and Galaxy Infra and Engineering Pvt. Ltd.?
A: The main legal issue addressed was whether there was a valid arbitration agreement between Pravin Electricals Pvt. Ltd. and Galaxy Infra and Engineering Pvt. Ltd. regarding a Consultancy Agreement dated 7th July, 2014.
Case: Pravin Electricals Pvt. Ltd. vs. Galaxy Infra and Engineering Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What were the key arguments presented by the appellant, Pravin Electricals Pvt. Ltd.?
A: Pravin Electricals Pvt. Ltd. argued that the Consultancy Agreement dated 7th July, 2014, was a concocted document, citing a CFSL report that found discrepancies in the signatures. They also highlighted that subsequent negotiations and a draft agreement indicated the original agreement did not exist.
Case: Pravin Electricals Pvt. Ltd. vs. Galaxy Infra and Engineering Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What was the respondent, Galaxy Infra and Engineering Pvt. Ltd.'s, primary defense against the appellant's allegations?
A: Galaxy Infra and Engineering Pvt. Ltd. claimed that despite the discrepancies, the correspondence and actions between the parties indicated a mutual understanding and existence of a Consultancy Agreement. They argued that Pravin Electricals Pvt. Ltd. had acknowledged and acted upon this agreement.
Case: Pravin Electricals Pvt. Ltd. vs. Galaxy Infra and Engineering Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: How did the court view the CFSL report and its implications in the judgment involving Pravin Electricals Pvt. Ltd. and Galaxy Infra and Engineering Pvt. Ltd.?
A: The court noted that the CFSL report was inconclusive regarding the authenticity of the signatures on the Consultancy Agreement. This, combined with other inconsistencies, led the court to decide that a deeper examination by an arbitrator was necessary.
Case: Pravin Electricals Pvt. Ltd. vs. Galaxy Infra and Engineering Pvt. Ltd.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case P. Nazeer vs. Salafi Trust?
A: The main issue was the right to management and administration of a mosque and its properties between Salafi Juma Masjid Mahal Committee and Salafi Trust, involving cross-suits regarding the validity of a certificate issued by the Kerala Waqf Board.
Case: P. Nazeer etc. vs. Salafi Trust & Anr. etc.

---

Q: What were the reliefs sought by the Salafi Juma Masjid Mahal Committee in O.S No.9 of 2004?
A: They sought a declaration that the certificate dated 24.03.2004 issued by the Chief Executive Officer of the Kerala Waqf Board was null and void, and a permanent injunction restraining the defendants from interfering with the management and administration of the mosque.
Case: P. Nazeer etc. vs. Salafi Trust & Anr. etc.

---

Q: What was the decision of the Waqf Tribunal regarding the suit O.S No.9 of 2004?
A: The Waqf Tribunal granted the relief of permanent injunction but refused to declare the certificate as null and void.
Case: P. Nazeer etc. vs. Salafi Trust & Anr. etc.

---

Q: What is the relationship between the appellant and the respondent in the case of Somakka (Dead) By Lrs. Vs. K.P. Basavaraj (Dead) By Lrs.?
A: The appellant is the own sister of the sole respondent.
Case: Somakka (Dead) By Lrs. Vs. K.P. Basavaraj (Dead) By Lrs.

---

Q: What were the technical issues faced by the appellants with the photo processing machine?
A: The appellants faced various technical, mechanical, and software problems with the 'Agfa Minilab D-Lab.1 Allrounder' machine, including the existence of grains in the print and the machine not performing to the assured standard, even after multiple attempts by engineers to fix it.
Case: M/s. Paramount Digital Color Lab & Ors. Etc. v. M/s. Agfa India Pvt. Ltd. & Ors. Etc.

---

Q: What is the main issue in the case of Najiya Neermunda & Anr. Etc. vs. Kunhitharuvai Memorial Charitable Trust & Ors. Etc.?
A: The main issue is the fee fixation by the Admission and Fee Regulatory Committee for MBBS students in private self-financing medical colleges in Kerala.
Case: Najiya Neermunda & Anr. Etc. vs. Kunhitharuvai Memorial Charitable Trust & Ors. Etc.

---

Q: What legislation governs the fee fixation in this case?
A: The fee fixation is governed by the Kerala Medical Education (Regulation and Control of Admission to Private Medical Educational Institutions) Act, 2017.
Case: Najiya Neermunda & Anr. Etc. vs. Kunhitharuvai Memorial Charitable Trust & Ors. Etc.

---

Q: What were the main contentions of the managements of private self-financing medical colleges?
A: The managements contended that the Committee acted in excess of its jurisdiction in fixing the fee and that all members of the Committee were not parties to the order of fee fixation.
Case: Najiya Neermunda & Anr. Etc. vs. Kunhitharuvai Memorial Charitable Trust & Ors. Etc.

---

Q: What was the primary issue in the case of Smt. Sulakshna vs Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd. & Anr.?
A: The primary issue was whether the insurance company was liable to pay the claim amount for the policies when the premium was paid before the insured's death but credited to the insurance company's account after the insured's death.
Case: Smt. Sulakshna vs Oriental Insurance Co. Ltd. & Anr.

---

Q: What percentage of the Committee of Creditors approved the Resolution Plan?
A: The Resolution Plan was approved by the Committee of Creditors with a majority of 89.92%.
Case: Kridhan Infrastructure Pvt Ltd (Now known as Krish Steel and Trading Pvt Ltd) vs. Venkatesan Sankaranarayan & Ors.

---

Q: What actions did the appellant fail to fulfill under the Resolution Plan?
A: The appellant failed to fulfill its obligations, including the equity infusion, despite numerous opportunities over a period of six months.
Case: Kridhan Infrastructure Pvt Ltd (Now known as Krish Steel and Trading Pvt Ltd) vs. Venkatesan Sankaranarayan & Ors.

---

Q: What was the decision of the NCLAT regarding the liquidation of Tecpro Systems Limited?
A: The NCLAT upheld the order of liquidation, noting that the appellant had failed to implement the Resolution Plan for over eight months and declined to exercise its jurisdiction under Rule 11 of the NCLAT Rules, 2016.
Case: Kridhan Infrastructure Pvt Ltd (Now known as Krish Steel and Trading Pvt Ltd) vs. Venkatesan Sankaranarayan & Ors.

---

Q: What is the case of Anita Rani v. Ashok Kumar about?
A: The case involves Anita Rani filing two money suits against Ashok Kumar and others for the recovery of sums amounting to Rs. 10,48,000 and Rs. 67,31,000 respectively, which she claimed were either loans not repaid or unauthorized withdrawals from her bank account.
Case: Anita Rani vs. Ashok Kumar & Ors.

---

Q: What was the defense of Ashok Kumar and the other respondents in the suits?
A: The respondents claimed that the money was either for investment in real estate business, authorized payments, or a gratuitous payment out of love and affection. They also contended that the payment of Rs. 5,00,000 was in full and final settlement of the claim.
Case: Anita Rani vs. Ashok Kumar & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main legal issue in the case of Arun Kumar Jagatramka vs Jindal Steel and Power Ltd.?
A: The main legal issue was whether a person who is ineligible under Section 29A of the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016 (IBC) to submit a resolution plan is also barred from proposing a scheme of compromise and arrangement under Section 230 of the Companies Act, 2013.
Case: Arun Kumar Jagatramka vs. Jindal Steel and Power Ltd. & Anr.

---

Q: What was the decision of the National Company Law Appellate Tribunal (NCLAT) on 24 October 2019?
A: The NCLAT held that a person ineligible under Section 29A of the IBC to submit a resolution plan is also barred from proposing a scheme of compromise and arrangement under Section 230 of the Companies Act, 2013. This decision was rendered in favor of Jindal Steel and Power Limited (JSPL) and reversed the earlier decision of the National Company Law Tribunal (NCLT).
Case: Arun Kumar Jagatramka vs. Jindal Steel and Power Ltd. & Anr.

---

Q: Why did Arun Kumar Jagatramka challenge the decision of the NCLAT?
A: Arun Kumar Jagatramka challenged the NCLAT decision on the grounds that Section 230 of the Companies Act, 2013 does not place any embargo on any person for submitting a scheme. He argued that the NCLAT’s interpretation of Section 29A of the IBC into Section 230 amounted to a judicial reframing of legislation.
Case: Arun Kumar Jagatramka vs. Jindal Steel and Power Ltd. & Anr.

---

Q: What was the origin of the litigation in the case of Dipali Biswas & Ors. vs. Nirmalendu Mukherjee & Ors.?
A: The litigation originated from a suit filed by Ms. Rama Rani Devi in 1971 for the recovery of a sum of Rs.3000 against Sasadhar Biswas. The suit was decreed ex parte on 25.07.1974, directing the defendant to pay the amount in six equal instalments with a default clause.
Case: Dipali Biswas & Ors. vs. Nirmalendu Mukherjee & Ors.

---

Q: What was the result of the auction sale held on 30.05.1979?
A: The auction sale held on 30.05.1979 resulted in two brothers, Sachindra Nath Mukherjee and Dulal Kanti Mukherjee, becoming the highest bidders with an offer of Rs.5500. They deposited the money into court.
Case: Dipali Biswas & Ors. vs. Nirmalendu Mukherjee & Ors.

---

Q: What was the dispute in the case Jaipur Zila Dugdh Utpadak Sahkari Sangh Limited vs. Ajay Sales Suppliers?
A: The dispute arose from a distributorship agreement dated 31.03.2015 for the distribution of milk and buttermilk in certain zones in Jaipur. The respondent pointed out grievances and a commercial dispute arose between the parties.
Case: Jaipur Zila Dugdh Utpadak Sahkari Sangh Limited & Ors. vs. M/s Ajay Sales & Suppliers

---

Q: What was the arbitration clause in the distributorship agreement?
A: Clause 13 of the distributorship agreement provided for arbitration. It stated that all disputes arising out of or concerning the agreement shall be referred to the sole Arbitrator, the Chairman of Jaipur Zila Dugdh Utpadak Sahkari Sangh Ltd., whose decision would be final and binding.
Case: Jaipur Zila Dugdh Utpadak Sahkari Sangh Limited & Ors. vs. M/s Ajay Sales & Suppliers

---

Q: What argument did the appellants (State of Uttar Pradesh) present in defense of Regulation 101?
A: The appellants argued that Regulation 101 was a policy decision made in the public interest to ensure financial efficiency and that such policy decisions are not amenable to challenge unless there is manifest arbitrariness.
Case: State of Uttar Pradesh & Ors. vs. Principal Abhay Nandan Inter College & Ors.

---

Q: What was the main issue in the case of Shri Kshetrimayum Biren Singh v. The Hon'ble Speaker, Manipur Legislative Assembly & Ors.?
A: The main issue was the disqualification of Shri Kshetrimayum Biren Singh as a Member of the Legislative Assembly of Manipur on the grounds that he had voluntarily given up his membership of the Indian National Congress (INC) and joined the Bharatiya Janata Party (BJP).
Case: Shri Kshetrimayum Biren Singh vs. The Hon'ble Speaker, Manipur Legislative Assembly & Ors.

---

Q: What was the prosecution's case in brief in the Amar Nath Jha vs. Nand Kishore Singh & Etc. judgment?
A: The prosecution's case was that on the night of 21st-22nd April 1999, 30-40 unknown persons committed dacoity in the houses of the informant (PW9) and his uncle Madhukant Jha (deceased), during which Madhukant Jha was killed by a gunshot.
Case: Amar Nath Jha vs. Nand Kishore Singh & Etc.
Date: 3rd August 2018

---

Q: What was the allegation against the appellants in the case of B V Seshaiah vs. The State of Telangana & Anr.?
A: The allegation against the appellants was that they took money from Respondent No. 2 under the guise of making investments and made wrongful gains for their profits.
Case: B V Seshaiah vs. The State of Telangana & Anr.
Date: 1st February 2023

---

Q: What was Clause 8 of the Memorandum of Understanding in the case of B V Seshaiah vs. The State of Telangana & Anr.?
A: Clause 8 of the Memorandum of Understanding stated that any dispute should be resolved amicably, and if not, it should be referred to a sole arbitrator, whose decision would be final and binding.
Case: B V Seshaiah vs. The State of Telangana & Anr.
Date: 1st February 2023

---

Q: What were the charges against Leen Martin in the case of Union of India vs. Leen Martin & Anr.?
A: Leen Martin was charged under Sections 8(c), 20(b)(ii)(c), and 28 read with Section 23 of the NDPS Act for possession and attempted trafficking of narcotic substances.
Case: Union of India vs. Leen Martin & Anr.
Date: 1st February 2018

---

Q: What was the significance of the statements made by independent witnesses in the case of Union of India vs. Leen Martin & Anr.?
A: The statements made by independent witnesses (PW-8 and PW-9) were significant because they contradicted the prosecution's account of the seizure and inspection, leading to doubts about the credibility of the evidence.
Case: Union of India vs. Leen Martin & Anr.
Date: 1st February 2018

---

Q: What was the significance of the post-mortem report in the case of Aqeel Ahmad vs. State of Uttar Pradesh & Another?
A: The post-mortem report indicated that Khursheed Ahmad was severely assaulted, with fractured ribs and injuries sufficient to cause death, which highlighted the seriousness of the crime and the respondents' involvement.
Case: Aqeel Ahmad vs. State of Uttar Pradesh & Another
Date: 5th April 2024

Q: What was the prosecution's case in the incident leading to the death of Ladduram in Arvind Kumar @ Nemichand & Ors. vs. State of Rajasthan?

Date: 22nd November 2021

---

Q: What evidence was presented by the prosecution against Vikas Rathi in the case of Vikas Rathi vs. The State of U.P. & Anr.?
A: The prosecution presented statements from witnesses, including an eye witness, and a retracted statement recorded under Section 164 of the Cr.P.C., but these were found insufficient to establish Vikas Rathi's involvement in the crime.
Case: Vikas Rathi vs. The State of U.P. & Anr.
Date: 1st March 2023

---

Q: What evidence did the prosecution rely on in the case of Uppala Bixam @ Bixmaiah vs. The State of Andhra Pradesh?
A: The prosecution relied on the recovery of the deceased's body based on the appellant's alleged confessional statement and the sale of sheep by the appellant to Lingaiah, which were later found in possession of Shaik Naseem.
Case: Uppala Bixam @ Bixmaiah vs. The State of Andhra Pradesh
Date: 11th October 2018

---

Q: What was Vahitha's defense in the case of Vahitha vs. State of Tamil Nadu?
A: Vahitha's defense was that she was not present at the scene of the crime and was with her father in Kolakkudi at the time of the incident, as testified by her father, who supported her alibi.
Case: Vahitha vs. State of Tamil Nadu
Date: 22nd February 2023

---

Q: What discrepancies were pointed out by Vahitha's defense in the witness testimonies?
A: Vahitha's defense highlighted inconsistencies in the statements of key witnesses, particularly regarding the timing and sequence of events on the day of the crime, and discrepancies in the description of the saree used for strangulation.
Case: Vahitha vs. State of Tamil Nadu
Date: 22nd February 2023

---

Q: What did the Special Court, NIA, conclude regarding the appellants' cooperation with the investigation in the case of Kekhriesatuo Tep vs. National Investigation Agency?

Case: Kekhriesatuo Tep vs. National Investigation Agency

Q: What evidence did the complainant (PW-9) provide in the case of Sandeep Kumar vs. The State of Haryana & Anr.?

Q: What did the trial court conclude regarding the allegations of dowry harassment in the case of Smt. Chintambramma & Anr. vs. State of Karnataka?

Q: What was the content of Gaurav Wahi's suicide note in the case of V.P. Singh vs. The State of Punjab & Ors.?

Case: V.P. Singh vs. The State of Punjab & Ors.

---

Q: What were the key pieces of circumstantial evidence against Irappa Siddappa Murgannavar?
A: The key pieces of circumstantial evidence included: (i) the appellant taking the victim, R, from a neighbor's house; (ii) witnesses seeing the appellant carrying R and a gunny bag towards the Bennihalla stream; and (iii) the recovery of R's dead body in a gunny bag from Bennihalla based on the appellant's disclosure statement.
Case: Irappa Siddappa Murgannavar vs. State of Karnataka
Date: 8th November 2021

---

Q: What discrepancies were highlighted by the defense in the testimonies of key witnesses?
A: The defense highlighted discrepancies such as inconsistencies in the dates when witnesses claimed to have seen the appellant with the victim, and the initial police complaint not mentioning the appellant's involvement despite later claims by witnesses.
Case: Irappa Siddappa Murgannavar vs. State of Karnataka
Date: 8th November 2021

---

Q: What was Manoharan's defense during the trial?

Date: 1st August 2019

---

Q: What was the primary legal issue in the case of M/S. Jermyn Capital LLC Dubai vs. Central Bureau of Investigation & Ors.?

Case: M/S. Jermyn Capital LLC Dubai vs. Central Bureau of Investigation & Ors.

Q: Who represented the appellant in the case of M/S. Jermyn Capital LLC Dubai vs. Central Bureau of Investigation & Ors.?

Q: What evidence was presented against Sagar during the trial?
A: The complainant (PW1) and his father (PW2) mentioned Sagar's presence with Nitin before the incident, and the removal of the electric wire by Sagar was noted. However, the investigating officer did not find sufficient evidence to charge Sagar in the initial investigation.
Case: Sagar vs. State of U.P. & Another
Date: 10th March 2022

---

Q: What was the significance of the statements recorded under Section 50 of the PMLA in this case?
A: The statements recorded under Section 50 of the PMLA were significant as they provided admissible evidence of the involvement of Satyendar Kumar Jain and others in the money laundering activities.
Case: Satyendar Kumar Jain vs. Directorate of Enforcement
Date: 18th March 2024

---

Q: Who were the appellants in this case and what were their roles?
